1	# actor-x1
2	
3	An experiment in creating an actor model in rust
4	see [notes/design.md](notes/design.md) for details.
5	
6	This is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using
7	a bot to help in the development of a coding project. The goal
8	is that this main repo contains the "what", while the partner
9	bot repo contains "why" and "how". The key to the convention
10	is each change is cross-referenced to the other. Thus there
11	is a coherent story of the development of the project across time.
12	
13	The beginnings of that tool is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)
14	which currently does achieve this goal, but is being used as a
15	first test bed.
16	
17	## Cloning
18	
19	The Bot session repo is setup as a git submodule which
20	makes it easier to clone both repos. The easiest is to
21	clone both when doing the initial clone:
22	```
23	git clone --recurisve git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1
24	```
25	
26	If you forget to use --recurisze you need to do two additional
27	steps "init" and "update":
28	```
29	git clone git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1
30	git submodule init
31	git submodule update
32	```
33	
34	And these can be combined using 'update --init' if you like
35	```
36	git clone git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1
37	git submodule update --init
38	```
39	
40	## jj Tips for Git Users
41	
42	See [Steve Klabnik](https://github.com/steveklabnik)
43	[Jujutsu-tutorial](https://steveklabnik.github.io/jujutsu-tutorial)
44	and [jj docs](https://docs.jj-vcs.dev/latest/).
45	
46	### Initial Commit for a repo
47	
48	Create create directory add files.
49	
50	Minimal commands to push 
51	
52	```
53	jj git init .
54	jj describe
55	jj git remote add origin git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1
56	jj bookmark create main -r @
57	jj bookmark track main --remote=origin
58	jj git push
59	```
60	
61	### Push a change to main
62	
63	Assuming that this is to be push to main you
64	set the bookmark to the appropriate commit and
65	then just push:
66	
67	```
68	jj bookmark set main -r @
69	jj git push
70	```

100	### Example of modifying an existing commit and "force" push
101	
102	Tweak a commit and push it using `jj edit` then "force" push:
103	
104	Minimum steps changing xx but it could be any commit on main
105	or other bookmark/branch the last step repositions @ so @- is main:
106	
107	```
108	jj edit -r xxx --ignore-immutable
109	<Modify the commit such as, `jj describe or `vi README.md`>
110	jj git push --bookmark main
111	jj new main
112	```

195	
196	### Why `jj log` shows fewer commits than `gitk`
197	
198	If you're coming from git, jj's log output can be surprising compared to
199	tools like `gitk --all`.
200	
201	jj tracks *changes* (identified by change IDs), not individual git commits.
202	When you rewrite a change (`jj describe`, `jj rebase`, `jj squash`, etc.),
203	jj creates a new git commit and keeps the old one under `refs/jj/keep/*` as
204	undo history. `gitk --all` sees all of these obsolete commits; `jj log` only
205	shows the current version of each change.
206	
207	### Useful commands
208	
209	| Command | Description |
210	|---------|-------------|
211	| `jj log` | Show recent visible commits (default revset) |
212	| `jj log -r ::@` | Show **all** ancestors of the working copy |
213	| `jj log -r 'all()'` | Show all non-hidden commits (needed if you have multiple heads/branches) |
214	| `jj st | Show the status of the Working and Parent commits |
215	| `jj st -r <chid> | Status of the commit, <chid> such as `@`, `@-`, `xyz` |
216	| `jj show | Show the Working commit, -r @ |
217	| `jj show -r <chid> | Show the commit, <chid> such as `@`, `@-`, `xyz` |
218	| `jj evolog -r <chid>` | Show the evolution history of a single change |
219	| `jj op log` | Show operation history (each rewrite operation) |
220	
221	
222	In a single-branch workflow, `jj log -r ::@` and `jj log -r 'all()'` give
223	the same result. Use `all()` when you have multiple branches or heads.
224	
225	## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
226	
227	Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
228	to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo. The `ochid`
229	(Other Change ID) trailer contains a workspace-root-relative path
230	and jj changeID:
231	
232	```
233	ochid: /.claude/xvzvruqo   # points to a .claude repo change
234	ochid: /wtpmottv            # points to an app repo change
235	```
236	
237	Paths always start with `/` (the workspace root, i.e. vc-x1).
238	Each repo has a `.vc-config.toml` that identifies its location
239	within the workspace, so tools can resolve these paths locally.
240	
241	For full details see:
242	- [Git trailer convention](./notes/chores-01.md#git-trailer-convention)
243	  — [ochid (Other Change ID)](./notes/chores-01.md#ochid-other-change-id)
244	  — [ChangeID path syntax](./notes/chores-01.md#changeid-path-syntax)
245	  — [.vc-config.toml](./notes/chores-01.md#vc-configtoml)
246	
247	## Contributing
248	
249	Bot-following workflow, commit conventions, and code style are
250	canonical in [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md):
251	
252	- [Versioning during development](CLAUDE.md#versioning) — `-N`
253	  pre-release suffix convention (single-step vs multi-step).
254	- [Commit message style](CLAUDE.md#commit-message-style).
255	- [Commit-Push-Finalize Flow](CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) —
256	  two-checkpoint per-step discipline.
257	- [Code Conventions](CLAUDE.md#code-conventions) — doc comments on
258	  every file / fn / method, `// OK: …` on `unwrap*` calls,
259	  ask-on-ambiguity, stuck detection.
260	- [Pre-commit checklist](CLAUDE.md#pre-commit-checklist).
261	
262	Task tracking and release details live under [notes/](notes/):
263	near-term tasks in [notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md), per-release
264	details in `notes/chores-*.md`, and notes-specific formatting
265	rules in [notes/README.md](notes/README.md).

1	# Notes
2	
3	See [design.md](design.md)
4	
5	
6	## jj tips
7	
8	For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
9	
10	```
11	## Chores format
12	
13	Filename: "chores-XX.md"
14	example: chores-01.md
15	
16	Format of section labels: "## <short description> (X.Y.Z)"
17	example: "## Topic format description (0.1.0)"
18	
19	Example chore file:
20	```
21	# Chores-01.md
22	 
23	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
24	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
25	how and why of a particular chore.
26	
27	## Do something (1.3.1)
28	
29	Describe something
30	```
31	
32	## Versioning during development
33	
34	This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
35	git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
36	we go.
37	
38	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
39	
40	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
41	  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
42	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
43	  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
44	
45	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
46	
47	For multi-step:
48	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
49	2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
50	3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
51	
52	The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
53	prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
54	commits were exploratory vs final.
55	
56	## Todo format
57	
58	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
59	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
60	references.
61	
62	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
63	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
64	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
65	
66	Examples:
67	
68	# Todo
69	
70	- Add new feature X [details](chores-01.md#feature-x)
71	- Fix bug Y [1]
72	
73	# Done
74	
75	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
76	
77	[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
78	
```

1	# actor-x1
2	
3	## Overview
4	
5	Actor-x1 is a system that uses Communicating
6	Sequential Process (CSP) invented by Tony Hoare
7	In 1978 and is commonly termed the actor model.
8	
9	 - There will be a management system (MS) that:
10	
11	   - Provides the capability to interact with
12	     the runtime providing control and to capture
13	     logs and assists in developing the app
14	   - Creates an hello world scaffolding
15	   - provides discovery capabilities of other
16	     other actors.
17	
18	 - A runtime that drives the system and provides
19	   resources and services for the system
20	   - interfaces between the manager and the application
21	 
22	 - One or more actors running on one or more
23	   threads. Each actor adheres to these
24	   invariants:
25	   - Has on basic entry point; handler()
26	   - The handler() handles one message at a time and
27	     must run to completion as quickly as possible.
28	   - The handler() never ever blocks
29	   - If a handler() has tasks that take to much time
30	     a technique that can be used is to spawn
31	     threads to do work. That thread would then use
32	     messages to communicate with its parent and
33	     other actors as it does its work.
34	
35	## Future capabilities
36	   - I would like Actors to be implemented as an HSM.
37	     This potentially allows an Actor to implement
38	     sophisticated algorithms and prove correct operation.
39	
40	## Stage1 runtime
41	
42	Design and implement the simplest runtime possible
43	
44	### Message
45	
46	struct Message {}
47	
48	### Actor
49	
50	Actor implements an Actor trait
51	
52	trait Actor {
53	  // Handles a message 
54	  fn handle_message(msg: Message)
55	
56	  // other methods..
57	}
58	
59	### Goal1
60	
61	- Run two actors on one thread.
62	- A single message which is an empty struct
63	- Message ping pongs between the two actors
64	  for duration in seconds specified as a f64
65	  in seconds from the command line
66	
67	## Goal2
68	
69	- Same as Goal1 but each actor on a separate thread.
70	
71	## Stage2 runtime
72	
73	We will design and implement the slightly more capable runtime
74	
75	It supports 3 actors A, B and C
76	
77	### Message
78	
79	struct Message {
80	  src_id: u32,
81	  dst_id: u32,
82	  send_count: u64,
83	}
84	
85	### Actor
86	
87	Actor implements an Actor trait
88	
89	trait Actor {
90	  // Create a new actor
91	  fn new(id: u32, name: &str) -> Self;
92	
93	  // Return the actors name
94	  fn name(&Self) -> &str;
95	
96	  // Handle a message
97	  fn handle_message(msg: mut Message);
98	
99	  // other methods..
100	}
101	
102	### Goal1
103	
104	- Run 3 actors on one thread.
105	- Runtime "sends" 5 messages each to a random destination actor
106	- Each actor forwards the message they receive to a random
107	  dst_id that they select.
108	
109	### Goal2
110	- Same as Goal1 but each actor on a separate thread.
111	
```

> AGENT

Acquainted. Summary of what I found:

**Project**: `actor-x1` — a Rust experiment in the actor model (CSP, Hoare 1978). Currently design-only; no source code yet.

**Design stages** (notes/design.md):
- **Stage1**: minimal runtime — 2 actors ping-pong a unit `Message`; Goal1 single-thread, Goal2 per-thread.
- **Stage2**: 3 actors (A/B/C), `Message { src_id, dst_id, send_count }`, random forwarding.
- **Future**: actors as HSMs.

**Todo** (notes/todo.md): Initial design, PoC runtime, PoC manager — nothing in progress.

**Repo layout**: dual-repo — app repo at `.`, bot session repo at `.claude/` (git submodule + jj). Cross-linked via `ochid:` commit trailers.

**Conventions I'll follow** (CLAUDE.md):
- Conventional Commits with trailing `(X.Y.Z)`; every plan starts with a version bump (single-step or `X.Y.Z-0`/`-1`/… multi-step).
- Two-checkpoint Commit → Push+Finalize flow per step, with explicit approval at each checkpoint; hard stop after finalize.
- `//!` module doc + `///` on every fn/method; `// OK: …` justification on every non-test `.unwrap*`.
- Ask on ambiguity; don't use the memory dir (CLAUDE.md is canonical).
- "The bot thinks …" prefix for speculation in durable text.
- Last recent commit: `6029a15 docs: Update CLAUDE and README to latest version`.

Ready for direction.

> DEVELOPER

Propose an implementation for Stage 1

> AGENT

## Stage 1 implementation proposal

**Recommended approach**: multi-step, `0.1.0-0 → 0.1.0-1 → 0.1.0-2 → 0.1.0`. Stage 1 bundles three discrete pieces (bootstrap, Goal1, Goal2), each non-trivial. One-step would work but multi-step gives a clean per-goal checkpoint. Happy to flip to single-step per-goal (`0.1.0`, `0.2.0`) if you'd rather see each Goal as its own "done" marker.

**Scaffolding (0.1.0-0, chore marker, no behavior)**
- `cargo init --lib` at repo root → crate named `actor_x1`.
- `src/lib.rs` — re-exports `runtime` and `actor` modules.
- `src/actor.rs` — `Message`, `Actor` trait.
- `src/runtime.rs` — single-threaded and threaded runtimes.
- `src/bin/goal1.rs`, `src/bin/goal2.rs` — each takes one f64 positional arg (duration seconds). Start with hand-rolled `std::env::args` parsing; add `clap` only if we grow.
- `Cargo.toml` version = `0.1.0-0`.

**Goal1 — single thread, 2 actors (0.1.0-1)**
- `struct Message;` (unit per spec).
- `trait Actor { fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut Context, msg: Message); }` — **deviates slightly from the design sketch** (adds `&mut self` and a `Context`). See open question below.
- `Context::send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message)` pushes onto a runtime queue.
- `Runtime` owns `Vec<Box<dyn Actor>>` and a `VecDeque<(dst_id, Message)>`. Loop: pop → dispatch → repeat until `Instant::now() >= deadline`. Seed with one message from actor 0 → actor 1. Report total messages handled.

**Goal2 — one thread per actor (0.1.0-2)**
- Each actor owns a `mpsc::Receiver<Message>`; every actor holds a `Vec<Sender<Message>>` indexed by `dst_id` (or a `HashMap`).
- Spawn a thread per actor: `while let Ok(msg) = rx.recv() { actor.handle_message(msg); }`.
- Seed: main sends one `Message` into actor 0's channel, sleeps `duration`, then drops all senders. `recv()` errors → threads exit cleanly → `join`.
- No shared mutable state beyond the channels; each actor's state is thread-local.

**Final (0.1.0)** — drop the `-N` suffix, update `notes/todo.md` (move 'PoC runtime' to Done), add `notes/chores-01.md` section "Stage 1 runtime (0.1.0)".

**Open question I want to resolve before coding**

The design's trait signature `fn handle_message(msg: Message)` has no `&self` and no way to emit a reply. With an empty `Message {}`, ping-pong requires *some* way for a handler to trigger the next send. My proposal adds `&mut self` + a `Context` handle (option b). Alternatives:
- (a) `fn handle_message(&mut self, msg: Message) -> Option<(u32, Message)>` — handler returns the next send. Simpler; less extensible.
- (c) Keep the signature literal and drive ping-pong from the runtime loop itself (actors become no-ops) — matches spec but isn't really an actor.

**The bot thinks** (b) is the right call because Stage 2 will need a multi-target send anyway, so starting there avoids a churn rewrite. But this is a design decision — want to confirm (b), or pick (a) or (c)?

> DEVELOPER

One other thing, add a step in Goal1 to measure performance, using tprobe2 in ../iiac-perf/src for now copy the necessary code into actor-x1 and we'll make it a lib model in the future.

1	//! Scope-based measurement probe: a named, single-writer
2	//! histogram plus a record buffer, populated via `start` /
3	//! `end` rather than `record(ticks)`.
4	//!
5	//! `start(site_id)` reads the hardware tick counter and returns
6	//! an opaque [`TProbe2RecId`] carrying `(site_id, start_tsc)`;
7	//! `end(id)` reads the tick counter again and appends a complete
8	//! `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)` record to the probe's
9	//! internal buffer. No delta math, histogram ingestion, or
10	//! tick→ns conversion happens on the hot path — all of that is
11	//! deferred to [`TProbe2::report`], which drains pending records
12	//! into the histogram before rendering.
13	//!
14	//! This primitive preserves record-order information across
15	//! interleaved scopes and sites (non-stack nesting is supported
16	//! by construction; see ideas.md — Tprobe, Option B) and gives
17	//! future evolution space for per-site grouping, bounded buffers,
18	//! background drain threads, and long-term trace retention.
19	//!
20	//! The trade-off vs. [`crate::tprobe::TProbe`]: a growing
21	//! `Vec<Record>` in the hot path adds cache pressure and
22	//! reallocation cost in long, high-rate runs. For high-rate
23	//! single-histogram measurement prefer `TProbe`. Run
24	//! [`crate::benches::tp_pc`] and [`crate::benches::tp2_pc`]
25	//! back-to-back to see the hot-path cost of the scope API on a
26	//! matched workload.
27	
28	use hdrhistogram::Histogram;
29	
30	use crate::band_table;
31	use crate::ticks;
32	
33	/// Opaque handle returned by [`TProbe2::start`], consumed by
34	/// [`TProbe2::end`]. Carries the caller-supplied `site_id` and
35	/// the start-time tick reading; no probe-internal allocation
36	/// happens at `start` time.
37	///
38	/// `#[must_use]` — dropping the id without passing it to
39	/// [`TProbe2::end`] leaks the scope (no record is appended).
40	#[must_use]
41	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
42	pub struct TProbe2RecId {
43	    site_id: u64,
44	    start_tsc: u64,
45	}
46	
47	/// A complete scope record: `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)`.
48	/// Appended at [`TProbe2::end`] time; the record buffer only
49	/// ever holds complete records. Drained into the histogram at
50	/// [`TProbe2::report`] time.
51	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
52	struct Record {
53	    #[allow(dead_code)] // read once per-site grouping lands.
54	    site_id: u64,
55	    start_tsc: u64,
56	    end_tsc: u64,
57	}
58	
59	/// A named, single-writer histogram of hardware tick-counter
60	/// deltas plus a scope-record buffer. Not `Sync`; cross-thread
61	/// *sharing* is out of scope. `Send` so probes can be moved
62	/// between threads (e.g. returned via a `JoinHandle<TProbe2>`
63	/// on shutdown).
64	pub struct TProbe2 {
65	    name: String,
66	    hist: Histogram<u64>,
67	    records: Vec<Record>,
68	}
69	
70	impl TProbe2 {
71	    /// Create an empty probe. Histogram upper bound is 1e12
72	    /// ticks (~250 s at 4 GHz, ~100 s at 10 GHz), 3 significant
73	    /// figures.
74	    ///
75	    /// Exits the process (code 1) if the hardware tick counter
76	    /// isn't usable — see [`crate::ticks::require_ok`].
77	    pub fn new(name: &str) -> Self {
78	        ticks::require_ok();
79	        let _ = ticks::ticks_per_ns();
80	        Self {
81	            name: name.to_string(),
82	            hist: Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000_000, 3).unwrap(),
83	            records: Vec::new(),
84	        }
85	    }
86	
87	    /// Begin a scope. Reads the hardware tick counter and
88	    /// returns an opaque [`TProbe2RecId`] carrying `(site_id,
89	    /// start_tsc)`. The id must eventually be passed to
90	    /// [`TProbe2::end`]; a dropped id leaves no record.
91	    #[inline]
92	    pub fn start(&mut self, site_id: u64) -> TProbe2RecId {
93	        TProbe2RecId {
94	            site_id,
95	            start_tsc: ticks::read_ticks(),
96	        }
97	    }
98	
99	    /// End the scope started by [`TProbe2::start`]. Reads the
100	    /// hardware tick counter and appends a complete record
101	    /// `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)` to the probe's record
102	    /// buffer. Delta and histogram ingestion are deferred to
103	    /// [`TProbe2::report`].
104	    #[inline]
105	    pub fn end(&mut self, tpri: TProbe2RecId) {
106	        let end_tsc = ticks::read_ticks();
107	        self.records.push(Record {
108	            site_id: tpri.site_id,
109	            start_tsc: tpri.start_tsc,
110	            end_tsc,
111	        });
112	    }
113	
114	    /// Render a band-table report for this probe. `as_ticks`
115	    /// controls the display unit: `false` converts stored tick
116	    /// deltas to nanoseconds (default for the CLI); `true` shows
117	    /// raw ticks (`-t`/`--ticks`).
118	    ///
119	    /// Drains any pending `start`/`end` records into the histogram
120	    /// before rendering: `delta = end_tsc − start_tsc`, clamped to
121	    /// `1` since the histogram lower bound is 1.
122	    pub fn report(&mut self, as_ticks: bool) {
123	        for r in self.records.drain(..) {
124	            let delta = r.end_tsc.saturating_sub(r.start_tsc);
125	            self.hist.record(delta.max(1)).unwrap();
126	        }
127	        band_table::render("tprobe2", &self.name, &self.hist, as_ticks);
128	    }
129	}
130	
131	#[cfg(test)]
132	mod tests {
133	    use super::*;
134	
135	    #[test]
136	    fn start_end_appends_one_record() {
137	        let mut p = TProbe2::new("t");
138	        let id = p.start(42);
139	        p.end(id);
140	        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 1);
141	        let r = &p.records[0];
142	        assert_eq!(r.site_id, 42);
143	        assert!(r.end_tsc >= r.start_tsc);
144	    }
145	
146	    #[test]
147	    fn start_end_preserves_start_tsc() {
148	        let mut p = TProbe2::new("t");
149	        let id = p.start(7);
150	        let saved_start = id.start_tsc;
151	        p.end(id);
152	        let r = &p.records[0];
153	        assert_eq!(r.site_id, 7);
154	        assert_eq!(r.start_tsc, saved_start);
155	    }
156	
157	    #[test]
158	    fn start_end_interleaved_non_stack() {
159	        let mut p = TProbe2::new("t");
160	        let a = p.start(1);
161	        let b = p.start(2);
162	        p.end(a);
163	        p.end(b);
164	        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 2);
165	        assert_eq!(p.records[0].site_id, 1);
166	        assert_eq!(p.records[1].site_id, 2);
167	    }
168	
169	    #[test]
170	    fn report_drains_records_into_histogram() {
171	        let mut p = TProbe2::new("t");
172	        let id1 = p.start(1);
173	        p.end(id1);
174	        let id2 = p.start(2);
175	        p.end(id2);
176	        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);
177	        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 2);
178	
179	        p.report(false);
180	        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 0);
181	        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);
182	
183	        // Idempotent: a second report drains nothing, hist unchanged.
184	        p.report(false);
185	        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);
186	    }
187	}
188	
```

1	//! Hardware tick counter abstraction: thin wrapper over the
2	//! target architecture's fixed-rate monotonic counter.
3	//!
4	//! Probes call three functions; the per-arch impl lives in a
5	//! child module gated by `#[cfg(target_arch = ...)]`:
6	//!
7	//! - [`read_ticks`] — current counter value.
8	//! - [`ticks_per_ns`] — calibrated conversion ratio.
9	//! - [`require_ok`] — exit the process if the counter isn't
10	//!   usable for probe measurements.
11	//!
12	//! Only `x86_64` is implemented today. AArch64 (`CNTVCT_EL0`)
13	//! and RISC-V (`time` CSR) both have architecturally invariant
14	//! counters by ISA spec, so their `require_ok` will be a no-op
15	//! once those impls land.
16	
17	#[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
18	mod x86_64;
19	
20	#[cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")]
21	use x86_64 as imp;
22	
23	#[cfg(not(target_arch = "x86_64"))]
24	compile_error!(
25	    "iiac-perf probes currently only support target_arch = \"x86_64\". \
26	     Add a per-arch impl module (AArch64: CNTVCT_EL0; RISC-V: time CSR) \
27	     and wire it into src/ticks.rs."
28	);
29	
30	/// Read the current tick counter. Monotonic and fixed-rate.
31	#[inline(always)]
32	pub fn read_ticks() -> u64 {
33	    imp::read_ticks()
34	}
35	
36	/// Calibrated conversion ratio: counter ticks per nanosecond.
37	/// Cached — the first call does the work.
38	pub fn ticks_per_ns() -> f64 {
39	    imp::ticks_per_ns()
40	}
41	
42	/// Verify the tick counter is usable for probe measurements;
43	/// exit the process (code 1) with a diagnostic if not. The
44	/// checks performed depend on the target architecture.
45	pub fn require_ok() {
46	    imp::require_ok();
47	}
48	
```

```
1	//! Shared band-table renderer for tick-valued histograms.
2	//!
3	//! Both `TProbe` (fast path, direct-histogram) and `TProbe2`
4	//! (scope API, records → drain) store hardware tick deltas and
5	//! want the same band-table output shape — min/p1/…/p99/max
6	//! rows with first/last/range/count/mean columns, plus summary
7	//! lines for mean, stdev, mean min-p99, stdev min-p99. This
8	//! module provides a single implementation both can call into.
9	//!
10	//! Display unit is chosen by `as_ticks`: `false` converts stored
11	//! tick values to nanoseconds via [`crate::ticks::ticks_per_ns`];
12	//! `true` shows raw ticks.
13	
14	use hdrhistogram::Histogram;
15	
16	use crate::harness::{fmt_commas, fmt_commas_f64};
17	use crate::ticks;
18	
19	const BOUNDARY_PCTS: &[f64] = &[
20	    0.0, 0.01, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.99, 1.0,
21	];
22	const BOUNDARY_NAMES: &[&str] = &[
23	    "min", "p1", "p10", "p20", "p30", "p40", "p50", "p60", "p70", "p80", "p90", "p99", "max",
24	];
25	
26	/// Render a band-table report for `hist`, interpreting stored
27	/// values as hardware ticks. `kind` is the header label
28	/// (`"tprobe"`, `"tprobe2"`, …) and `name` is the probe's name.
29	/// `as_ticks=false` converts to ns; `true` keeps raw ticks.
30	pub(crate) fn render(kind: &str, name: &str, hist: &Histogram<u64>, as_ticks: bool) {
31	    let sample_count = hist.len();
32	    println!("  {kind}: {name} [count={}]", fmt_commas(sample_count));
33	    if sample_count == 0 {
34	        println!();
35	        return;
36	    }
37	
38	    let unit = if as_ticks { "tk" } else { "ns" };
39	    let tpn = ticks::ticks_per_ns();
40	    let conv = |v: u64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v as f64 } else { v as f64 / tpn } };
41	    let conv_f = |v: f64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v } else { v / tpn } };
42	
43	    let n_bands = BOUNDARY_PCTS.len() - 1;
44	    let mut band_first = vec![u64::MAX; n_bands];
45	    let mut band_last = vec![0u64; n_bands];
46	    let mut band_count = vec![0u64; n_bands];
47	    let mut band_sum = vec![0u128; n_bands];
48	
49	    let mut cumulative = 0u64;
50	    for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {
51	        let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
52	        let count = iv.count_at_value();
53	        let mid_rank = (cumulative as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
54	        let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]
55	            .iter()
56	            .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
57	            .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
58	        band_first[idx] = band_first[idx].min(value);
59	        band_last[idx] = band_last[idx].max(value);
60	        band_count[idx] += count;
61	        band_sum[idx] += value as u128 * count as u128;
62	        cumulative += count;
63	    }
64	
65	    struct BandRow {
66	        label: String,
67	        first: String,
68	        last: String,
69	        range: String,
70	        count: String,
71	        mean: String,
72	    }
73	
74	    let mut rows: Vec<BandRow> = Vec::new();
75	    for i in 0..n_bands {
76	        if band_count[i] == 0 {
77	            continue;
78	        }
79	        let mean_val = band_sum[i] as f64 / band_count[i] as f64;
80	        let range_raw = band_last[i] - band_first[i] + 1;
81	        rows.push(BandRow {
82	            label: format!("{}-{}", BOUNDARY_NAMES[i], BOUNDARY_NAMES[i + 1]),
83	            first: fmt_commas_f64(conv(band_first[i]), 0),
84	            last: fmt_commas_f64(conv(band_last[i]), 0),
85	            range: fmt_commas_f64(conv(range_raw), 0),
86	            count: fmt_commas(band_count[i]),
87	            mean: fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(mean_val), 0),
88	        });
89	    }
90	
91	    let label_w = rows
92	        .iter()
93	        .map(|r| r.label.len())
94	        .max()
95	        .unwrap_or(0)
96	        .max("stdev min-p99".len());
97	    let first_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.first.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
98	    let last_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.last.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
99	    let range_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.range.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
100	    let count_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.count.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
101	    let mean_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0);
102	
103	    const INDENT: &str = "    ";
104	    const GAP: &str = "    ";
105	
106	    let first_col = INDENT.len() + label_w + 1 + first_w;
107	    let unit_len = 1 + unit.len();
108	    let last_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + last_w;
109	    let range_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + range_w;
110	    let count_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + count_w;
111	    let mean_gap = GAP.len() + mean_w;
112	    println!(
113	        "{:>first_col$}{:>last_gap$}{:>range_gap$}{:>count_gap$}{:>mean_gap$}",
114	        "first", "last", "range", "count", "mean",
115	    );
116	
117	    for r in &rows {
118	        println!(
119	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>first_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>last_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>range_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>count_w$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
120	            r.label, r.first, r.last, r.range, r.count, r.mean,
121	        );
122	    }
123	
124	    let hist_mean = hist.mean();
125	    let skip = first_w
126	        + unit_len
127	        + GAP.len()
128	        + last_w
129	        + unit_len
130	        + GAP.len()
131	        + range_w
132	        + unit_len
133	        + GAP.len()
134	        + count_w;
135	    println!(
136	        "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
137	        "mean",
138	        "",
139	        fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(hist_mean), 0),
140	    );
141	    println!(
142	        "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
143	        "stdev",
144	        "",
145	        fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(hist.stdev()), 0),
146	    );
147	
148	    let trim_count: u64 = band_count[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();
149	    if trim_count > 0 {
150	        let trim_sum: u128 = band_sum[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();
151	        let trim_mean = trim_sum as f64 / trim_count as f64;
152	
153	        let mut trim_var_sum = 0.0f64;
154	        let mut trim_var_count = 0u64;
155	        let mut cum = 0u64;
156	        for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {
157	            let value = iv.value_iterated_to();
158	            let count = iv.count_at_value();
159	            let mid_rank = (cum as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;
160	            let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]
161	                .iter()
162	                .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)
163	                .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1);
164	            if idx < n_bands - 1 {
165	                let diff = value as f64 - trim_mean;
166	                trim_var_sum += diff * diff * count as f64;
167	                trim_var_count += count;
168	            }
169	            cum += count;
170	        }
171	        let trim_stdev = if trim_var_count > 1 {
172	            (trim_var_sum / trim_var_count as f64).sqrt()
173	        } else {
174	            0.0
175	        };
176	
177	        println!(
178	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
179	            "mean min-p99",
180	            "",
181	            fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(trim_mean), 0),
182	        );
183	        println!(
184	            "{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}",
185	            "stdev min-p99",
186	            "",
187	            fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(trim_stdev), 0),
188	        );
189	    }
190	    println!();
191	}
192

1	//! x86_64 impl of the tick-counter abstraction: `rdtsc` for
2	//! reads, CPUID-based invariant-TSC detection, and a 10 ms
3	//! spin-loop calibration for ticks-per-nanosecond.
4	
5	use std::sync::OnceLock;
6	use std::time::Duration;
7	
8	#[inline(always)]
9	pub fn read_ticks() -> u64 {
10	    // Safe on any x86_64 CPU: TSC has been present since the
11	    // original Pentium.
12	    unsafe { core::arch::x86_64::_rdtsc() }
13	}
14	
15	static TICKS_PER_NS: OnceLock<f64> = OnceLock::new();
16	
17	pub fn ticks_per_ns() -> f64 {
18	    *TICKS_PER_NS.get_or_init(calibrate)
19	}
20	
21	/// Spin for ~10 ms while reading `minstant::Instant` elapsed ns
22	/// and raw `rdtsc` ticks at each end. `minstant` computes its
23	/// own nanos-per-cycle internally but doesn't expose it, so we
24	/// rederive the ratio from the two independent measurements.
25	fn calibrate() -> f64 {
26	    let start_instant = minstant::Instant::now();
27	    let start_tsc = read_ticks();
28	    let target = Duration::from_millis(10);
29	    loop {
30	        let elapsed = start_instant.elapsed();
31	        if elapsed >= target {
32	            let end_tsc = read_ticks();
33	            let dtk = end_tsc.wrapping_sub(start_tsc) as f64;
34	            let dns = elapsed.as_nanos() as f64;
35	            return dtk / dns;
36	        }
37	        core::hint::spin_loop();
38	    }
39	}
40	
41	pub fn require_ok() {
42	    if !has_invariant_tsc() {
43	        eprintln!(
44	            "error: invariant TSC not supported by this CPU \
45	             (CPUID.80000007h:EDX[bit 8] = 0). iiac-perf probes \
46	             require a fixed-rate, non-stopping TSC; refusing to run."
47	        );
48	        std::process::exit(1);
49	    }
50	    if !minstant::is_tsc_available() {
51	        eprintln!(
52	            "error: TSC not selected as a kernel clocksource. \
53	             The CPU advertises invariant TSC, but the kernel has \
54	             rejected it — likely a sync or drift issue. iiac-perf \
55	             won't use a clock source the kernel considers unreliable; \
56	             refusing to run."
57	        );
58	        std::process::exit(1);
59	    }
60	}
61	
62	/// `CPUID.80000007h:EDX[bit 8]` — invariant TSC. Set iff the
63	/// TSC runs at a constant rate regardless of P-state changes
64	/// and keeps ticking in deep C-states. Both Intel and AMD
65	/// expose the feature at this bit.
66	fn has_invariant_tsc() -> bool {
67	    use core::arch::x86_64::__cpuid;
68	    let max_ext = __cpuid(0x8000_0000).eax;
69	    if max_ext < 0x8000_0007 {
70	        return false;
71	    }
72	    let leaf = __cpuid(0x8000_0007);
73	    (leaf.edx >> 8) & 1 == 1
74	}
75	
```

155	
156	fn round_elapsed(elapsed_ns: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {
157	    (elapsed_ns + inner / 2) / inner
158	}
159	
160	/// Format an integer with thousands separators, e.g.
161	/// `12345` → `"12,345"`.
162	pub fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {
163	    let s = n.to_string();
164	    let mut result = String::new();
165	    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {
166	        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {
167	            result.push(',');
168	        }
169	        result.push(c);
170	    }
171	    result.chars().rev().collect()
172	}
173	
174	/// Format a float with `decimals` fractional digits and thousands
175	/// separators on the integer part.
176	pub fn fmt_commas_f64(n: f64, decimals: usize) -> String {
177	    let s = format!("{n:.decimals$}");
178	    let (sign, body) = match s.strip_prefix('-') {
179	        Some(rest) => ("-", rest),
180	        None => ("", s.as_str()),
181	    };
182	    let (int_part, frac_part) = match body.find('.') {
183	        Some(i) => (&body[..i], &body[i..]),
184	        None => (body, ""),
185	    };
186	    let int_num: u64 = int_part.parse().unwrap_or(0);
187	    format!("{sign}{}{frac_part}", fmt_commas(int_num))
188	}

## Addendum — perf measurement via `tprobe2`

**What `tprobe2` needs** (minimum viable copy from `../iiac-perf/src`):

| iiac-perf file | actor-x1 destination | notes |
|---|---|---|
| `tprobe2.rs` | `src/tprobe2.rs` | unchanged |
| `band_table.rs` | `src/band_table.rs` | rewrite one `use` to point at our fmt module |
| `ticks.rs` | `src/ticks.rs` | unchanged |
| `ticks/x86_64.rs` | `src/ticks/x86_64.rs` | unchanged |
| `fmt_commas` + `fmt_commas_f64` (from `harness.rs` lines 162–188) | `src/fmt.rs` *(new, ~30 lines)* | extract just those two fns; skips harness's 393-line benchmark apparatus we don't need |

**Crate dependencies added to `Cargo.toml`**: `hdrhistogram = "7"`, `minstant = "0.1"`. Nothing else from iiac-perf's chain is required.

**Arch caveat**: tprobe2's tick counter is x86_64-only today — `ticks.rs` has a `compile_error!` on other arches. **The bot thinks** this is fine for us (your dev box is x86_64) but worth naming so it isn't a later surprise.

**Where the probe sites go in Goal1**
- `TProbe2` lives on the single-threaded runtime alongside the message queue.
- Wrap the dispatch call: `let id = probe.start(actor_id as u64); actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, msg); probe.end(id);` — `site_id = actor_id` so per-actor latency shows up separately if we add per-site grouping later.
- After the `Instant::now() >= deadline` loop exits, call `probe.report(false)` — prints the band table in ns alongside the existing message-count summary.
- Add `--ticks` flag (or `-t`) to flip to raw ticks, matching the iiac-perf CLI convention.

**Revised version ladder**
- `0.1.0-0` — bootstrap Cargo + plan marker
- `0.1.0-1` — Goal1 base (ping-pong + duration + count)
- `0.1.0-2` — copy `tprobe2` + wire probe sites into Goal1 (this is the added step)
- `0.1.0-3` — Goal2
- `0.1.0` — final: todo/chores update, drop `-N` suffix

**Notes/chores entry for the copy**: a `## Vendored perf probe (0.1.0-2)` section in `chores-01.md` recording the source path (`../iiac-perf/src`), files copied, and the "promote to shared lib later" intent.

**Things to confirm before I start**
1. Scope granularity: **per-dispatch** (proposed above — one `start`/`end` per `handle_message` call) or **per-round-trip** (probe wraps one ping-pong, actor-pair latency)? Per-dispatch matches tprobe2's design more naturally; round-trip tells you end-to-end messaging cost.
2. Apply probe to **Goal2 too**? `TProbe2` is `Send` but not `Sync`, so each threaded actor would own its own probe and report on shutdown. Straightforward but adds surface area.
3. Module naming: `src/tprobe2.rs` at top level (flat, matches source) vs `src/perf/{tprobe2,band_table,ticks,fmt}.rs` (grouped, easier to extract to a lib later). I lean `src/perf/` for the grouping; happy either way.

  "new_string": "## Stage 1 runtime — plan marker (0.1.0-0)\n\nBegins the Stage 1 implementation described in [design.md](design.md).\nMulti-step ladder (see [Versioning during development](../CLAUDE.md#versioning)):\n\n- `0.1.0-0` — bootstrap Cargo crate + this plan marker (no behavior).\n- `0.1.0-1` — Goal1: two actors, single thread, ping-pong an empty\n  `Message` for a caller-supplied duration in seconds. Reports\n  total messages handled.\n- `0.1.0-2` — vendor `tprobe2` (and its transitive deps: `band_table`,\n  `ticks`, `fmt_commas*`) from `../iiac-perf/src` into `src/perf/`.\n  Wrap per-dispatch `handle_message` calls with `probe.start`/`probe.end`\n  using `site_id = actor_id`; print a band-table report at shutdown\n  (ns by default, `-t/--ticks` for raw ticks). The bot thinks promoting\n  this to a shared crate is worthwhile once Stage 2 is underway, but\n  copying in now avoids a cross-repo dependency for the PoC.\n- `0.1.0-3` — Goal2: same workload as Goal1 but each actor on its own\n  thread, communicating via `std::sync::mpsc` channels. Shutdown by\n  dropping senders. Probe instrumented the same way (per-thread\n  `TProbe2`, report on join).\n- `0.1.0` — final: drop the `-N` suffix, update `notes/todo.md` and\n  `notes/README.md`.\n\n### Design decisions recorded here\n\n- **Actor trait signature** deviates from the design sketch by adding\n  `&mut self` and a `&mut dyn Context` parameter, where `Context::send(dst_id, msg)`\n  is the only way for a handler to emit messages. Required because\n  the empty `Message {}` carries no reply-to information and the\n  sketched signature `fn handle_message(msg: Message)` has nowhere\n  to thread outbound sends through.\n- **Two `Context` implementations**: the single-threaded runtime's\n  context writes to a `VecDeque`; the multi-threaded runtime's\n  context holds `Sender<Message>` per destination. Actors see\n  only `&mut dyn Context` and are agnostic to which runtime is\n  driving them.\n- **x86_64-only for now**: `tprobe2`'s tick counter uses `rdtsc`\n  and `iiac-perf/src/ticks.rs` emits a `compile_error!` on other\n  arches. Development target is x86_64 so this is acceptable; the\n  restriction is documented at the vendored module."
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/lib.rs",
  "content": "//! actor-x1 — an experiment in the actor model in Rust.\n//!\n//! See `notes/design.md` for the staged design. Stage 1 provides\n//! a minimal runtime with two actors that ping-pong an empty\n//! [`Message`] for a caller-supplied duration; Goal1 runs both\n//! actors on one thread, Goal2 runs each actor on its own thread.\n//!\n//! Public surface today:\n//! - [`Message`]: the empty unit message Stage 1 actors exchange.\n//! - [`Actor`]: the single-method actor trait.\n//! - [`Context`]: the send-side handle passed to each dispatch.\n//! - [`runtime`]: concrete runtimes (single-thread for Goal1; the\n//!   multi-thread runtime will land in 0.1.0-3).\n\npub mod runtime;\n\n/// Empty unit-type message exchanged between Stage 1 actors.\n/// Carries no payload by design — Stage 1's contract is only\n/// \"dispatch happens\"; Stage 2 replaces this with a richer type.\npub struct Message;\n\n/// Actor behavior: handle one [`Message`] at a time, never blocking.\n///\n/// The only method is [`Actor::handle_message`]; all outbound\n/// communication goes through the [`Context`] the runtime supplies.\npub trait Actor {\n    /// Handle a single incoming message. Use `ctx` to emit any\n    /// resulting sends. Must not block.\n    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, msg: Message);\n}\n\n/// Runtime-supplied send handle passed to each\n/// [`Actor::handle_message`] call. Implementations differ between\n/// runtimes: the single-thread runtime writes to a shared queue;\n/// the (future) multi-thread runtime writes to per-actor channels.\npub trait Context {\n    /// Enqueue `msg` for delivery to the actor identified by\n    /// `dst_id`. Returns immediately; delivery is the runtime's job.\n    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message);\n}\n"
}

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/runtime.rs",
  "content": "//! Runtimes that drive [`Actor`]s. The single-thread runtime lives\n//! here today; the multi-thread runtime arrives in 0.1.0-3.\n\nuse std::collections::VecDeque;\nuse std::time::{Duration, Instant};\n\nuse crate::{Actor, Context, Message};\n\n/// Single-threaded runtime: all actors run on the calling thread,\n/// sharing one FIFO message queue. Dispatch loops until either the\n/// caller-supplied deadline expires or the queue drains.\npub struct SingleThreadRuntime {\n    actors: Vec<Box<dyn Actor>>,\n    queue: VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,\n}\n\nimpl SingleThreadRuntime {\n    /// Create an empty runtime with no actors or pending messages.\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            actors: Vec::new(),\n            queue: VecDeque::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Register `actor`; the returned `u32` is its stable id for\n    /// the lifetime of the runtime and is usable as `dst_id` in\n    /// [`Context::send`]. Ids are allocated sequentially from 0.\n    pub fn add_actor(&mut self, actor: Box<dyn Actor>) -> u32 {\n        let id = self.actors.len() as u32;\n        self.actors.push(actor);\n        id\n    }\n\n    /// Enqueue an initial message for `dst_id`. Used to prime the\n    /// dispatch loop before [`Self::run_for`].\n    pub fn seed(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {\n        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));\n    }\n\n    /// Dispatch messages until `duration` elapses or the queue\n    /// empties. Returns the total number of messages handled.\n    pub fn run_for(&mut self, duration: Duration) -> u64 {\n        let deadline = Instant::now() + duration;\n        let mut count = 0u64;\n        while Instant::now() < deadline {\n            let Some((dst, msg)) = self.queue.pop_front() else {\n                break;\n            };\n            let Self { actors, queue } = self;\n            let actor = &mut actors[dst as usize];\n            let mut ctx = SingleCtx { queue };\n            actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, msg);\n            count += 1;\n        }\n        count\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Default for SingleThreadRuntime {\n    /// Same as [`SingleThreadRuntime::new`].\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self::new()\n    }\n}\n\n/// [`Context`] impl used by [`SingleThreadRuntime`]: pushes onto\n/// the runtime's shared queue.\nstruct SingleCtx<'a> {\n    queue: &'a mut VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,\n}\n\nimpl Context for SingleCtx<'_> {\n    /// Push `(dst_id, msg)` onto the shared dispatch queue.\n    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {\n        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// Minimal actor that drops every message without replying.\n    struct Sink;\n\n    impl Actor for Sink {\n        fn handle_message(&mut self, _ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {}\n    }\n\n    /// Bounded ping-pong actor: replies to its peer up to `max`\n    /// times, then goes silent. Used to produce a deterministic\n    /// message count in unit tests without wall-clock dependence.\n    struct Bouncer {\n        peer: u32,\n        sent: u32,\n        max: u32,\n    }\n\n    impl Actor for Bouncer {\n        fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {\n            if self.sent < self.max {\n                ctx.send(self.peer, Message);\n                self.sent += 1;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn seed_is_handled_and_queue_drains() {\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new();\n        let id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink));\n        rt.seed(id, Message);\n        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_millis(1));\n        assert_eq!(n, 1);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn bounded_ping_pong_count() {\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new();\n        let a = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 1,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 5,\n        }));\n        let _b = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 0,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 5,\n        }));\n        rt.seed(a, Message);\n        // seed (1) + 5 from a + 5 from b = 11\n        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs(1));\n        assert_eq!(n, 11);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn add_actor_assigns_sequential_ids() {\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new();\n        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 0);\n        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 1);\n        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 2);\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs",
  "content": "//! Goal1 binary: two actors on a single thread ping-pong an empty\n//! [`Message`] for a caller-supplied duration in seconds (f64).\n//!\n//! Usage: `goal1 <duration_secs>`\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse actor_x1::runtime::SingleThreadRuntime;\nuse actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};\n\n/// Actor that, on every message received, sends exactly one message\n/// back to its configured peer.\nstruct PingPongActor {\n    peer_id: u32,\n}\n\nimpl Actor for PingPongActor {\n    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`. Never blocks.\n    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {\n        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);\n    }\n}\n\n/// Parse the duration from argv, run two ping-pong actors for that\n/// long on one thread, and print messages handled plus throughput.\nfn main() {\n    let mut args = std::env::args().skip(1);\n    let duration_s: f64 = args\n        .next()\n        .expect(\"usage: goal1 <duration_secs>\")\n        .parse()\n        .expect(\"duration must be a non-negative number of seconds\");\n\n    let duration = Duration::from_secs_f64(duration_s);\n\n    let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new();\n    let a_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 }));\n    let b_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 }));\n    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));\n    rt.seed(a_id, Message);\n\n    let count = rt.run_for(duration);\n    let mmps = count as f64 / duration_s / 1e6;\n    println!(\"goal1: {count} messages in {duration_s:.3}s ({mmps:.3} M msg/s)\");\n}\n"
}

Development target is x86_64 so this is acceptable; the\n  restriction is documented at the vendored module.\n\n## Goal1: single-thread ping-pong runtime (0.1.0-1)\n\nImplements Goal1 of Stage 1: two actors, one thread, ping-pong an\nempty [`Message`] for a caller-supplied duration in seconds, then\nreport the message count and throughput.\n\n- `src/lib.rs`: `Message` unit struct, `Actor` trait\n  (`handle_message(&mut self, &mut dyn Context, Message)`),\n  `Context` trait (`send(dst_id: u32, msg: Message)`), and\n  `pub mod runtime`.\n- `src/runtime.rs`: `SingleThreadRuntime` owns a\n  `Vec<Box<dyn Actor>>` and a `VecDeque<(u32, Message)>`; `run_for`\n  pops a message, field-split-borrows `actors` and `queue`,\n  constructs a private `SingleCtx` wrapping the queue, dispatches,\n  counts. Step 0.1.0-1 complete. Summary:

- **`Cargo.toml`** — version bumped `0.1.0-0` → `0.1.0-1`; `Cargo.lock` follows.
- **`src/lib.rs`** — defines `Message` (unit struct), `Actor` trait (`handle_message(&mut self, &mut dyn Context, Message)`), `Context` trait (`send(dst_id, msg)`), and `pub mod runtime`.
- **`src/runtime.rs`** (new) — `SingleThreadRuntime`: `add_actor`, `seed`, `run_for(duration)`; internal `SingleCtx` wraps a `VecDeque<(u32, Message)>`. Field-split borrow (`let Self { actors, queue } = self;`) lets us index an actor and build a `Context` over the queue without fighting the borrow checker. Unit tests: seed drain, bounded ping-pong count = 11 (= seed + 5·2), sequential id assignment.
- **`src/bin/goal1.rs`** (new) — CLI `goal1 <duration_secs>`; two `PingPongActor`s each forward once per received message; prints `goal1: N messages in S.SSSs (M.MMM M msg/s)`.
- **`notes/chores-01.md`** — new `## Goal1: single-thread ping-pong runtime (0.1.0-1)` section plus a deviation note (`Message {}` → unit struct).

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/fmt.rs",
  "content": "//! Number formatting helpers: thousands separators for integer and\n//! float display. Extracted from `iiac-perf/src/harness.rs` so the\n//! band-table renderer has its string-formatting dependencies\n//! without vendoring the whole benchmark harness.\n\n/// Format an integer with thousands separators, e.g.\n/// `12345` → `\"12,345\"`.\npub fn fmt_commas(n: u64) -> String {\n    let s = n.to_string();\n    let mut result = String::new();\n    for (i, c) in s.chars().rev().enumerate() {\n        if i > 0 && i % 3 == 0 {\n            result.push(',');\n        }\n        result.push(c);\n    }\n    result.chars().rev().collect()\n}\n\n/// Format a float with `decimals` fractional digits and thousands\n/// separators on the integer part.\npub fn fmt_commas_f64(n: f64, decimals: usize) -> String {\n    let s = format!(\"{n:.decimals$}\");\n    let (sign, body) = match s.strip_prefix('-') {\n        Some(rest) => (\"-\", rest),\n        None => (\"\", s.as_str()),\n    };\n    let (int_part, frac_part) = match body.find('.') {\n        Some(i) => (&body[..i], &body[i..]),\n        None => (body, \"\"),\n    };\n    let int_num: u64 = int_part.parse().unwrap_or(0); // OK: f64 Display always produces a non-empty integer part; 0 is an inert fallback\n    format!(\"{sign}{}{frac_part}\", fmt_commas(int_num))\n}\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/ticks.rs",
  "content": "//! Hardware tick counter abstraction: thin wrapper over the\n//! target architecture's fixed-rate monotonic counter.\n//!\n//! Probes call three functions; the per-arch impl lives in a\n//! child module gated by `#[cfg(target_arch = ...)]`:\n//!\n//! - [`read_ticks`] — current counter value.\n//! - [`ticks_per_ns`] — calibrated conversion ratio.\n//! - [`require_ok`] — exit the process if the counter isn't\n//!   usable for probe measurements.\n//!\n//! Only `x86_64` is implemented today. AArch64 (`CNTVCT_EL0`)\n//! and RISC-V (`time` CSR) both have architecturally invariant\n//! counters by ISA spec, so their `require_ok` will be a no-op\n//! once those impls land.\n\n#[cfg(target_arch = \"x86_64\")]\nmod x86_64;\n\n#[cfg(target_arch = \"x86_64\")]\nuse x86_64 as imp;\n\n#[cfg(not(target_arch = \"x86_64\"))]\ncompile_error!(\n    \"actor-x1 probes currently only support target_arch = \\\"x86_64\\\". \\\n     Add a per-arch impl module (AArch64: CNTVCT_EL0; RISC-V: time CSR) \\\n     and wire it into src/perf/ticks.rs.\"\n);\n\n/// Read the current tick counter. Monotonic and fixed-rate.\n#[inline(always)]\npub fn read_ticks() -> u64 {\n    imp::read_ticks()\n}\n\n/// Calibrated conversion ratio: counter ticks per nanosecond.\n/// Cached — the first call does the work.\npub fn ticks_per_ns() -> f64 {\n    imp::ticks_per_ns()\n}\n\n/// Verify the tick counter is usable for probe measurements;\n/// exit the process (code 1) with a diagnostic if not. The\n/// checks performed depend on the target architecture.\npub fn require_ok() {\n    imp::require_ok();\n}\n"
}

id: toolu_0132Df94DtV1D3rhGQzgUcSz
```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/ticks/x86_64.rs",
  "content": "//! x86_64 impl of the tick-counter abstraction: `rdtsc` for\n//! reads, CPUID-based invariant-TSC detection, and a 10 ms\n//! spin-loop calibration for ticks-per-nanosecond.\n\nuse std::sync::OnceLock;\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\n/// Read the raw time-stamp counter via the `rdtsc` instruction.\n#[inline(always)]\npub fn read_ticks() -> u64 {\n    // Safe on any x86_64 CPU: TSC has been present since the\n    // original Pentium.\n    unsafe { core::arch::x86_64::_rdtsc() }\n}\n\nstatic TICKS_PER_NS: OnceLock<f64> = OnceLock::new();\n\n/// Cached ratio of TSC ticks per nanosecond (first call calibrates).\npub fn ticks_per_ns() -> f64 {\n    *TICKS_PER_NS.get_or_init(calibrate)\n}\n\n/// Spin for ~10 ms while reading `minstant::Instant` elapsed ns\n/// and raw `rdtsc` ticks at each end. `minstant` computes its\n/// own nanos-per-cycle internally but doesn't expose it, so we\n/// rederive the ratio from the two independent measurements.\nfn calibrate() -> f64 {\n    let start_instant = minstant::Instant::now();\n    let start_tsc = read_ticks();\n    let target = Duration::from_millis(10);\n    loop {\n        let elapsed = start_instant.elapsed();\n        if elapsed >= target {\n            let end_tsc = read_ticks();\n            let dtk = end_tsc.wrapping_sub(start_tsc) as f64;\n            let dns = elapsed.as_nanos() as f64;\n            return dtk / dns;\n        }\n        core::hint::spin_loop();\n    }\n}\n\n/// Bail out unless the CPU has invariant TSC and the kernel\n/// selected TSC as its clocksource.\npub fn require_ok() {\n    if !has_invariant_tsc() {\n        eprintln!(\n            \"error: invariant TSC not supported by this CPU \\\n             (CPUID.80000007h:EDX[bit 8] = 0). actor-x1 probes \\\n             require a fixed-rate, non-stopping TSC; refusing to run.\"\n        );\n        std::process::exit(1);\n    }\n    if !minstant::is_tsc_available() {\n        eprintln!(\n            \"error: TSC not selected as a kernel clocksource. \\\n             The CPU advertises invariant TSC, but the kernel has \\\n             rejected it — likely a sync or drift issue. actor-x1 \\\n             won't use a clock source the kernel considers unreliable; \\\n             refusing to run.\"\n        );\n        std::process::exit(1);\n    }\n}\n\n/// `CPUID.80000007h:EDX[bit 8]` — invariant TSC. Set iff the\n/// TSC runs at a constant rate regardless of P-state changes\n/// and keeps ticking in deep C-states. Both Intel and AMD\n/// expose the feature at this bit.\nfn has_invariant_tsc() -> bool {\n    use core::arch::x86_64::__cpuid;\n    let max_ext = unsafe { __cpuid(0x8000_0000) }.eax;\n    if max_ext < 0x8000_0007 {\n        return false;\n    }\n    let leaf = unsafe { __cpuid(0x8000_0007) };\n    (leaf.edx >> 8) & 1 == 1\n}\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/band_table.rs",
  "content": "//! Shared band-table renderer for tick-valued histograms.\n//!\n//! Both `TProbe` (fast path, direct-histogram) and `TProbe2`\n//! (scope API, records → drain) store hardware tick deltas and\n//! want the same band-table output shape — min/p1/…/p99/max\n//! rows with first/last/range/count/mean columns, plus summary\n//! lines for mean, stdev, mean min-p99, stdev min-p99. This\n//! module provides a single implementation both can call into.\n//!\n//! Display unit is chosen by `as_ticks`: `false` converts stored\n//! tick values to nanoseconds via [`crate::perf::ticks::ticks_per_ns`];\n//! `true` shows raw ticks.\n\nuse hdrhistogram::Histogram;\n\nuse crate::perf::fmt::{fmt_commas, fmt_commas_f64};\nuse crate::perf::ticks;\n\nconst BOUNDARY_PCTS: &[f64] = &[\n    0.0, 0.01, 0.10, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.99, 1.0,\n];\nconst BOUNDARY_NAMES: &[&str] = &[\n    \"min\", \"p1\", \"p10\", \"p20\", \"p30\", \"p40\", \"p50\", \"p60\", \"p70\", \"p80\", \"p90\", \"p99\", \"max\",\n];\n\n/// Render a band-table report for `hist`, interpreting stored\n/// values as hardware ticks. `kind` is the header label\n/// (`\"tprobe\"`, `\"tprobe2\"`, …) and `name` is the probe's name.\n/// `as_ticks=false` converts to ns; `true` keeps raw ticks.\npub(crate) fn render(kind: &str, name: &str, hist: &Histogram<u64>, as_ticks: bool) {\n    let sample_count = hist.len();\n    println!(\"  {kind}: {name} [count={}]\", fmt_commas(sample_count));\n    if sample_count == 0 {\n        println!();\n        return;\n    }\n\n    let unit = if as_ticks { \"tk\" } else { \"ns\" };\n    let tpn = ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n    let conv = |v: u64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v as f64 } else { v as f64 / tpn } };\n    let conv_f = |v: f64| -> f64 { if as_ticks { v } else { v / tpn } };\n\n    let n_bands = BOUNDARY_PCTS.len() - 1;\n    let mut band_first = vec![u64::MAX; n_bands];\n    let mut band_last = vec![0u64; n_bands];\n    let mut band_count = vec![0u64; n_bands];\n    let mut band_sum = vec![0u128; n_bands];\n\n    let mut cumulative = 0u64;\n    for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {\n        let value = iv.value_iterated_to();\n        let count = iv.count_at_value();\n        let mid_rank = (cumulative as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;\n        let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]\n            .iter()\n            .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)\n            .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1); // OK: mid_rank in [0,1]; last boundary is 1.0 so position() only misses at the exact upper edge, which maps to the last band\n        band_first[idx] = band_first[idx].min(value);\n        band_last[idx] = band_last[idx].max(value);\n        band_count[idx] += count;\n        band_sum[idx] += value as u128 * count as u128;\n        cumulative += count;\n    }\n\n    struct BandRow {\n        label: String,\n        first: String,\n        last: String,\n        range: String,\n        count: String,\n        mean: String,\n    }\n\n    let mut rows: Vec<BandRow> = Vec::new();\n    for i in 0..n_bands {\n        if band_count[i] == 0 {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let mean_val = band_sum[i] as f64 / band_count[i] as f64;\n        let range_raw = band_last[i] - band_first[i] + 1;\n        rows.push(BandRow {\n            label: format!(\"{}-{}\", BOUNDARY_NAMES[i], BOUNDARY_NAMES[i + 1]),\n            first: fmt_commas_f64(conv(band_first[i]), 0),\n            last: fmt_commas_f64(conv(band_last[i]), 0),\n            range: fmt_commas_f64(conv(range_raw), 0),\n            count: fmt_commas(band_count[i]),\n            mean: fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(mean_val), 0),\n        });\n    }\n\n    let label_w = rows\n        .iter()\n        .map(|r| r.label.len())\n        .max()\n        .unwrap_or(0) // OK: sample_count==0 returned above; rows is non-empty here\n        .max(\"stdev min-p99\".len());\n    let first_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.first.len()).max().unwrap_or(0); // OK: rows non-empty (see above)\n    let last_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.last.len()).max().unwrap_or(0); // OK: rows non-empty (see above)\n    let range_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.range.len()).max().unwrap_or(0); // OK: rows non-empty (see above)\n    let count_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.count.len()).max().unwrap_or(0); // OK: rows non-empty (see above)\n    let mean_w = rows.iter().map(|r| r.mean.len()).max().unwrap_or(0); // OK: rows non-empty (see above)\n\n    const INDENT: &str = \"    \";\n    const GAP: &str = \"    \";\n\n    let first_col = INDENT.len() + label_w + 1 + first_w;\n    let unit_len = 1 + unit.len();\n    let last_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + last_w;\n    let range_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + range_w;\n    let count_gap = unit_len + GAP.len() + count_w;\n    let mean_gap = GAP.len() + mean_w;\n    println!(\n        \"{:>first_col$}{:>last_gap$}{:>range_gap$}{:>count_gap$}{:>mean_gap$}\",\n        \"first\", \"last\", \"range\", \"count\", \"mean\",\n    );\n\n    for r in &rows {\n        println!(\n            \"{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>first_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>last_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>range_w$} {unit}{GAP}{:>count_w$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}\",\n            r.label, r.first, r.last, r.range, r.count, r.mean,\n        );\n    }\n\n    let hist_mean = hist.mean();\n    let skip = first_w\n        + unit_len\n        + GAP.len()\n        + last_w\n        + unit_len\n        + GAP.len()\n        + range_w\n        + unit_len\n        + GAP.len()\n        + count_w;\n    println!(\n        \"{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}\",\n        \"mean\",\n        \"\",\n        fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(hist_mean), 0),\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}\",\n        \"stdev\",\n        \"\",\n        fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(hist.stdev()), 0),\n    );\n\n    let trim_count: u64 = band_count[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();\n    if trim_count > 0 {\n        let trim_sum: u128 = band_sum[..n_bands - 1].iter().sum();\n        let trim_mean = trim_sum as f64 / trim_count as f64;\n\n        let mut trim_var_sum = 0.0f64;\n        let mut trim_var_count = 0u64;\n        let mut cum = 0u64;\n        for iv in hist.iter_recorded() {\n            let value = iv.value_iterated_to();\n            let count = iv.count_at_value();\n            let mid_rank = (cum as f64 + count as f64 / 2.0) / sample_count as f64;\n            let idx = BOUNDARY_PCTS[1..]\n                .iter()\n                .position(|&b| mid_rank < b)\n                .unwrap_or(n_bands - 1); // OK: mid_rank in [0,1]; same reasoning as above\n            if idx < n_bands - 1 {\n                let diff = value as f64 - trim_mean;\n                trim_var_sum += diff * diff * count as f64;\n                trim_var_count += count;\n            }\n            cum += count;\n        }\n        let trim_stdev = if trim_var_count > 1 {\n            (trim_var_sum / trim_var_count as f64).sqrt()\n        } else {\n            0.0\n        };\n\n        println!(\n            \"{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}\",\n            \"mean min-p99\",\n            \"\",\n            fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(trim_mean), 0),\n        );\n        println!(\n            \"{INDENT}{:<label_w$} {:>skip$}{GAP}{:>mean_w$} {unit}\",\n            \"stdev min-p99\",\n            \"\",\n            fmt_commas_f64(conv_f(trim_stdev), 0),\n        );\n    }\n    println!();\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/tprobe2.rs",
  "content": "//! Scope-based measurement probe: a named, single-writer\n//! histogram plus a record buffer, populated via `start` /\n//! `end` rather than `record(ticks)`.\n//!\n//! `start(site_id)` reads the hardware tick counter and returns\n//! an opaque [`TProbe2RecId`] carrying `(site_id, start_tsc)`;\n//! `end(id)` reads the tick counter again and appends a complete\n//! `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)` record to the probe's\n//! internal buffer. No delta math, histogram ingestion, or\n//! tick→ns conversion happens on the hot path — all of that is\n//! deferred to [`TProbe2::report`], which drains pending records\n//! into the histogram before rendering.\n//!\n//! This primitive preserves record-order information across\n//! interleaved scopes and sites (non-stack nesting is supported\n//! by construction) and gives future evolution space for per-site\n//! grouping, bounded buffers, background drain threads, and\n//! long-term trace retention.\n\nuse hdrhistogram::Histogram;\n\nuse crate::perf::band_table;\nuse crate::perf::ticks;\n\n/// Opaque handle returned by [`TProbe2::start`], consumed by\n/// [`TProbe2::end`]. Carries the caller-supplied `site_id` and\n/// the start-time tick reading; no probe-internal allocation\n/// happens at `start` time.\n///\n/// `#[must_use]` — dropping the id without passing it to\n/// [`TProbe2::end`] leaks the scope (no record is appended).\n#[must_use]\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\npub struct TProbe2RecId {\n    site_id: u64,\n    start_tsc: u64,\n}\n\n/// A complete scope record: `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)`.\n/// Appended at [`TProbe2::end`] time; the record buffer only\n/// ever holds complete records. Drained into the histogram at\n/// [`TProbe2::report`] time.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\nstruct Record {\n    #[allow(dead_code)] // read once per-site grouping lands.\n    site_id: u64,\n    start_tsc: u64,\n    end_tsc: u64,\n}\n\n/// A named, single-writer histogram of hardware tick-counter\n/// deltas plus a scope-record buffer. Not `Sync`; cross-thread\n/// *sharing* is out of scope. `Send` so probes can be moved\n/// between threads (e.g. returned via a `JoinHandle<TProbe2>`\n/// on shutdown).\npub struct TProbe2 {\n    name: String,\n    hist: Histogram<u64>,\n    records: Vec<Record>,\n}\n\nimpl TProbe2 {\n    /// Create an empty probe. Histogram upper bound is 1e12\n    /// ticks (~250 s at 4 GHz, ~100 s at 10 GHz), 3 significant\n    /// figures.\n    ///\n    /// Exits the process (code 1) if the hardware tick counter\n    /// isn't usable — see [`crate::perf::ticks::require_ok`].\n    pub fn new(name: &str) -> Self {\n        ticks::require_ok();\n        let _ = ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n        Self {\n            name: name.to_string(),\n            hist: Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000_000, 3).unwrap(), // OK: bounds are constant and valid (1 ≤ low < high, sigfigs in [0,5])\n            records: Vec::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Begin a scope. Reads the hardware tick counter and\n    /// returns an opaque [`TProbe2RecId`] carrying `(site_id,\n    /// start_tsc)`. The id must eventually be passed to\n    /// [`TProbe2::end`]; a dropped id leaves no record.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn start(&mut self, site_id: u64) -> TProbe2RecId {\n        TProbe2RecId {\n            site_id,\n            start_tsc: ticks::read_ticks(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// End the scope started by [`TProbe2::start`]. Reads the\n    /// hardware tick counter and appends a complete record\n    /// `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc)` to the probe's record\n    /// buffer. Delta and histogram ingestion are deferred to\n    /// [`TProbe2::report`].\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn end(&mut self, tpri: TProbe2RecId) {\n        let end_tsc = ticks::read_ticks();\n        self.records.push(Record {\n            site_id: tpri.site_id,\n            start_tsc: tpri.start_tsc,\n            end_tsc,\n        });\n    }\n\n    /// Render a band-table report for this probe. `as_ticks`\n    /// controls the display unit: `false` converts stored tick\n    /// deltas to nanoseconds (default for the CLI); `true` shows\n    /// raw ticks (`-t`/`--ticks`).\n    ///\n    /// Drains any pending `start`/`end` records into the histogram\n    /// before rendering: `delta = end_tsc − start_tsc`, clamped to\n    /// `1` since the histogram lower bound is 1.\n    pub fn report(&mut self, as_ticks: bool) {\n        for r in self.records.drain(..) {\n            let delta = r.end_tsc.saturating_sub(r.start_tsc);\n            self.hist.record(delta.max(1)).unwrap(); // OK: histogram bounds are [1, 1e12]; delta.max(1) ≥ 1, and tick deltas within probe lifetime stay well under 1e12\n        }\n        band_table::render(\"tprobe2\", &self.name, &self.hist, as_ticks);\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn start_end_appends_one_record() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(42);\n        p.end(id);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 1);\n        let r = &p.records[0];\n        assert_eq!(r.site_id, 42);\n        assert!(r.end_tsc >= r.start_tsc);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn start_end_preserves_start_tsc() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(7);\n        let saved_start = id.start_tsc;\n        p.end(id);\n        let r = &p.records[0];\n        assert_eq!(r.site_id, 7);\n        assert_eq!(r.start_tsc, saved_start);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn start_end_interleaved_non_stack() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let a = p.start(1);\n        let b = p.start(2);\n        p.end(a);\n        p.end(b);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 2);\n        assert_eq!(p.records[0].site_id, 1);\n        assert_eq!(p.records[1].site_id, 2);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn report_drains_records_into_histogram() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id1 = p.start(1);\n        p.end(id1);\n        let id2 = p.start(2);\n        p.end(id2);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 2);\n\n        p.report(false);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n\n        // Idempotent: a second report drains nothing, hist unchanged.\n        p.report(false);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n    }\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/runtime.rs",
  "content": "//! Runtimes that drive [`Actor`]s. The single-thread runtime lives\n//! here today; the multi-thread runtime arrives in 0.1.0-3.\n\nuse std::collections::VecDeque;\nuse std::time::{Duration, Instant};\n\nuse crate::perf::TProbe2;\nuse crate::{Actor, Context, Message};\n\n/// Single-threaded runtime: all actors run on the calling thread,\n/// sharing one FIFO message queue. Dispatch loops until either the\n/// caller-supplied deadline expires or the queue drains.\n///\n/// Every `handle_message` call is wrapped in a [`TProbe2`] scope\n/// (`site_id = dst_id`), so per-actor dispatch latency is available\n/// via [`Self::probe_mut`] after [`Self::run_for`] returns.\npub struct SingleThreadRuntime {\n    actors: Vec<Box<dyn Actor>>,\n    queue: VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,\n    probe: TProbe2,\n}\n\nimpl SingleThreadRuntime {\n    /// Create an empty runtime with no actors or pending messages.\n    /// `probe_name` names the embedded probe in its band-table\n    /// report (e.g. `\"goal1.dispatch\"`).\n    pub fn new(probe_name: &str) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            actors: Vec::new(),\n            queue: VecDeque::new(),\n            probe: TProbe2::new(probe_name),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Register `actor`; the returned `u32` is its stable id for\n    /// the lifetime of the runtime and is usable as `dst_id` in\n    /// [`Context::send`]. Ids are allocated sequentially from 0.\n    pub fn add_actor(&mut self, actor: Box<dyn Actor>) -> u32 {\n        let id = self.actors.len() as u32;\n        self.actors.push(actor);\n        id\n    }\n\n    /// Enqueue an initial message for `dst_id`. Used to prime the\n    /// dispatch loop before [`Self::run_for`].\n    pub fn seed(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {\n        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));\n    }\n\n    /// Dispatch messages until `duration` elapses or the queue\n    /// empties. Returns the total number of messages handled.\n    /// Each dispatch is bracketed by a probe `start` / `end` pair\n    /// with `site_id = dst_id`.\n    pub fn run_for(&mut self, duration: Duration) -> u64 {\n        let deadline = Instant::now() + duration;\n        let mut count = 0u64;\n        while Instant::now() < deadline {\n            let Some((dst, msg)) = self.queue.pop_front() else {\n                break;\n            };\n            let Self {\n                actors,\n                queue,\n                probe,\n            } = self;\n            let actor = &mut actors[dst as usize];\n            let mut ctx = SingleCtx { queue };\n            let id = probe.start(dst as u64);\n            actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, msg);\n            probe.end(id);\n            count += 1;\n        }\n        count\n    }\n\n    /// Mutable access to the embedded probe — used by callers to\n    /// invoke [`TProbe2::report`] after [`Self::run_for`] returns.\n    pub fn probe_mut(&mut self) -> &mut TProbe2 {\n        &mut self.probe\n    }\n}\n\n/// [`Context`] impl used by [`SingleThreadRuntime`]: pushes onto\n/// the runtime's shared queue.\nstruct SingleCtx<'a> {\n    queue: &'a mut VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,\n}\n\nimpl Context for SingleCtx<'_> {\n    /// Push `(dst_id, msg)` onto the shared dispatch queue.\n    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {\n        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// Minimal actor that drops every message without replying.\n    struct Sink;\n\n    impl Actor for Sink {\n        fn handle_message(&mut self, _ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {}\n    }\n\n    /// Bounded ping-pong actor: replies to its peer up to `max`\n    /// times, then goes silent. Used to produce a deterministic\n    /// message count in unit tests without wall-clock dependence.\n    struct Bouncer {\n        peer: u32,\n        sent: u32,\n        max: u32,\n    }\n\n    impl Actor for Bouncer {\n        fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {\n            if self.sent < self.max {\n                ctx.send(self.peer, Message);\n                self.sent += 1;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn seed_is_handled_and_queue_drains() {\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"test.drain\");\n        let id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink));\n        rt.seed(id, Message);\n        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_millis(1));\n        assert_eq!(n, 1);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn bounded_ping_pong_count() {\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"test.bounded\");\n        let a = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 1,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 5,\n        }));\n        let _b = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 0,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 5,\n        }));\n        rt.seed(a, Message);\n        // seed (1) + 5 from a + 5 from b = 11\n        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs(1));\n        assert_eq!(n, 11);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn add_actor_assigns_sequential_ids() {\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"test.ids\");\n        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 0);\n        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 1);\n        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 2);\n    }\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs",
  "content": "//! Goal1 binary: two actors on a single thread ping-pong an empty\n//! [`Message`] for a caller-supplied duration in seconds (f64).\n//! Prints messages-handled/throughput and a `tprobe2` band-table\n//! report of per-dispatch latency (ns by default, ticks with\n//! `-t`/`--ticks`).\n//!\n//! Usage: `goal1 <duration_secs> [-t|--ticks]`\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse actor_x1::runtime::SingleThreadRuntime;\nuse actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};\n\n/// Actor that, on every message received, sends exactly one message\n/// back to its configured peer.\nstruct PingPongActor {\n    peer_id: u32,\n}\n\nimpl Actor for PingPongActor {\n    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`. Never blocks.\n    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {\n        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);\n    }\n}\n\n/// Parsed command-line arguments.\nstruct Cli {\n    duration_s: f64,\n    as_ticks: bool,\n}\n\n/// Parse argv. Exits(2) with a usage line on any parse error.\nfn parse_cli() -> Cli {\n    let mut as_ticks = false;\n    let mut duration_s: Option<f64> = None;\n    for arg in std::env::args().skip(1) {\n        match arg.as_str() {\n            \"-t\" | \"--ticks\" => as_ticks = true,\n            other => {\n                if duration_s.is_some() {\n                    eprintln!(\"goal1: unexpected argument: {other}\");\n                    usage_and_exit();\n                }\n                match other.parse::<f64>() {\n                    Ok(v) if v >= 0.0 && v.is_finite() => duration_s = Some(v),\n                    _ => {\n                        eprintln!(\"goal1: duration must be a non-negative finite number: {other}\");\n                        usage_and_exit();\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    let Some(duration_s) = duration_s else {\n        usage_and_exit();\n    };\n    Cli {\n        duration_s,\n        as_ticks,\n    }\n}\n\n/// Print usage to stderr and exit with code 2.\nfn usage_and_exit() -> ! {\n    eprintln!(\"usage: goal1 <duration_secs> [-t|--ticks]\");\n    std::process::exit(2);\n}\n\n/// Run two ping-pong actors on one thread for the CLI-supplied\n/// duration, then print message count, throughput, and the\n/// per-dispatch `tprobe2` band-table report.\nfn main() {\n    let cli = parse_cli();\n    let duration = Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.duration_s);\n\n    let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"goal1.dispatch\");\n    let a_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 }));\n    let b_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 }));\n    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));\n    rt.seed(a_id, Message);\n\n    let count = rt.run_for(duration);\n    let mmps = count as f64 / cli.duration_s / 1e6;\n    println!(\n        \"goal1: {count} messages in {:.3}s ({mmps:.3} M msg/s)\",\n        cli.duration_s,\n    );\n    rt.probe_mut().report(cli.as_ticks);\n}\n"
}
```
