> AGENT

Step 0.1.0-2 complete. Summary:

- **`Cargo.toml`** — bump to `0.1.0-2`; add `hdrhistogram = "7"` and `minstant = "0.1"`.
- **`src/perf/`** (new module) — vendored from `../iiac-perf/src`:
  - `mod.rs` — module root documenting source and divergences.
  - `tprobe2.rs`, `band_table.rs`, `ticks.rs`, `ticks/x86_64.rs` — vendored near-verbatim; import paths rewritten to `crate::perf::*`, the `compile_error!` text now says "actor-x1 probes", and every `unwrap*` gained a `// OK: …` justification per our convention.
  - `fmt.rs` — new, extracts just `fmt_commas` + `fmt_commas_f64` from `iiac-perf/src/harness.rs` (lines 160–188) so we don't drag in the 393-line harness.
- **`src/lib.rs`** — adds `pub mod perf;`.
- **`src/runtime.rs`** — `SingleThreadRuntime::new(probe_name)` now embeds a `TProbe2`; `run_for` wraps each dispatch with `probe.start(dst as u64)` / `probe.end(id)`; `probe_mut()` lets the caller report; `Default` impl dropped (new takes an arg); tests updated to pass a name.
- **`src/bin/goal1.rs`** — new arg parser: `<duration_secs> [-t|--ticks]`; usage errors now `exit(2)` with the usage line; calls `rt.probe_mut().report(as_ticks)` after the run.
- **`notes/chores-01.md`** — new `## Vendored perf probe + Goal1 instrumentation (0.1.0-2)` section with smoke numbers.

Pre-commit checklist:
- `cargo fmt --check`: clean.
- `cargo clippy -D warnings`: clean (new crate deps compiled too: `hdrhistogram`, `minstant`, transitive ~20 crates).
- `cargo test`: 7/7 pass (3 runtime + 4 tprobe2).
- `cargo install --path .`: built release, `goal1` replaced in `~/.cargo/bin`.
- Retest: `goal1 0.5` → 10.56 M msgs (21.1 M msg/s), per-dispatch mean min-p99 = 9 ns. `goal1 0.5 -t` → same, 34 tk. `goal1` with no args → usage + exit 2.

Throughput drops roughly in half vs. 0.1.0-1 (38.7 → 21.1 M msg/s) — the bot thinks this is two `rdtsc` reads + `Vec::push` per dispatch, matching the ~20 ns delta; the 9 ns the probe reports is the `handle_message` body itself.

`notes/todo.md` — Stage 1 PoC is already in-progress; no change. `notes/README.md` — nothing functional worth pointing at yet (per-binary usage is still in `//!` docstrings); deferring any README update until Stage 1 is fully done.

Ready for your review.

> DEVELOPER

let's to p1-10,p10-p20 .. p90-99,p99-max and why are two of the first,last both 10,10?

> AGENT

**Why two rows show `10 ns, 10 ns`**: look at the `-t` output — those rows are at **37 tk** and **38 tk**. Those are different histogram buckets, but at ~3.7 GHz both convert to ~10 ns, and `fmt_commas_f64(..., 0)` rounds to 0 decimals → both display as "10". Different data, same rendered ns. Not a bug, just the display resolution being coarser than the underlying tick delta.

The "bands we can see" gap comes from a different cause: the renderer iterates **histogram buckets** (not samples) and assigns each bucket to the band whose range contains the bucket's cumulative mid-rank. When the distribution is a tight spike with just a few huge buckets, most percentile bands end up with zero buckets assigned and get skipped. The `if band_count[i] == 0 { continue; }` then omits the row.

**To always render the bands you listed**, drop the skip-when-empty guard and emit a row per band regardless. Two variants:

| Variant | Bands rendered | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | `p1-p10, p10-p20, …, p90-p99, p99-max` (11) | Matches your listing literally; `min-p1` disappears but samples at mid-rank < 0.01 would have nowhere to go (we'd reassign them to `p1-p10`) |
| **B** | `min-p1, p1-p10, …, p99-max` (12) | Adds `min-p1` as the first row; the bot thinks this is the spirit of your ask and preserves all data bins |

The bot thinks you probably meant **B** (the `..` in your enumeration likely elides the tail, not the head; dropping `min-p1` loses the bottom 1% of samples in the general case), but your enumeration reads as **A** literally. Which do you want?

**Scope note**: this modification lives in `src/perf/band_table.rs`, which we vendored verbatim from `iiac-perf`. This change diverges from upstream — I'll document it in the `## Vendored perf probe …` chores entry as another divergence. Want me to also drop a quick note in `iiac-perf` later, or just let the drift sit until the shared-lib promotion?

> DEVELOPER

Maybe we need to add an "inner" in collect better data?

> AGENT

Yes — an `inner` loop amortizes the probe overhead across N dispatches per scope, and just as usefully, pushes the stored tick values into a range where `hdrhistogram`'s 0.1% relative buckets actually resolve small variations. Right now our samples live at 37–38 tk, where the bucket width is 1 tk and a real ~2 ns jitter has nowhere to go; at `inner=1000` the scope measures ~38,000 tk and a few hundred ticks of jitter naturally spreads across many buckets, which should populate many more percentile bands *without* any change to `band_table.rs`.

**Concrete proposal**

- CLI: `goal1 <duration_secs> [--inner N] [-t|--ticks]` (default `inner=1`, preserves current behavior).
- Runtime: `run_for(duration, inner: u64) -> u64` wraps `inner` dispatches per probe scope; if the queue empties mid-batch, discard the partial scope.
- `TProbe2`: add `end_batch(id, n)` that records `delta / n` so the histogram always stores per-event cost — the report reads per-dispatch no matter what `inner` is. Existing `end(id)` becomes `end_batch(id, 1)` semantically.

**Tradeoff**: you lose per-dispatch outliers — a single OS preemption inside a batch shows up as `1/inner` of its true magnitude smeared across the scope. For a ping-pong workload that's an acceptable loss since we're measuring average dispatch cost anyway, but if we later want to catch individual tail events we'd want a second probe with inner=1 (or a separate mode flag).

**Recommendation**: do `inner` first, then re-examine the band-rendering output. The bot thinks the "two 10/10 rows" and "only 4 bands" issues will both resolve on their own once the histogram has real dynamic range to work with. If they don't, we can still switch to always-show-all-bands after.

Sound good? And do you want `inner` as its own step (`0.1.0-3`) and push Goal2 to `-4`, or fold it into the end of `0.1.0-2`?

> DEVELOPER

We also should add a --warmup with a default of 10s, maybe time to move to clap?

> AGENT

Yes, time for clap. We're at four args (`<duration>`, `--inner`, `--warmup`, `-t/--ticks`) with defaults and validation — the hand-rolled parser has outgrown its complexity budget, and iiac-perf already uses `clap = { version = "4", features = ["derive", "wrap_help"] }` so we have a shape to match.

**Warmup semantics** (proposing):
- Run the dispatch loop identically for `--warmup <seconds>` (default **10s**).
- Clear the probe (new `TProbe2::clear()` that empties both records and the histogram).
- Run the measurement loop for `<duration_secs>`, record, report.
- **Only measurement-phase counts** are printed — warmup is silent.

`TProbe2::clear()` is a small addition (`self.records.clear(); self.hist.clear();` — hdrhistogram has `.clear()`) and genuinely belongs on the type. Divergence from upstream noted in chores.

**Revised step 0.1.0-3** (all as one commit):
- Add `clap` dep to `Cargo.toml`.
- `Cli` derive-struct in `src/bin/goal1.rs` with `duration_s` positional, `--inner` (default 1), `--warmup` (default 10.0), `-t/--ticks`.
- Add `TProbe2::clear()` + `TProbe2::end_batch(id, n)`.
- `SingleThreadRuntime::run_for(duration, inner)` — scope wraps `inner` dispatches; partial trailing batch discarded (no record, no count).
- New `SingleThreadRuntime::warmup_for(duration, inner)` — same loop, same record/count accounting, but caller clears the probe afterward.

**Revised ladder**: `0.1.0-3` = inner + warmup + clap (this step), `0.1.0-4` = Goal2, `0.1.0` = final. Five total commits, vs. four planned — one extra but they're each small.

**Tradeoffs worth naming**:
- clap pulls in ~15 small crates (proc-macro2, syn, quote, …) — compile time goes up ~3–5s first build. Not runtime cost.
- "Partial trailing batch discarded" means very short `duration` may record 0 samples. At `--inner 1000` and duration 0.001s that's likely; at typical usage (duration ≥ 0.1s, inner ≤ 10000) it's a non-issue.
- Warmup messages still count as messages handled from the system's perspective, but we don't expose that count. The bot thinks that's the right call — users care about measured throughput, not total work done.

OK to proceed with step 0.1.0-3 as specified? Any tweaks to warmup default or semantics before I start?

> DEVELOPER

From a timing pov warmup shouldn't count, I don't think at a minimum it introduces more noise making it harder to evaluate code changes, thoughts?

> AGENT

Agreed — as proposed, the warmup count is already discarded (the `u64` from `warmup_for` goes nowhere; only `run_for`'s count drives the throughput line). Your reasoning is the right framing: each reported number reflects exactly `<duration>` seconds of stable-state dispatch, so code-change A/B is clean.

One small design choice worth settling while we're here — does warmup run the probe or not?

- **Option X**: warmup calls `probe.start`/`end` same as measurement; we clear records after. Upside: warmup's instruction stream is identical to measurement's, so cache/branch predictor/freq land in the same state. Only difference between phases is `probe.clear()` at the boundary.
- **Option Y**: warmup skips the probe calls entirely. Slightly fewer instructions during warmup; no records to clear.

The bot thinks **X** is the more defensible default — the whole point of warmup is to match what measurement will look like, and the probe itself is part of the measurement-phase hot path. Also collapses down to one shared inner helper instead of two divergent loops. Sound right?

> DEVELOPER

X, also eventually I want to subtract measure & loop overhead which we'd do after warmup, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good call, and "after warmup" is the right point in the pipeline — calibration needs a thermally-stable system to produce meaningful numbers.

**Two different things worth disambiguating**:
- **Measure overhead** (what iiac-perf's `overhead.rs` subtracts) — the cost *inside* the scope that isn't the measured work: the second `rdtsc` return, the call/ret into `end()`, and any out-of-order serialization. Calibrated by running many empty `probe.start(); probe.end()` pairs with no work between and taking the min/p1 of the delta. This is what contaminates probe readings today.
- **Loop overhead** — the per-iteration `pop_front`, deadline check, split-borrow, `SingleCtx` construction. This is *outside* the probe scope in our current design, so it doesn't pollute probe readings — but it does account for a chunk of the gap between per-dispatch probe mean (~9 ns) and total throughput (~47 ns/dispatch). The bot thinks this one doesn't need subtraction from probe samples; it'd be a separate diagnostic line ("loop overhead ≈ total_ns/count − probe_mean") if we want it visible.

**Recommended step split**:
- `0.1.0-3` — inner + warmup + clap (Option X). Keep it scoped.
- `0.1.0-4` — overhead calibration: add `Overhead` struct (vendored-ish from iiac-perf), calibration phase between warmup and measurement, subtract from probe samples at report time. `--raw` or `--no-overhead` flag added then, not preemptively.
- `0.1.0-5` — Goal2.
- `0.1.0` — final.

Six commits now, which is more than we started with, but each is focused. Alternative if six feels too many: fold overhead into `0.1.0-4` *alongside* Goal2 — risks a noisier diff but saves one commit. The bot leans toward keeping them separate since overhead calibration touches the perf module and Goal2 touches the runtime module — different surface areas.

Start `0.1.0-3` now?

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/tprobe2.rs",
  "content": "//! Scope-based measurement probe: a named, single-writer\n//! histogram plus a record buffer, populated via `start` /\n//! `end` (or `end_batch`) rather than `record(ticks)`.\n//!\n//! `start(site_id)` reads the hardware tick counter and returns\n//! an opaque [`TProbe2RecId`] carrying `(site_id, start_tsc)`;\n//! `end(id)` reads the tick counter again and appends a complete\n//! `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc, batch=1)` record to the probe's\n//! internal buffer. [`TProbe2::end_batch`] is the same but records\n//! a caller-supplied batch size `n`, which [`TProbe2::report`]\n//! uses to divide the scope's tick delta back down to a per-event\n//! cost before histogram ingestion. No delta math, histogram\n//! ingestion, or tick→ns conversion happens on the hot path.\n//!\n//! This primitive preserves record-order information across\n//! interleaved scopes and sites (non-stack nesting is supported\n//! by construction) and gives future evolution space for per-site\n//! grouping, bounded buffers, background drain threads, and\n//! long-term trace retention.\n//!\n//! Divergences from the `iiac-perf` original that this file was\n//! vendored from:\n//! - [`Record`] gained a `batch: u64` field.\n//! - [`TProbe2::end_batch`] added — scope covers `n` events,\n//!   histogram stores per-event cost.\n//! - [`TProbe2::clear`] added — resets records and histogram,\n//!   used at the warmup→measurement boundary.\n\nuse hdrhistogram::Histogram;\n\nuse crate::perf::band_table;\nuse crate::perf::ticks;\n\n/// Opaque handle returned by [`TProbe2::start`], consumed by\n/// [`TProbe2::end`] or [`TProbe2::end_batch`]. Carries the\n/// caller-supplied `site_id` and the start-time tick reading;\n/// no probe-internal allocation happens at `start` time.\n///\n/// `#[must_use]` — dropping the id without passing it to `end*`\n/// leaks the scope (no record is appended).\n#[must_use]\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\npub struct TProbe2RecId {\n    site_id: u64,\n    start_tsc: u64,\n}\n\n/// A complete scope record: `(site_id, start_tsc, end_tsc, batch)`.\n/// Appended at `end*` time; the record buffer only ever holds\n/// complete records. Drained into the histogram at\n/// [`TProbe2::report`] time, dividing `end_tsc − start_tsc` by\n/// `batch` so histogram values are always per-event costs.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]\nstruct Record {\n    #[allow(dead_code)] // read once per-site grouping lands.\n    site_id: u64,\n    start_tsc: u64,\n    end_tsc: u64,\n    batch: u64,\n}\n\n/// A named, single-writer histogram of hardware tick-counter\n/// deltas plus a scope-record buffer. Not `Sync`; cross-thread\n/// *sharing* is out of scope. `Send` so probes can be moved\n/// between threads (e.g. returned via a `JoinHandle<TProbe2>`\n/// on shutdown).\npub struct TProbe2 {\n    name: String,\n    hist: Histogram<u64>,\n    records: Vec<Record>,\n}\n\nimpl TProbe2 {\n    /// Create an empty probe. Histogram upper bound is 1e12\n    /// ticks (~250 s at 4 GHz, ~100 s at 10 GHz), 3 significant\n    /// figures.\n    ///\n    /// Exits the process (code 1) if the hardware tick counter\n    /// isn't usable — see [`crate::perf::ticks::require_ok`].\n    pub fn new(name: &str) -> Self {\n        ticks::require_ok();\n        let _ = ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n        Self {\n            name: name.to_string(),\n            hist: Histogram::<u64>::new_with_bounds(1, 1_000_000_000_000, 3).unwrap(), // OK: bounds are constant and valid (1 ≤ low < high, sigfigs in [0,5])\n            records: Vec::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Begin a scope. Reads the hardware tick counter and\n    /// returns an opaque [`TProbe2RecId`] carrying `(site_id,\n    /// start_tsc)`. The id must eventually be passed to\n    /// [`TProbe2::end`] or [`TProbe2::end_batch`]; a dropped id\n    /// leaves no record.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn start(&mut self, site_id: u64) -> TProbe2RecId {\n        TProbe2RecId {\n            site_id,\n            start_tsc: ticks::read_ticks(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// End a single-event scope (batch = 1). Reads the hardware\n    /// tick counter and appends a complete record. Delta and\n    /// histogram ingestion are deferred to [`TProbe2::report`].\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn end(&mut self, tpri: TProbe2RecId) {\n        let end_tsc = ticks::read_ticks();\n        self.records.push(Record {\n            site_id: tpri.site_id,\n            start_tsc: tpri.start_tsc,\n            end_tsc,\n            batch: 1,\n        });\n    }\n\n    /// End a batched scope covering `n` events. The histogram\n    /// stores `(end − start) / n` at [`Self::report`] time so\n    /// per-event cost is what gets rendered, regardless of batch\n    /// size. Larger batches amortize probe apparatus overhead\n    /// and push stored tick values into a range where the\n    /// histogram's 0.1 %-relative buckets actually resolve small\n    /// variations.\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn end_batch(&mut self, tpri: TProbe2RecId, n: u64) {\n        let end_tsc = ticks::read_ticks();\n        self.records.push(Record {\n            site_id: tpri.site_id,\n            start_tsc: tpri.start_tsc,\n            end_tsc,\n            batch: n,\n        });\n    }\n\n    /// Discard all pending records and zero the histogram. Used\n    /// at the warmup → measurement boundary so only steady-state\n    /// data reaches the report.\n    pub fn clear(&mut self) {\n        self.records.clear();\n        self.hist.reset();\n    }\n\n    /// Render a band-table report for this probe. `as_ticks`\n    /// controls the display unit: `false` converts stored tick\n    /// deltas to nanoseconds (default for the CLI); `true` shows\n    /// raw ticks (`-t`/`--ticks`).\n    ///\n    /// Drains any pending `start` / `end*` records into the\n    /// histogram before rendering: `delta = (end_tsc − start_tsc)\n    /// / batch`, clamped to `1` since the histogram lower bound\n    /// is 1.\n    pub fn report(&mut self, as_ticks: bool) {\n        for r in self.records.drain(..) {\n            let delta = r.end_tsc.saturating_sub(r.start_tsc);\n            let batch = r.batch.max(1);\n            let per_event = (delta + batch / 2) / batch;\n            self.hist.record(per_event.max(1)).unwrap(); // OK: histogram bounds are [1, 1e12]; per_event.max(1) ≥ 1, and per-event tick deltas stay well under 1e12\n        }\n        band_table::render(\"tprobe2\", &self.name, &self.hist, as_ticks);\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn start_end_appends_one_record() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(42);\n        p.end(id);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 1);\n        let r = &p.records[0];\n        assert_eq!(r.site_id, 42);\n        assert_eq!(r.batch, 1);\n        assert!(r.end_tsc >= r.start_tsc);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn start_end_preserves_start_tsc() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(7);\n        let saved_start = id.start_tsc;\n        p.end(id);\n        let r = &p.records[0];\n        assert_eq!(r.site_id, 7);\n        assert_eq!(r.start_tsc, saved_start);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn start_end_interleaved_non_stack() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let a = p.start(1);\n        let b = p.start(2);\n        p.end(a);\n        p.end(b);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 2);\n        assert_eq!(p.records[0].site_id, 1);\n        assert_eq!(p.records[1].site_id, 2);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn end_batch_stores_batch_size() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(3);\n        p.end_batch(id, 100);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 1);\n        assert_eq!(p.records[0].site_id, 3);\n        assert_eq!(p.records[0].batch, 100);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn report_divides_batched_delta() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        // Inject a synthetic record: 1000 tick delta, batch 10.\n        p.records.push(Record {\n            site_id: 0,\n            start_tsc: 0,\n            end_tsc: 1000,\n            batch: 10,\n        });\n        p.report(true);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 1);\n        let v = p.hist.iter_recorded().next().unwrap().value_iterated_to();\n        // (1000 + 5) / 10 = 100.\n        assert_eq!(v, 100);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn report_drains_records_into_histogram() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id1 = p.start(1);\n        p.end(id1);\n        let id2 = p.start(2);\n        p.end(id2);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 2);\n\n        p.report(false);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n\n        // Idempotent: a second report drains nothing, hist unchanged.\n        p.report(false);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn clear_empties_records_and_hist() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(1);\n        p.end(id);\n        p.report(false); // moves record into hist\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 1);\n        p.clear();\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);\n    }\n}\n"
}

  "content": "//! Runtimes that drive [`Actor`]s. The single-thread runtime lives\n//! here today; the multi-thread runtime arrives in a later step.\n\nuse std::collections::VecDeque;\nuse std::time::{Duration, Instant};\n\nuse crate::perf::TProbe2;\nuse crate::{Actor, Context, Message};\n\n/// Single-threaded runtime: all actors run on the calling thread,\n/// sharing one FIFO message queue. Dispatch loops until either the\n/// caller-supplied deadline expires or the queue drains.\n///\n/// Dispatch is wrapped in a [`TProbe2`] batched scope covering\n/// `inner` consecutive `handle_message` calls; per-event latency\n/// is available via [`Self::probe_mut`] after [`Self::run_for`]\n/// returns. The scope's `site_id` is the first `dst_id` in the\n/// batch (arbitrary-but-stable when `inner > 1`).\npub struct SingleThreadRuntime {\n    actors: Vec<Box<dyn Actor>>,\n    queue: VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,\n    probe: TProbe2,\n}\n\nimpl SingleThreadRuntime {\n    /// Create an empty runtime with no actors or pending messages.\n    /// `probe_name` names the embedded probe in its band-table\n    /// report (e.g. `\"goal1.dispatch\"`).\n    pub fn new(probe_name: &str) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            actors: Vec::new(),\n            queue: VecDeque::new(),\n            probe: TProbe2::new(probe_name),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Register `actor`; the returned `u32` is its stable id for\n    /// the lifetime of the runtime and is usable as `dst_id` in\n    /// [`Context::send`]. Ids are allocated sequentially from 0.\n    pub fn add_actor(&mut self, actor: Box<dyn Actor>) -> u32 {\n        let id = self.actors.len() as u32;\n        self.actors.push(actor);\n        id\n    }\n\n    /// Enqueue an initial message for `dst_id`. Used to prime the\n    /// dispatch loop before [`Self::run_for`].\n    pub fn seed(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {\n        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));\n    }\n\n    /// Dispatch messages until `duration` elapses or the queue\n    /// empties. Each probe scope wraps `inner` consecutive\n    /// dispatches. A partial trailing batch (queue emptied\n    /// mid-scope) is discarded — neither recorded nor counted —\n    /// so throughput numbers reflect whole-batch work only.\n    ///\n    /// Returns the total number of messages counted toward\n    /// whole batches.\n    pub fn run_for(&mut self, duration: Duration, inner: u64) -> u64 {\n        let deadline = Instant::now() + duration;\n        let mut count = 0u64;\n        while Instant::now() < deadline {\n            let first_dst = match self.queue.front() {\n                Some((d, _)) => *d,\n                None => break,\n            };\n            let Self {\n                actors,\n                queue,\n                probe,\n            } = self;\n            let id = probe.start(first_dst as u64);\n            let mut done: u64 = 0;\n            while done < inner {\n                let Some((dst, msg)) = queue.pop_front() else {\n                    break;\n                };\n                let actor = &mut actors[dst as usize];\n                let mut ctx = SingleCtx { queue };\n                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, msg);\n                done += 1;\n            }\n            if done == inner {\n                probe.end_batch(id, inner);\n                count += done;\n            } else {\n                // Partial trailing batch: drop the scope (don't\n                // record or count) and stop.\n                break;\n            }\n        }\n        count\n    }\n\n    /// Mutable access to the embedded probe — used by callers to\n    /// invoke [`TProbe2::clear`] at the warmup → measurement\n    /// boundary and [`TProbe2::report`] after [`Self::run_for`]\n    /// returns.\n    pub fn probe_mut(&mut self) -> &mut TProbe2 {\n        &mut self.probe\n    }\n}\n\n/// [`Context`] impl used by [`SingleThreadRuntime`]: pushes onto\n/// the runtime's shared queue.\nstruct SingleCtx<'a> {\n    queue: &'a mut VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,\n}\n\nimpl Context for SingleCtx<'_> {\n    /// Push `(dst_id, msg)` onto the shared dispatch queue.\n    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {\n        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// Minimal actor that drops every message without replying.\n    struct Sink;\n\n    impl Actor for Sink {\n        fn handle_message(&mut self, _ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {}\n    }\n\n    /// Bounded ping-pong actor: replies to its peer up to `max`\n    /// times, then goes silent. Used to produce a deterministic\n    /// message count in unit tests without wall-clock dependence.\n    struct Bouncer {\n        peer: u32,\n        sent: u32,\n        max: u32,\n    }\n\n    impl Actor for Bouncer {\n        fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {\n            if self.sent < self.max {\n                ctx.send(self.peer, Message);\n                self.sent += 1;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn seed_is_handled_and_queue_drains() {\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"test.drain\");\n        let id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink));\n        rt.seed(id, Message);\n        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_millis(1), 1);\n        assert_eq!(n, 1);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn bounded_ping_pong_count() {\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"test.bounded\");\n        let a = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 1,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 5,\n        }));\n        let _b = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 0,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 5,\n        }));\n        rt.seed(a, Message);\n        // seed (1) + 5 from a + 5 from b = 11, all whole batches at inner=1.\n        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs(1), 1);\n        assert_eq!(n, 11);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn add_actor_assigns_sequential_ids() {\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"test.ids\");\n        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 0);\n        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 1);\n        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 2);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn partial_trailing_batch_is_dropped() {\n        // 3 total dispatches available, inner=2 ⇒ one whole batch\n        // counted (2) and one partial (1) discarded.\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"test.partial\");\n        let a = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 1,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 1,\n        }));\n        let _b = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 0,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 1,\n        }));\n        rt.seed(a, Message);\n        // Events: seed→b, b→a (b.sent=1), a→b (a.sent=1), b stops.\n        // 3 dispatches total. inner=2 ⇒ first batch of 2 recorded\n        // (count=2), then partial batch of 1 discarded.\n        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs(1), 2);\n        assert_eq!(n, 2);\n    }\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/bin/goal1.rs",
  "content": "//! Goal1 binary: two actors on a single thread ping-pong an empty\n//! [`Message`] for a caller-supplied duration in seconds (f64),\n//! after an optional warmup phase. Prints messages-handled /\n//! throughput and a `tprobe2` band-table report of per-dispatch\n//! latency.\n//!\n//! Usage: `goal1 --help`\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse actor_x1::runtime::SingleThreadRuntime;\nuse actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};\nuse clap::Parser;\n\n/// Actor that, on every message received, sends exactly one message\n/// back to its configured peer.\nstruct PingPongActor {\n    peer_id: u32,\n}\n\nimpl Actor for PingPongActor {\n    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`. Never blocks.\n    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {\n        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);\n    }\n}\n\n/// CLI for goal1: single-thread two-actor ping-pong with warmup,\n/// per-dispatch probe batching, and ns-or-ticks band-table report.\n#[derive(Parser, Debug)]\n#[command(version, about, long_about = None)]\nstruct Cli {\n    /// Measurement window in seconds (runs after warmup).\n    #[arg(value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]\n    duration_s: f64,\n\n    /// Warmup duration in seconds before measurement. Runs the\n    /// same dispatch loop as the measurement phase (probe active)\n    /// so cache/branch-predictor/frequency land in the same state\n    /// by the time the probe is cleared and measurement begins.\n    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 10.0, value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]\n    warmup: f64,\n\n    /// Number of dispatches per probe scope. Larger values\n    /// amortize probe apparatus overhead and push stored tick\n    /// values into a range where the histogram's 0.1 %-relative\n    /// buckets resolve small variations.\n    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 1, value_parser = clap::value_parser!(u64).range(1..))]\n    inner: u64,\n\n    /// Display probe values as raw ticks instead of nanoseconds.\n    #[arg(short = 't', long)]\n    ticks: bool,\n}\n\n/// `value_parser` helper: reject negative, NaN, and infinite values.\nfn parse_non_negative_secs(s: &str) -> Result<f64, String> {\n    let v: f64 = s.parse().map_err(|e: std::num::ParseFloatError| e.to_string())?;\n    if !v.is_finite() || v < 0.0 {\n        Err(format!(\"'{s}' is not a non-negative finite number\"))\n    } else {\n        Ok(v)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Parse CLI, run warmup + measurement on two single-thread\n/// ping-pong actors, print message count + throughput, then\n/// render the per-dispatch band-table report.\nfn main() {\n    let cli = Cli::parse();\n\n    let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"goal1.dispatch\");\n    let a_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 }));\n    let b_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 }));\n    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));\n    rt.seed(a_id, Message);\n\n    // Warmup: same loop, probe active, records discarded at the boundary.\n    rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.warmup), cli.inner);\n    rt.probe_mut().clear();\n\n    // Measurement.\n    let count = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.duration_s), cli.inner);\n    let mmps = count as f64 / cli.duration_s / 1e6;\n    println!(\n        \"goal1: {count} messages in {:.3}s ({mmps:.3} M msg/s, inner={})\",\n        cli.duration_s, cli.inner,\n    );\n    rt.probe_mut().report(cli.ticks);\n}\n"
}

```
An experiment in the actor model in Rust (Communicating Sequential Processes, Hoare 1978).

Usage: goal1 [OPTIONS] <DURATION_S>

Arguments:
  <DURATION_S>  Measurement window in seconds (runs after warmup)

Options:
      --warmup <WARMUP>  Warmup duration in seconds before measurement. Runs the same dispatch loop
                         as the measurement phase (probe active) so cache/branch-predictor/frequency
                         land in the same state by the time the probe is cleared and measurement
                         begins [default: 10]
      --inner <INNER>    Number of dispatches per probe scope. Larger values amortize probe
                         apparatus overhead and push stored tick values into a range where the
                         histogram's 0.1 %-relative buckets resolve small variations [default: 1]
  -t, --ticks            Display probe values as raw ticks instead of nanoseconds
  -h, --help             Print help
  -V, --version          Print version
```

  "new_string": "Adding the probe cuts throughput roughly in half vs. 0.1.0-1\n(~38.7 → ~21.1 M msg/s). The bot thinks the delta is dominated by\ntwo `rdtsc` reads plus `Vec::push` per dispatch (~20 ns of the\n~47 ns per-dispatch cost); the 9 ns measured by the probe is the\n`handle_message` body alone.\n\n## Inner batching, warmup, clap (0.1.0-3)\n\nAdds CLI polish and measurement-quality improvements to Goal1.\nScope:\n\n- **Inner batching** — one probe scope now covers `--inner N`\n  consecutive dispatches (default 1). At larger `N` the probe's\n  two-`rdtsc`-plus-push apparatus cost is amortized, and stored\n  tick values land in a range where the histogram's 0.1 %-relative\n  buckets actually resolve jitter.\n- **Warmup** — `--warmup SECS` (default **10.0**) runs the same\n  dispatch loop with the probe active, then clears probe state;\n  measurement phase inherits stable-state CPU freq / cache /\n  branch-predictor conditions. Warmup message counts are never\n  reported.\n- **clap** — hand-rolled arg parsing replaced with a derive-based\n  `Cli`. Validates non-negative finite `duration_s` and `warmup`;\n  enforces `inner ≥ 1`. Auto-generated `--help` / `--version`.\n- **TProbe2 additions** (vendored-code divergence from iiac-perf):\n  - `Record` gains a `batch: u64` field.\n  - `TProbe2::end_batch(id, n)` appends with `batch = n`; existing\n    `end(id)` sets `batch = 1`.\n  - `TProbe2::report` now divides stored deltas by `batch` at\n    drain time so the histogram always holds per-event cost,\n    regardless of scope granularity.\n  - `TProbe2::clear()` resets records buffer and histogram;\n    called between warmup and measurement.\n\nFile-level edits:\n\n- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-3; add\n  `clap = { version = \"4\", features = [\"derive\", \"wrap_help\"] }`.\n- `src/perf/tprobe2.rs`: `Record.batch`, `end_batch`, `clear`,\n  per-event division in `report`. Doc comment at file head lists\n  divergences from upstream. Three new tests\n  (`end_batch_stores_batch_size`, `report_divides_batched_delta`,\n  `clear_empties_records_and_hist`).\n- `src/runtime.rs`: `run_for(duration, inner)` wraps each batch\n  of `inner` dispatches in a probe scope; site_id is the first\n  `dst_id` in the batch. Partial trailing batches (queue empties\n  mid-scope) are discarded — scope dropped, count not incremented\n  — so throughput numbers reflect whole-batch work only. New test\n  `partial_trailing_batch_is_dropped` covers the drop path.\n- `src/bin/goal1.rs`: clap-derive `Cli` with `duration_s`,\n  `--warmup`, `--inner`, `-t/--ticks`. Runs warmup, clears probe,\n  runs measurement, prints count/throughput (now also reports\n  `inner=N`), then calls `probe.report`.\n\n### Smoke measurements (release, `--warmup 0`)\n\n| Args | Throughput | Probe mean (min-p99) | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `0.5` (inner=1) | 19.7 M msg/s | 9 ns | Apparatus overhead dominates, distribution stuck in two spike buckets |\n| `0.5 --inner 1000` | 205 M msg/s | 5 ns | ~10× throughput; per-dispatch mean drops because apparatus is amortized |\n| `0.5 --inner 1000 -t` | 204 M msg/s | 18 tk | Ticks view; tail p99-max 449 tk vs ~115 k tk at inner=1 (batch smooths OS interrupts) |\n\nWarmup wall-clock sanity: `goal1 0.3 --warmup 1 --inner 1000` ran\nin 1.33s total. Argv error paths (`-1`, `--inner 0`, `--warmup nan`)\nall exit 2 with clap-formatted messages.\n\nThe bot thinks the inner=1 vs inner=1000 gap (9 ns vs 5 ns\nper-event) is ~4 ns of apparatus overhead; 0.1.0-4's overhead\ncalibration will confirm that estimate directly."

> AGENT

Stop — I need to flag a mistake I made before we commit. I skipped committing step 0.1.0-2 entirely. After "Ready for your review" on 0.1.0-2, I went straight into the band-display discussion, then inner/warmup/clap, and implemented 0.1.0-3 on top of the still-uncommitted 0.1.0-2 working copy. `jj log` confirms: `@-` is 0.1.0-1, and the working copy contains both 0.1.0-2 and 0.1.0-3 changes combined.

Three ways out:

1. **Single commit at 0.1.0-3** — commit everything as-is; chores has both `## (0.1.0-2)` and `## (0.1.0-3)` sections inside one commit labeled 0.1.0-3. Simple, but the history mentions a 0.1.0-2 that never existed as its own commit.
2. **Single commit at 0.1.0-2** — renumber Cargo.toml back to `0.1.0-2`, merge the two chores sections into one, drop the ladder step. Clean one-to-one between commit version and Cargo version, but we lose the `-2 → -3` split we planned.
3. **Split via `jj split`** — interactively split the working copy: revert 0.1.0-3 changes to files (tprobe2.rs, runtime.rs, goal1.rs, Cargo.toml, chores-01.md), commit as 0.1.0-2, then re-apply 0.1.0-3 and commit. Clean history, more work — fiddly because the split cuts through the middle of several files.

The bot thinks **1** is the pragmatic call: the two-phase conceptual split stays visible in chores (future readers see exactly what thinking happened), and no information is lost. **2** is a close runner-up if you want version = commit parity. **3** is the "right" answer for history purity but costs us time on a PoC.

Your call — which do you want?

## Checkpoint 1 — revised commit block

**Title** (58 chars): `feat: tprobe2 + inner/warmup/clap for Goal1 (0.1.0-3)`

**Unified body**:
```
Two ladder steps combined into one commit.

  0.1.0-2 was never committed on its own — the work was done,
  "ready for review" was reached, then the conversation pivoted
  to band-display / inner / warmup / clap without a commit pause,
  and 0.1.0-3 was built on top of the uncommitted 0.1.0-2 state.
  Rather than back-split the working copy, both versions' changes
  land here. The chores file keeps the per-version narrative
  intact — see `## (0.1.0-2)` and `## (0.1.0-3)` sections.

── 0.1.0-2: vendored perf probe + Goal1 instrumentation ──
- Cargo.toml: add hdrhistogram, minstant
- src/perf/{mod,tprobe2,band_table,ticks,ticks/x86_64,fmt}.rs:
  vendored from ../iiac-perf/src with actor-x1 import paths
  and // OK: justifications on unwrap* per this crate's rules
- src/lib.rs: pub mod perf
- src/runtime.rs: SingleThreadRuntime embeds TProbe2;
  per-dispatch probe scope wraps handle_message
- src/bin/goal1.rs: initial perf-aware CLI (hand-rolled parser)

> AGENT

Good catch — you're right, my "smokes" were all `--warmup 0` for speed, which tests the code path but not the tool's actual intended use. Proposed dogfood matrix using the real defaults (10s warmup):

```
goal1 2 --inner 1              # baseline — per-dispatch + apparatus
goal1 2 --inner 100            # modest amortization
goal1 2 --inner 1000           # the regime where histogram resolves
goal1 2 --inner 10000          # deep amortization, see if it saturates
goal1 2 --inner 1000 -t        # ticks view for the "good" regime
goal1 2 --inner 1000           # ×2 more runs — run-to-run variance
goal1 2 --inner 1000
```

~90s total compute. Things I'll be looking for:
- Reports readable and the distribution actually spreads across bands at `--inner ≥ 100`.
- Run-to-run throughput variance (~few %) vs. tail shape (big spikes from OS interrupts?).
- Whether the 10s warmup actually matters — can compare against our earlier `--warmup 0` numbers and see if mean shifts / tail cleans up.
- Defaults feel right: is 10s warmup too long for iterative use? Is inner=1 a confusing default now that we know inner=1000 gives better signal?

Is that the shape of dogfooding you have in mind, or do you want a different workload / more targeted check?

> AGENT

Expected artifact, not a regression — you're hitting two effects at `inner=10`:

1. **Integer division at `end_batch`**: we store `(delta + n/2) / n`. At `inner=10` and a raw scope of ~185 ticks, any variation smaller than ~5 ticks in the raw delta collapses to the same stored value. Effective resolution per stored sample is ~5 ticks (~1.4 ns). Raising `inner` helps linearly: `inner=100` → ~0.5 tick resolution, `inner=1000` → ~0.05 tick.

2. **Bucket-to-band assignment by mid-rank**: the renderer walks hdrhistogram *buckets* (not samples) and puts each bucket into exactly one percentile band based on where its cumulative mid-rank falls. A tight distribution has most samples in 3–5 buckets, so only 3–5 bands ever get populated — the rest stay at count=0 and are currently suppressed. Even `inner=1000` only lit 5 of 11 bands in our smokes.

**The "always show all bands" question from earlier is back, and it's really the right fix here.** Two flavors:

- **Cheap** (my earlier option B): just drop the `if band_count[i] == 0 { continue; }` guard and emit `—` / `0` rows for empty bands. Keeps the bucket-based algorithm, but makes the shape of the distribution visually obvious — you see "p20-p30 got everything, the rest are empty" at a glance.
- **Principled**: switch the renderer to compute percentile values directly via `hist.value_at_quantile(q)`. Each band then reports the *value* at its boundary percentiles plus count/mean between them, regardless of which buckets contributed. No more "band exists only if a bucket landed in it." Bigger change to `band_table.rs`.

The bot thinks **cheap** is the right next move — small, honest about what's there vs missing, and doesn't require rethinking band semantics. **Principled** would be a follow-up if we still find the display misleading after cheap lands.

Want me to fold the cheap fix into `0.1.0-4` alongside overhead calibration, or do it as its own `0.1.0-4` and push overhead to `-5`?

> DEVELOPER

I vote always see everything if not data show the 0's

> AGENT

Got it — always show all 12 bands (including `min-p1`), empty bands render as all-zeros (`0 ns / 0 ns / 0 ns / 0 / 0 ns`) so the display stays unambiguous and aligned.

**Recommended scope**: its own step, `0.1.0-4 = always-show-all-bands`, push overhead calibration to `0.1.0-5`, Goal2 to `0.1.0-6`. Small focused commit; the change is ~5 lines of code in `src/perf/band_table.rs` (drop the skip-on-empty guard, emit zero placeholders) plus a chores section.

Ladder now: `-4 → -5 → -6 → 0.1.0`. Seven commits total for Stage 1 — longer than originally planned but every step has been small and focused, which is the point of `-N`.

Start `0.1.0-4`?

> AGENT

I'm not sure which "combine" you mean — let me ask rather than guess, since the two readings lead to very different changes:

**Reading 1** — "don't combine empty bands into one row": always emit 12 separate rows, never collapse adjacent empty bands into a single "p20–p50: no data" summary. Each empty band gets its own zero row. *(This is what I already proposed — 5 lines in band_table.rs.)*

**Reading 2** — "don't combine buckets into one band": today the renderer walks hdrhistogram *buckets* and lumps each bucket's samples into one band by mid-rank, which is why we see `p50-p60: 8,772,297` (83 % of samples) while neighboring bands show zero — a single hot bucket at "10 ns" gets attributed to one band, not spread across the percentile range those samples actually span. Fixing this means switching to per-percentile value lookups (`hist.value_at_quantile(q)` at each boundary) so each band reports the samples in *its* percentile range honestly. *(Bigger change — rewrites the band-filling loop in band_table.rs.)*

The bot thinks you mean **Reading 2** given the context (you've been flagging the odd-looking distribution repeatedly), but **Reading 1** is the literal parsing of "don't combine [rows]." Which one?

- **R2**: rewrite band-filling to use `hist.value_at_quantile(q)` so samples in one hot bucket are distributed across the percentile bands they actually span (~30–50 lines, rewrites the filling loop)

The existing\n`goal1: …` line below carries the binary identity, so the banner\ndoesn't repeat \"goal1\". Implemented in two places: a `println!`\nat the top of `main` for normal runs, and clap's `before_help`\nattribute for help output (`concat!(env!(CARGO_PKG_NAME), \" \",\nenv!(CARGO_PKG_VERSION))` — compile-time concat of package env\nvars)."
}

1	//! Apparatus-overhead calibration. Fits a two-point line through an
2	//! empty bench at two `inner` sizes (`N_LOW`, `N_HIGH`) to separate
3	//! per-sample timer-pair framing from per-iteration loop cost.
4	
5	use std::hint::black_box;
6	use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
7	
8	use crate::harness::Bench;
9	
10	/// Calibration warmup iterations — long enough for CPU frequency
11	/// boost to ramp before the first `min_low` measurement.
12	pub const CAL_WARMUP: u64 = 100_000;
13	
14	/// Samples per `measure()` call; the reported minimum approaches
15	/// the hardware floor as samples grow.
16	pub const CAL_SAMPLES: u64 = 100_000;
17	
18	/// Inner-loop count for the low-N calibration point.
19	pub const N_LOW: u64 = 100;
20	
21	/// Inner-loop count for the high-N calibration point. A wide spread
22	/// (`N_HIGH / (N_HIGH - N_LOW) ≈ 1.01`) keeps noise amplification on
23	/// the fitted framing small — the 0.6.0 fix for framing instability.
24	pub const N_HIGH: u64 = 10_000;
25	
26	/// Apparatus-overhead model fitted by [`calibrate`]. Bench code uses
27	/// [`Overhead::per_call_ns`] to subtract per-call overhead from
28	/// reported latencies.
29	#[derive(Debug)]
30	pub struct Overhead {
31	    /// Fixed per-sample timer-pair overhead
32	    /// (`Instant::now()` before and after a batch), in nanoseconds.
33	    pub framing_per_sample_ns: f64,
34	    /// Per-inner-iteration loop overhead (branch + `black_box`),
35	    /// in nanoseconds.
36	    pub loop_per_iter_ns: f64,
37	    /// Raw `min_low` measurement (ns). Preserved for `-v` logging.
38	    pub cal_min_low_ns: u64,
39	    /// Raw `min_high` measurement (ns). Preserved for `-v` logging.
40	    pub cal_min_high_ns: u64,
41	    /// Wall-clock duration of the full calibration run.
42	    pub cal_duration: Duration,
43	}
44	
45	impl Overhead {
46	    /// Per-call apparatus overhead at a given `inner`, in nanoseconds:
47	    /// `framing_per_sample / inner + loop_per_iter`.
48	    pub fn per_call_ns(&self, inner: u64) -> f64 {
49	        self.framing_per_sample_ns / inner as f64 + self.loop_per_iter_ns
50	    }
51	}
52	
53	struct EmptyBench;
54	
55	impl Bench for EmptyBench {
56	    fn name(&self) -> &str {
57	        "empty"
58	    }
59	
60	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {
61	        black_box(1)
62	    }
63	}
64	
65	/// Run the two-point calibration and return the fitted [`Overhead`].
66	/// Blocks for ~500 ms on a typical modern x86.
67	pub fn calibrate() -> Overhead {
68	    let mut bench = EmptyBench;
69	    let cal_start = Instant::now();
70	    for _ in 0..CAL_WARMUP {
71	        black_box(bench.step());
72	    }
73	
74	    let min_low = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_LOW);
75	    let min_high = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_HIGH);
76	
77	    // Two-point fit: per_sample = framing + inner * loop_per_iter.
78	    // Slope between (N_LOW, min_low) and (N_HIGH, min_high) gives loop_per_iter,
79	    // intercept gives framing. Cancels TSC pipelining of the framing pair.
80	    //
81	    // Noise amplification: d(framing)/d(min_low) = N_HIGH / (N_HIGH - N_LOW).
82	    // At 100 / 10_000 that's ~1.01, so ~10 ns of slop on min_low produces
83	    // only ~10 ns on framing. Previously 100 / 1_000 gave ~1.11, which was
84	    // the cause of the "exact 2×" framing anomaly pre-0.6.0.
85	    let loop_per_iter_ns = if min_high > min_low {
86	        (min_high - min_low) as f64 / (N_HIGH - N_LOW) as f64
87	    } else {
88	        0.0
89	    };
90	    let framing_per_sample_ns = (min_low as f64 - N_LOW as f64 * loop_per_iter_ns).max(0.0);
91	
92	    Overhead {
93	        framing_per_sample_ns,
94	        loop_per_iter_ns,
95	        cal_min_low_ns: min_low,
96	        cal_min_high_ns: min_high,
97	        cal_duration: cal_start.elapsed(),
98	    }
99	}
100	
101	fn measure(bench: &mut EmptyBench, samples: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {
102	    let mut min_ns = u64::MAX;
103	    for _ in 0..samples {
104	        let start = minstant::Instant::now();
105	        for _ in 0..inner {
106	            black_box(bench.step());
107	        }
108	        let e = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
109	        if e < min_ns {
110	            min_ns = e;
111	        }
112	    }
113	    min_ns
114	}
115	
```

> AGENT

Simpler model than iiac-perf's two-point fit — direct measurement of back-to-back `rdtsc` cost gives us the framing floor in one pass. That's the bias on every stored scope delta since TProbe2's recorded delta is exactly `end_tsc - start_tsc` (the vec push happens after `end_tsc` is captured, so it doesn't contribute). No loop_per_iter model yet — user's "loop overhead" is outside our probe scope by design.

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/overhead.rs",
  "content": "//! Apparatus-overhead calibration for [`TProbe2`]. Measures the\n//! hardware floor cost of two back-to-back `rdtsc` reads — the\n//! bias contaminating every probe scope's stored `end_tsc -\n//! start_tsc` delta — and exposes it as a per-batch correction\n//! for [`TProbe2::report`].\n//!\n//! This is a simpler model than `iiac-perf/src/overhead.rs`,\n//! which vendored as inspiration but does a two-point fit to\n//! separate per-sample framing from per-inner-iteration loop cost.\n//! Here we only model per-scope framing because:\n//! - [`TProbe2::end`] captures `end_tsc` *before* `Vec::push`, so\n//!   the stored delta contains nothing but the two `rdtsc` reads\n//!   and whatever work the caller enclosed. No hidden loop cost\n//!   inside the scope apparatus.\n//! - Any per-iteration loop overhead in the caller's hot path\n//!   (queue pop, deadline check, etc.) is part of what the user\n//!   is measuring; subtracting it would be wrong.\n//!\n//! The bot thinks a future two-point extension would only be\n//! warranted if we start measuring apparatus that has its own\n//! inner loop (e.g. a timer that averages internally).\n//!\n//! [`TProbe2`]: crate::perf::TProbe2\n//! [`TProbe2::report`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::report\n//! [`TProbe2::end`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::end\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::time::{Duration, Instant};\n\nuse crate::perf::ticks;\n\n/// Calibration warmup iterations — lets CPU frequency boost ramp\n/// before the first real sample.\npub const CAL_WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;\n\n/// Samples taken in the minimum-pass. The reported floor approaches\n/// the hardware lower bound as samples grow; 100k takes ~1 ms on a\n/// modern x86 and is plenty.\npub const CAL_SAMPLES: u64 = 100_000;\n\n/// Apparatus-overhead model fitted by [`calibrate`]. Callers pass a\n/// reference to [`TProbe2::report`] (as `Option<&Overhead>`) to get\n/// framing subtracted from each stored per-event value.\n///\n/// [`TProbe2::report`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::report\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]\npub struct Overhead {\n    /// Minimum observed `end_tsc - start_tsc` for a pair of\n    /// back-to-back `rdtsc` reads over [`CAL_SAMPLES`] samples,\n    /// in raw ticks. This is the framing cost per probe scope,\n    /// regardless of scope `batch`.\n    pub framing_ticks: u64,\n    /// Wall-clock duration of the full calibration run.\n    pub cal_duration: Duration,\n}\n\nimpl Overhead {\n    /// Per-event framing correction at a given `batch`, in ticks:\n    /// the framing is paid once per scope and amortized across\n    /// `batch` events. Integer division truncates toward zero, so\n    /// at `batch > framing_ticks` the correction rounds to zero.\n    pub fn per_event_ticks(&self, batch: u64) -> u64 {\n        self.framing_ticks / batch.max(1)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Measure [`CAL_SAMPLES`] back-to-back `rdtsc` pairs, return the\n/// minimum delta. Preceded by [`CAL_WARMUP`] throwaway reads to\n/// warm CPU frequency / branch predictor. Blocks for ~1 ms on a\n/// modern x86.\npub fn calibrate() -> Overhead {\n    let cal_start = Instant::now();\n\n    for _ in 0..CAL_WARMUP {\n        let a = ticks::read_ticks();\n        let b = ticks::read_ticks();\n        black_box(b.wrapping_sub(a));\n    }\n\n    let mut min_ticks = u64::MAX;\n    for _ in 0..CAL_SAMPLES {\n        let start = ticks::read_ticks();\n        let end = ticks::read_ticks();\n        let delta = end.saturating_sub(start);\n        if delta < min_ticks {\n            min_ticks = delta;\n        }\n    }\n\n    Overhead {\n        framing_ticks: min_ticks,\n        cal_duration: cal_start.elapsed(),\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn per_event_ticks_amortizes_framing() {\n        let ovh = Overhead {\n            framing_ticks: 20,\n            cal_duration: Duration::from_millis(1),\n        };\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(1), 20);\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(10), 2);\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(100), 0); // truncates\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(0), 20); // batch=0 treated as 1\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn calibrate_returns_positive_ticks_quickly() {\n        let ovh = calibrate();\n        // Any modern x86 reads rdtsc in < 100 ticks; allow slack.\n        assert!(ovh.framing_ticks > 0);\n        assert!(ovh.framing_ticks < 10_000);\n        // Calibration should complete well under a second.\n        assert!(ovh.cal_duration < Duration::from_secs(1));\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/tprobe2.rs",
  "old_string": "    /// Render a band-table report for this probe. `as_ticks`\n    /// controls the display unit: `false` converts stored tick\n    /// deltas to nanoseconds (default for the CLI); `true` shows\n    /// raw ticks (`-t`/`--ticks`).\n    ///\n    /// Drains any pending `start` / `end*` records into the\n    /// histogram before rendering: `delta = (end_tsc − start_tsc)\n    /// / batch`, clamped to `1` since the histogram lower bound\n    /// is 1.\n    pub fn report(&mut self, as_ticks: bool) {\n        for r in self.records.drain(..) {\n            let delta = r.end_tsc.saturating_sub(r.start_tsc);\n            let batch = r.batch.max(1);\n            let per_event = (delta + batch / 2) / batch;\n            self.hist.record(per_event.max(1)).unwrap(); // OK: histogram bounds are [1, 1e12]; per_event.max(1) ≥ 1, and per-event tick deltas stay well under 1e12\n        }\n        band_table::render(\"tprobe2\", &self.name, &self.hist, as_ticks);\n    }",
  "new_string": "    /// Render a band-table report for this probe. `as_ticks`\n    /// controls the display unit: `false` converts stored tick\n    /// deltas to nanoseconds (default for the CLI); `true` shows\n    /// raw ticks (`-t`/`--ticks`). `overhead` (if `Some`)\n    /// subtracts per-event framing correction from each drained\n    /// record.\n    ///\n    /// Drains any pending `start` / `end*` records into the\n    /// histogram before rendering:\n    /// `per_event = (end_tsc − start_tsc) / batch - overhead_correction`,\n    /// clamped to `1` since the histogram lower bound is 1.\n    pub fn report(&mut self, as_ticks: bool, overhead: Option<&Overhead>) {\n        for r in self.records.drain(..) {\n            let delta = r.end_tsc.saturating_sub(r.start_tsc);\n            let batch = r.batch.max(1);\n            let per_event_raw = (delta + batch / 2) / batch;\n            let per_event = if let Some(ovh) = overhead {\n                per_event_raw.saturating_sub(ovh.per_event_ticks(batch))\n            } else {\n                per_event_raw\n            };\n            self.hist.record(per_event.max(1)).unwrap(); // OK: histogram bounds are [1, 1e12]; per_event.max(1) ≥ 1, and per-event tick deltas stay well under 1e12\n        }\n        band_table::render(\"tprobe2\", &self.name, &self.hist, as_ticks);\n    }"
}

  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn report_divides_batched_delta() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        // Inject a synthetic record: 1000 tick delta, batch 10.\n        p.records.push(Record {\n            site_id: 0,\n            start_tsc: 0,\n            end_tsc: 1000,\n            batch: 10,\n        });\n        p.report(true);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 1);\n        let v = p.hist.iter_recorded().next().unwrap().value_iterated_to();\n        // (1000 + 5) / 10 = 100.\n        assert_eq!(v, 100);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn report_drains_records_into_histogram() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id1 = p.start(1);\n        p.end(id1);\n        let id2 = p.start(2);\n        p.end(id2);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 2);\n\n        p.report(false);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n\n        // Idempotent: a second report drains nothing, hist unchanged.\n        p.report(false);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn clear_empties_records_and_hist() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(1);\n        p.end(id);\n        p.report(false); // moves record into hist\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 1);\n        p.clear();\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);\n    }",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn report_divides_batched_delta() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        // Inject a synthetic record: 1000 tick delta, batch 10.\n        p.records.push(Record {\n            site_id: 0,\n            start_tsc: 0,\n            end_tsc: 1000,\n            batch: 10,\n        });\n        p.report(true, None);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 1);\n        let v = p.hist.iter_recorded().next().unwrap().value_iterated_to();\n        // (1000 + 5) / 10 = 100.\n        assert_eq!(v, 100);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn report_subtracts_overhead() {\n        use std::time::Duration;\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        p.records.push(Record {\n            site_id: 0,\n            start_tsc: 0,\n            end_tsc: 1000,\n            batch: 10,\n        });\n        let ovh = Overhead {\n            framing_ticks: 100,\n            cal_duration: Duration::from_millis(0),\n        };\n        p.report(true, Some(&ovh));\n        let v = p.hist.iter_recorded().next().unwrap().value_iterated_to();\n        // per_event_raw = (1000 + 5) / 10 = 100. Correction = 100/10 = 10.\n        // Adjusted = 100 - 10 = 90.\n        assert_eq!(v, 90);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn report_drains_records_into_histogram() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id1 = p.start(1);\n        p.end(id1);\n        let id2 = p.start(2);\n        p.end(id2);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 2);\n\n        p.report(false, None);\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n\n        // Idempotent: a second report drains nothing, hist unchanged.\n        p.report(false, None);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 2);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn clear_empties_records_and_hist() {\n        let mut p = TProbe2::new(\"t\");\n        let id = p.start(1);\n        p.end(id);\n        p.report(false, None); // moves record into hist\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 1);\n        p.clear();\n        assert_eq!(p.records.len(), 0);\n        assert_eq!(p.hist.len(), 0);\n    }"
}

  "old_string": "fn main() {\n    let cli = Cli::parse();\n\n    println!(\"{} {}\", env!(\"CARGO_PKG_NAME\"), env!(\"CARGO_PKG_VERSION\"),);\n\n    let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"goal1.dispatch\");\n    let a_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 }));\n    let b_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 }));\n    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));\n    rt.seed(a_id, Message);\n\n    // Warmup: same loop, probe active, records discarded at the boundary.\n    rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.warmup), cli.inner);\n    rt.probe_mut().clear();\n\n    // Measurement.\n    let count = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.duration_s), cli.inner);\n    let mmps = count as f64 / cli.duration_s / 1e6;\n    println!(\n        \"goal1: {count} messages in {:.3}s ({mmps:.3} M msg/s, inner={})\",\n        cli.duration_s, cli.inner,\n    );\n    rt.probe_mut().report(cli.ticks);\n}",
  "new_string": "fn main() {\n    let cli = Cli::parse();\n\n    println!(\"{} {}\", env!(\"CARGO_PKG_NAME\"), env!(\"CARGO_PKG_VERSION\"),);\n\n    let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"goal1.dispatch\");\n    let a_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 }));\n    let b_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 }));\n    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));\n    rt.seed(a_id, Message);\n\n    // Warmup: same loop, probe active, records discarded at the boundary.\n    rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.warmup), cli.inner);\n    rt.probe_mut().clear();\n\n    // Calibration (skipped with --raw). Runs on the warmed system,\n    // before measurement begins, so freq/cache state matches what\n    // the probe will see during measurement.\n    let overhead = if cli.raw {\n        None\n    } else {\n        Some(perf::calibrate())\n    };\n\n    // Measurement.\n    let count = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.duration_s), cli.inner);\n    let mmps = count as f64 / cli.duration_s / 1e6;\n    println!(\n        \"goal1: {count} messages in {:.3}s ({mmps:.3} M msg/s, inner={})\",\n        cli.duration_s, cli.inner,\n    );\n    if let Some(ovh) = &overhead {\n        let framing_ns = ovh.framing_ticks as f64 / ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n        let per_event_tk = ovh.per_event_ticks(cli.inner);\n        let per_event_ns = per_event_tk as f64 / ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n        println!(\n            \"  apparatus: framing={} tk ({:.2} ns); per-event at inner={} = {} tk ({:.2} ns)\",\n            ovh.framing_ticks, framing_ns, cli.inner, per_event_tk, per_event_ns,\n        );\n    } else {\n        println!(\"  apparatus: raw (no overhead subtraction)\");\n    }\n    rt.probe_mut().report(cli.ticks, overhead.as_ref());\n}"
}

> AGENT

Wait — `framing=1 tk` is wrong. Back-to-back `rdtsc` without a fence gets reordered by OoO, so empty-scope delta collapses to near zero. The actual contamination in real-work scopes is ~15 ticks (~4 ns, matching the inner=1 vs inner=1000 gap).

Need `lfence`-serialized reads in calibration to measure the real floor.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/overhead.rs",
  "old_string": "//! Apparatus-overhead calibration for [`TProbe2`]. Measures the\n//! hardware floor cost of two back-to-back `rdtsc` reads — the\n//! bias contaminating every probe scope's stored `end_tsc -\n//! start_tsc` delta — and exposes it as a per-batch correction\n//! for [`TProbe2::report`].\n//!\n//! This is a simpler model than `iiac-perf/src/overhead.rs`,\n//! which vendored as inspiration but does a two-point fit to\n//! separate per-sample framing from per-inner-iteration loop cost.\n//! Here we only model per-scope framing because:\n//! - [`TProbe2::end`] captures `end_tsc` *before* `Vec::push`, so\n//!   the stored delta contains nothing but the two `rdtsc` reads\n//!   and whatever work the caller enclosed. No hidden loop cost\n//!   inside the scope apparatus.\n//! - Any per-iteration loop overhead in the caller's hot path\n//!   (queue pop, deadline check, etc.) is part of what the user\n//!   is measuring; subtracting it would be wrong.\n//!\n//! The bot thinks a future two-point extension would only be\n//! warranted if we start measuring apparatus that has its own\n//! inner loop (e.g. a timer that averages internally).\n//!\n//! [`TProbe2`]: crate::perf::TProbe2\n//! [`TProbe2::report`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::report\n//! [`TProbe2::end`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::end\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::time::{Duration, Instant};\n\nuse crate::perf::ticks;",
  "new_string": "//! Apparatus-overhead calibration for [`TProbe2`]. Measures the\n//! hardware floor cost of two back-to-back `rdtsc` reads — the\n//! bias contaminating every probe scope's stored `end_tsc -\n//! start_tsc` delta — and exposes it as a per-batch correction\n//! for [`TProbe2::report`].\n//!\n//! This is a simpler model than `iiac-perf/src/overhead.rs`,\n//! which vendored as inspiration but does a two-point fit to\n//! separate per-sample framing from per-inner-iteration loop cost.\n//! Here we only model per-scope framing because:\n//! - [`TProbe2::end`] captures `end_tsc` *before* `Vec::push`, so\n//!   the stored delta contains nothing but the two `rdtsc` reads\n//!   and whatever work the caller enclosed. No hidden loop cost\n//!   inside the scope apparatus.\n//! - Any per-iteration loop overhead in the caller's hot path\n//!   (queue pop, deadline check, etc.) is part of what the user\n//!   is measuring; subtracting it would be wrong.\n//!\n//! ### `lfence` bracketing\n//!\n//! `rdtsc` isn't a serializing instruction, so two back-to-back\n//! reads with no work between them can execute out-of-order and\n//! measure a delta of 0–1 ticks — far below the true apparatus\n//! cost. In the runtime hot path the enclosed work acts as an\n//! implicit pipeline barrier, so the framing actually contaminating\n//! each stored delta is the fully-serialized cost, not the\n//! optimistic OoO floor. Calibration uses `_mm_lfence` before and\n//! after each read to force the serialized reading so the measured\n//! floor matches what real scopes experience. **The bot thinks**\n//! this is close enough to the truth for Stage 1; a two-point fit\n//! on real workload would be strictly more correct and may land\n//! later if single-point calibration proves unstable.\n//!\n//! The bot thinks a future two-point extension would be worth it\n//! if we ever start measuring apparatus that has its own inner\n//! loop (e.g. a timer that averages internally).\n//!\n//! [`TProbe2`]: crate::perf::TProbe2\n//! [`TProbe2::report`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::report\n//! [`TProbe2::end`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::end\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::time::{Duration, Instant};"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/overhead.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Measure [`CAL_SAMPLES`] back-to-back `rdtsc` pairs, return the\n/// minimum delta. Preceded by [`CAL_WARMUP`] throwaway reads to\n/// warm CPU frequency / branch predictor. Blocks for ~1 ms on a\n/// modern x86.\npub fn calibrate() -> Overhead {\n    let cal_start = Instant::now();\n\n    for _ in 0..CAL_WARMUP {\n        let a = ticks::read_ticks();\n        let b = ticks::read_ticks();\n        black_box(b.wrapping_sub(a));\n    }\n\n    let mut min_ticks = u64::MAX;\n    for _ in 0..CAL_SAMPLES {\n        let start = ticks::read_ticks();\n        let end = ticks::read_ticks();\n        let delta = end.saturating_sub(start);\n        if delta < min_ticks {\n            min_ticks = delta;\n        }\n    }\n\n    Overhead {\n        framing_ticks: min_ticks,\n        cal_duration: cal_start.elapsed(),\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Fenced tick read: `lfence; rdtsc; lfence`. Forces the pipeline\n/// to serialize on either side so the reading reflects the true\n/// framing cost a real probe scope sees (where enclosed work also\n/// serializes the pipeline), not the OoO-reduced floor.\n#[cfg(target_arch = \"x86_64\")]\n#[inline(always)]\nfn read_ticks_fenced() -> u64 {\n    // Safe: `_mm_lfence` and `_rdtsc` are valid on any x86_64 CPU\n    // (LFENCE requires SSE2, universal on amd64).\n    unsafe {\n        core::arch::x86_64::_mm_lfence();\n        let t = core::arch::x86_64::_rdtsc();\n        core::arch::x86_64::_mm_lfence();\n        t\n    }\n}\n\n/// Measure [`CAL_SAMPLES`] back-to-back fenced `rdtsc` pairs,\n/// return the minimum delta. Preceded by [`CAL_WARMUP`] throwaway\n/// reads to warm CPU frequency / branch predictor. Blocks for\n/// ~5 ms on a modern x86 (fences are expensive).\npub fn calibrate() -> Overhead {\n    let cal_start = Instant::now();\n\n    for _ in 0..CAL_WARMUP {\n        let a = read_ticks_fenced();\n        let b = read_ticks_fenced();\n        black_box(b.wrapping_sub(a));\n    }\n\n    let mut min_ticks = u64::MAX;\n    for _ in 0..CAL_SAMPLES {\n        let start = read_ticks_fenced();\n        let end = read_ticks_fenced();\n        let delta = end.saturating_sub(start);\n        if delta < min_ticks {\n            min_ticks = delta;\n        }\n    }\n\n    Overhead {\n        framing_ticks: min_ticks,\n        cal_duration: cal_start.elapsed(),\n    }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "//! Apparatus-overhead calibration for [`TProbe2`]. Measures the\n//! hardware floor cost of two back-to-back `rdtsc` reads — the\n//! bias contaminating every probe scope's stored `end_tsc -\n//! start_tsc` delta — and exposes it as a per-batch correction\n//! for [`TProbe2::report`].\n//!\n//! This is a simpler model than `iiac-perf/src/overhead.rs`,\n//! which vendored as inspiration but does a two-point fit to\n//! separate per-sample framing from per-inner-iteration loop cost.\n//! Here we only model per-scope framing because:\n//! - [`TProbe2::end`] captures `end_tsc` *before* `Vec::push`, so\n//!   the stored delta contains nothing but the two `rdtsc` reads\n//!   and whatever work the caller enclosed. No hidden loop cost\n//!   inside the scope apparatus.\n//! - Any per-iteration loop overhead in the caller's hot path\n//!   (queue pop, deadline check, etc.) is part of what the user\n//!   is measuring; subtracting it would be wrong.\n//!\n//! ### `lfence` bracketing\n//!\n//! `rdtsc` isn't a serializing instruction, so two back-to-back\n//! reads with no work between them can execute out-of-order and\n//! measure a delta of 0–1 ticks — far below the true apparatus\n//! cost. In the runtime hot path the enclosed work acts as an\n//! implicit pipeline barrier, so the framing actually contaminating\n//! each stored delta is the fully-serialized cost, not the\n//! optimistic OoO floor. Calibration uses `_mm_lfence` before and\n//! after each read to force the serialized reading so the measured\n//! floor matches what real scopes experience. **The bot thinks**\n//! this is close enough to the truth for Stage 1; a two-point fit\n//! on real workload would be strictly more correct and may land\n//! later if single-point calibration proves unstable.\n//!\n//! The bot thinks a future two-point extension would be worth it\n//! if we ever start measuring apparatus that has its own inner\n//! loop (e.g. a timer that averages internally).\n//!\n//! [`TProbe2`]: crate::perf::TProbe2\n//! [`TProbe2::report`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::report\n//! [`TProbe2::end`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::end\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::time::{Duration, Instant};",
  "new_string": "//! Apparatus-overhead calibration for [`TProbe2`]. Measures the\n//! hardware floor cost of two back-to-back `rdtsc` reads — the\n//! bias contaminating every probe scope's stored `end_tsc -\n//! start_tsc` delta — via a two-point fit matching the approach\n//! in `iiac-perf/src/overhead.rs`.\n//!\n//! ### Why two-point, not single-point\n//!\n//! `rdtsc` isn't a serializing instruction. Two back-to-back\n//! reads with nothing between can execute out-of-order and\n//! measure a delta of 0–1 ticks — far below the true framing\n//! cost. Bracketing with `_mm_lfence` overcorrects (lfence drains\n//! the pipeline, which real work doesn't fully do), so single-\n//! point measurement is biased in both directions.\n//!\n//! Two-point fits a line through empty benches at two `inner`\n//! sizes (`N_LOW`, `N_HIGH`):\n//! - raw delta at inner=N = framing + N · loop_per_iter\n//! - slope between (N_LOW, raw_low) and (N_HIGH, raw_high) gives\n//!   loop_per_iter; intercept gives framing.\n//!\n//! The `black_box(1)` inside the inner loop keeps OoO from\n//! collapsing iterations and gives `loop_per_iter` a consistent\n//! value. Noise amplification on framing:\n//! `d(framing)/d(raw_low) = N_HIGH / (N_HIGH − N_LOW)`. At\n//! 100/10_000 that's ~1.01 — any slop on `raw_low` propagates\n//! roughly 1:1 to framing.\n//!\n//! We fit using raw tick deltas (not per-event values) in `f64`\n//! arithmetic so integer truncation can't hide small framing\n//! components.\n//!\n//! ### What gets subtracted\n//!\n//! Only framing. The two-point fit also yields\n//! `loop_per_iter_ticks` (cost of one black_box iteration), which\n//! we keep on the [`Overhead`] struct for diagnostics but do not\n//! subtract from probe samples — the caller's inner-loop cost\n//! (queue pop, deadline check, etc.) is part of what the user is\n//! actually measuring, and the `black_box(1)` loop_per_iter\n//! doesn't model it anyway.\n//!\n//! [`TProbe2`]: crate::perf::TProbe2\n//! [`TProbe2::report`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::report\n\nuse std::hint::black_box;\nuse std::time::{Duration, Instant};\n\nuse crate::perf::ticks;"
}

  "old_string": "/// Calibration warmup iterations — lets CPU frequency boost ramp\n/// before the first real sample.\npub const CAL_WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;\n\n/// Samples taken in the minimum-pass. The reported floor approaches\n/// the hardware lower bound as samples grow; 100k takes ~1 ms on a\n/// modern x86 and is plenty.\npub const CAL_SAMPLES: u64 = 100_000;\n\n/// Apparatus-overhead model fitted by [`calibrate`]. Callers pass a\n/// reference to [`TProbe2::report`] (as `Option<&Overhead>`) to get\n/// framing subtracted from each stored per-event value.\n///\n/// [`TProbe2::report`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::report\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]\npub struct Overhead {\n    /// Minimum observed `end_tsc - start_tsc` for a pair of\n    /// back-to-back `rdtsc` reads over [`CAL_SAMPLES`] samples,\n    /// in raw ticks. This is the framing cost per probe scope,\n    /// regardless of scope `batch`.\n    pub framing_ticks: u64,\n    /// Wall-clock duration of the full calibration run.\n    pub cal_duration: Duration,\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Calibration warmup iterations — lets CPU frequency boost ramp\n/// before the first real sample.\npub const CAL_WARMUP: u64 = 10_000;\n\n/// Samples per minimum-pass. The reported floor approaches the\n/// hardware lower bound as samples grow; 1,000 is enough for\n/// convergence and keeps total calibration wall-clock short.\npub const CAL_SAMPLES: u64 = 1_000;\n\n/// Inner-loop count for the low-N calibration point.\npub const N_LOW: u64 = 100;\n\n/// Inner-loop count for the high-N calibration point. Wide spread\n/// keeps noise amplification on fitted framing small\n/// (`N_HIGH / (N_HIGH − N_LOW) ≈ 1.01`).\npub const N_HIGH: u64 = 10_000;\n\n/// Apparatus-overhead model fitted by [`calibrate`]. Callers pass\n/// a reference to [`TProbe2::report`] (as `Option<&Overhead>`) to\n/// get framing subtracted from each stored per-event value.\n///\n/// [`TProbe2::report`]: crate::perf::TProbe2::report\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]\npub struct Overhead {\n    /// Fitted per-scope framing cost — the bias on every stored\n    /// `end_tsc − start_tsc` delta regardless of scope batch size,\n    /// in raw ticks. This is what gets subtracted via\n    /// [`Self::per_event_ticks`].\n    pub framing_ticks: u64,\n    /// Fitted per-iteration cost of the `black_box(1)` empty\n    /// bench loop body, in ticks. Kept for diagnostic visibility\n    /// only — not used in the subtraction path (the real dispatch\n    /// loop's per-iter cost is what we want to measure, not subtract).\n    pub loop_per_iter_ticks: f64,\n    /// Raw tick delta at `inner = N_LOW` (for `-v` style logging).\n    pub cal_raw_low_ticks: u64,\n    /// Raw tick delta at `inner = N_HIGH` (for `-v` style logging).\n    pub cal_raw_high_ticks: u64,\n    /// Wall-clock duration of the full calibration run.\n    pub cal_duration: Duration,\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/overhead.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Fenced tick read: `lfence; rdtsc; lfence`. Forces the pipeline\n/// to serialize on either side so the reading reflects the true\n/// framing cost a real probe scope sees (where enclosed work also\n/// serializes the pipeline), not the OoO-reduced floor.\n#[cfg(target_arch = \"x86_64\")]\n#[inline(always)]\nfn read_ticks_fenced() -> u64 {\n    // Safe: `_mm_lfence` and `_rdtsc` are valid on any x86_64 CPU\n    // (LFENCE requires SSE2, universal on amd64).\n    unsafe {\n        core::arch::x86_64::_mm_lfence();\n        let t = core::arch::x86_64::_rdtsc();\n        core::arch::x86_64::_mm_lfence();\n        t\n    }\n}\n\n/// Measure [`CAL_SAMPLES`] back-to-back fenced `rdtsc` pairs,\n/// return the minimum delta. Preceded by [`CAL_WARMUP`] throwaway\n/// reads to warm CPU frequency / branch predictor. Blocks for\n/// ~5 ms on a modern x86 (fences are expensive).\npub fn calibrate() -> Overhead {\n    let cal_start = Instant::now();\n\n    for _ in 0..CAL_WARMUP {\n        let a = read_ticks_fenced();\n        let b = read_ticks_fenced();\n        black_box(b.wrapping_sub(a));\n    }\n\n    let mut min_ticks = u64::MAX;\n    for _ in 0..CAL_SAMPLES {\n        let start = read_ticks_fenced();\n        let end = read_ticks_fenced();\n        let delta = end.saturating_sub(start);\n        if delta < min_ticks {\n            min_ticks = delta;\n        }\n    }\n\n    Overhead {\n        framing_ticks: min_ticks,\n        cal_duration: cal_start.elapsed(),\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Measure the minimum raw tick delta over `samples` iterations\n/// of an empty bench at inner size `inner`. Each iteration reads\n/// `rdtsc`, runs `inner × black_box(1)` as filler (keeps OoO from\n/// collapsing the scope to zero), reads `rdtsc` again.\n#[inline(never)]\nfn measure_empty_raw(samples: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {\n    let mut min_ticks = u64::MAX;\n    for _ in 0..samples {\n        let start = ticks::read_ticks();\n        for _ in 0..inner {\n            black_box(1u64);\n        }\n        let end = ticks::read_ticks();\n        let delta = end.saturating_sub(start);\n        if delta < min_ticks {\n            min_ticks = delta;\n        }\n    }\n    min_ticks\n}\n\n/// Two-point calibration. Runs [`CAL_WARMUP`] warmup iterations,\n/// then two minimum-passes at `inner = N_LOW` and `inner = N_HIGH`,\n/// and fits\n/// `raw_delta = framing + inner · loop_per_iter`. Blocks for\n/// ~10 ms on a modern x86.\npub fn calibrate() -> Overhead {\n    let cal_start = Instant::now();\n\n    for _ in 0..CAL_WARMUP {\n        let a = ticks::read_ticks();\n        black_box(a);\n    }\n\n    let raw_low = measure_empty_raw(CAL_SAMPLES, N_LOW);\n    let raw_high = measure_empty_raw(CAL_SAMPLES, N_HIGH);\n\n    let loop_per_iter_ticks = if raw_high > raw_low {\n        (raw_high - raw_low) as f64 / (N_HIGH - N_LOW) as f64\n    } else {\n        0.0\n    };\n    let framing_f = (raw_low as f64 - N_LOW as f64 * loop_per_iter_ticks).max(0.0);\n    let framing_ticks = framing_f.round() as u64;\n\n    Overhead {\n        framing_ticks,\n        loop_per_iter_ticks,\n        cal_raw_low_ticks: raw_low,\n        cal_raw_high_ticks: raw_high,\n        cal_duration: cal_start.elapsed(),\n    }\n}"
}
```
