{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/overhead.rs",
  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn per_event_ticks_amortizes_framing() {\n        let ovh = Overhead {\n            framing_ticks: 20,\n            cal_duration: Duration::from_millis(1),\n        };\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(1), 20);\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(10), 2);\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(100), 0); // truncates\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(0), 20); // batch=0 treated as 1\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn calibrate_returns_positive_ticks_quickly() {\n        let ovh = calibrate();\n        // Any modern x86 reads rdtsc in < 100 ticks; allow slack.\n        assert!(ovh.framing_ticks > 0);\n        assert!(ovh.framing_ticks < 10_000);\n        // Calibration should complete well under a second.\n        assert!(ovh.cal_duration < Duration::from_secs(1));\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn per_event_ticks_amortizes_framing() {\n        let ovh = Overhead {\n            framing_ticks: 20,\n            loop_per_iter_ticks: 0.0,\n            cal_raw_low_ticks: 0,\n            cal_raw_high_ticks: 0,\n            cal_duration: Duration::from_millis(1),\n        };\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(1), 20);\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(10), 2);\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(100), 0); // truncates\n        assert_eq!(ovh.per_event_ticks(0), 20); // batch=0 treated as 1\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn calibrate_returns_positive_ticks_quickly() {\n        let ovh = calibrate();\n        // Modern x86 rdtsc framing is ~15–50 ticks; allow slack.\n        assert!(ovh.framing_ticks > 0);\n        assert!(ovh.framing_ticks < 10_000);\n        // loop_per_iter should be small and non-negative.\n        assert!(ovh.loop_per_iter_ticks >= 0.0);\n        assert!(ovh.loop_per_iter_ticks < 100.0);\n        // Calibration should complete well under a second.\n        assert!(ovh.cal_duration < Duration::from_secs(1));\n    }\n}"
}

The two-point fit gives the right answer\nby varying `inner` on an empty bench and solving\n`raw_delta = framing + inner · loop_per_iter` in `f64` (no\ninteger-division loss at small values).\n\nOn this box the fit lands on **framing = 21 tk (~5.5 ns)**,\nreproducible across runs. With the correction applied,\n`inner=1` probe mean drops from 9 ns (raw) to 4 ns, which matches\nthe `inner=1000` raw mean of 5 ns (where the correction is\nnegligible) — both phases converge on the same actual\n`handle_message` cost.\n\n### File-level edits\n\n- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-6.\n- `src/perf/overhead.rs`: new module. Constants `CAL_WARMUP = 10_000`,\n  `CAL_SAMPLES = 1_000`, `N_LOW = 100`, `N_HIGH = 10_000`. `Overhead`\n  struct carries `framing_ticks`, `loop_per_iter_ticks`,\n  `cal_raw_low_ticks`, `cal_raw_high_ticks`, `cal_duration`.\n  `calibrate()` runs the fit; `Overhead::per_event_ticks(batch)`\n  returns `framing_ticks / batch.max(1)` for subtraction. Two unit\n  tests (amortization math + calibration smoke).\n- `src/perf/mod.rs`: `pub mod overhead` + re-export\n  `Overhead`/`calibrate`.\n- `src/perf/tprobe2.rs`: `TProbe2::report` signature gains\n  `overhead: Option<&Overhead>`; drain loop subtracts\n  `ovh.per_event_ticks(batch)` from each `per_event_raw` before\n  histogram insertion (via `saturating_sub`, clamp at 1). New\n  test `report_subtracts_overhead`. Old tests updated to pass `None`.\n- `src/bin/goal1.rs`: new `--raw` flag; calibrates on the warmed\n  system between `probe.clear()` and the measurement `run_for`;\n  prints an `apparatus: …` diagnostic line before the band-table\n  report.\n\n### What's *not* subtracted\n\nOnly `framing_ticks`. `loop_per_iter_ticks` (from the black_box\nempty bench) is kept on `Overhead` for diagnostic visibility but\ndeliberately not subtracted — the real dispatch loop's per-iter\ncost (queue pop, deadline check, SingleCtx construction, dynamic\ndispatch) is part of what the user wants to measure, and\n`black_box(1)`'s loop_per_iter doesn't model it anyway. User\nalready signed off on this scope.\n\n### Smoke\n\n- `goal1 0.5 --warmup 0 --inner 1` — 19.9 M msg/s, apparatus\n  21 tk / 5.54 ns, probe mean min-p99 **4 ns** (from 9 ns raw).\n- `goal1 0.5 --warmup 0 --inner 1000` — 204 M msg/s, apparatus\n  correction 0 tk (rounds down), probe mean min-p99 5 ns\n  (unchanged — raw already clean at this batch).\n- `goal1 0.5 --warmup 0 --inner 1 --raw` — apparatus: \"raw (no\n  overhead subtraction)\"; no calibration run.\n- 14/14 tests pass. Calibration adds ~10 ms to default runs."

Step 0.1.0-6 complete.

- **`Cargo.toml`**: bump to `0.1.0-6`.
- **`src/perf/overhead.rs`** (new): two-point-fit calibration. `Overhead` carries `framing_ticks`, `loop_per_iter_ticks`, raw low/high measurements, and `cal_duration`. `calibrate()` runs `CAL_WARMUP=10_000` + two minimum-passes at `N_LOW=100` and `N_HIGH=10_000` with `CAL_SAMPLES=1_000` each, fits `raw_delta = framing + inner · loop_per_iter` in `f64`. `Overhead::per_event_ticks(batch)` returns the per-batch correction.
- **`src/perf/mod.rs`**: `pub mod overhead` + re-export of `Overhead`/`calibrate`.
- **`src/perf/tprobe2.rs`**: `report` now takes `overhead: Option<&Overhead>`; drain loop subtracts via `saturating_sub` + clamp-at-1. New test `report_subtracts_overhead`. Existing tests updated.
- **`src/bin/goal1.rs`**: `--raw` flag; calibration runs between `probe.clear()` and measurement; `apparatus: …` diagnostic line before the band table.
- **`notes/chores-01.md`**: new `## Apparatus overhead calibration (0.1.0-6)` section covering why two-point was needed (single-point with fence overcorrects), fit math, what's subtracted (framing only, not loop_per_iter), and smoke numbers.

Pre-commit: fmt/clippy/test (14/14) clean, `cargo install` built. Smoke shows **framing = 21 tk (5.54 ns)** stable across three runs; adjusted inner=1 mean drops from 9 ns raw → 4 ns, matching inner=1000's 5 ns.

One honest wart: the first cut used a single-point fenced-`rdtsc` calibration and reported **framing = 38 tk**, which overcorrected (subtracted more than real work experiences). Caught it during smoke, switched to two-point fit matching `iiac-perf`'s approach. Documented fully in the module comment.

Ready for review.

> AGENT

Starting step 0.1.0-7: Goal2 (per-actor threads, mpsc channels).

Design thinking before code: with ping-pong holding 1 message in flight, `inner>1` batches can't fill on a per-actor queue (each actor only ever has 0 or 1 messages waiting) — so Goal2 fixes `inner=1` and doesn't expose the flag. Shutdown via an internal `Signal::{User(Message), ClearProbe, Shutdown}` enum threaded through the channels; actor factories (closures) instead of pre-boxed actors let us defer actor creation to the spawned thread, keeping the `Actor` trait free of a `Send` bound.

```
1	//! Runtimes that drive [`Actor`]s. The single-thread runtime lives
2	//! here today; the multi-thread runtime arrives in a later step.
3	
4	use std::collections::VecDeque;
5	use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
6	
7	use crate::perf::TProbe2;
8	use crate::{Actor, Context, Message};
9	
10	/// Single-threaded runtime: all actors run on the calling thread,
11	/// sharing one FIFO message queue. Dispatch loops until either the
12	/// caller-supplied deadline expires or the queue drains.
13	///
14	/// Dispatch is wrapped in a [`TProbe2`] batched scope covering
15	/// `inner` consecutive `handle_message` calls; per-event latency
16	/// is available via [`Self::probe_mut`] after [`Self::run_for`]
17	/// returns. The scope's `site_id` is the first `dst_id` in the
18	/// batch (arbitrary-but-stable when `inner > 1`).
19	pub struct SingleThreadRuntime {
20	    actors: Vec<Box<dyn Actor>>,
21	    queue: VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,
22	    probe: TProbe2,
23	}
24	
25	impl SingleThreadRuntime {
26	    /// Create an empty runtime with no actors or pending messages.
27	    /// `probe_name` names the embedded probe in its band-table
28	    /// report (e.g. `"goal1.dispatch"`).
29	    pub fn new(probe_name: &str) -> Self {
30	        Self {
31	            actors: Vec::new(),
32	            queue: VecDeque::new(),
33	            probe: TProbe2::new(probe_name),
34	        }
35	    }
36	
37	    /// Register `actor`; the returned `u32` is its stable id for
38	    /// the lifetime of the runtime and is usable as `dst_id` in
39	    /// [`Context::send`]. Ids are allocated sequentially from 0.
40	    pub fn add_actor(&mut self, actor: Box<dyn Actor>) -> u32 {
41	        let id = self.actors.len() as u32;
42	        self.actors.push(actor);
43	        id
44	    }
45	
46	    /// Enqueue an initial message for `dst_id`. Used to prime the
47	    /// dispatch loop before [`Self::run_for`].
48	    pub fn seed(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {
49	        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));
50	    }
51	
52	    /// Dispatch messages until `duration` elapses or the queue
53	    /// empties. Each probe scope wraps `inner` consecutive
54	    /// dispatches. A partial trailing batch (queue emptied
55	    /// mid-scope) is discarded — neither recorded nor counted —
56	    /// so throughput numbers reflect whole-batch work only.
57	    ///
58	    /// Returns the total number of messages counted toward
59	    /// whole batches.
60	    pub fn run_for(&mut self, duration: Duration, inner: u64) -> u64 {
61	        let deadline = Instant::now() + duration;
62	        let mut count = 0u64;
63	        while Instant::now() < deadline {
64	            let first_dst = match self.queue.front() {
65	                Some((d, _)) => *d,
66	                None => break,
67	            };
68	            let Self {
69	                actors,
70	                queue,
71	                probe,
72	            } = self;
73	            let id = probe.start(first_dst as u64);
74	            let mut done: u64 = 0;
75	            while done < inner {
76	                let Some((dst, msg)) = queue.pop_front() else {
77	                    break;
78	                };
79	                let actor = &mut actors[dst as usize];
80	                let mut ctx = SingleCtx { queue };
81	                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, msg);
82	                done += 1;
83	            }
84	            if done == inner {
85	                probe.end_batch(id, inner);
86	                count += done;
87	            } else {
88	                // Partial trailing batch: drop the scope (don't
89	                // record or count) and stop.
90	                break;
91	            }
92	        }
93	        count
94	    }
95	
96	    /// Mutable access to the embedded probe — used by callers to
97	    /// invoke [`TProbe2::clear`] at the warmup → measurement
98	    /// boundary and [`TProbe2::report`] after [`Self::run_for`]
99	    /// returns.
100	    pub fn probe_mut(&mut self) -> &mut TProbe2 {
101	        &mut self.probe
102	    }
103	}
104	
105	/// [`Context`] impl used by [`SingleThreadRuntime`]: pushes onto
106	/// the runtime's shared queue.
107	struct SingleCtx<'a> {
108	    queue: &'a mut VecDeque<(u32, Message)>,
109	}
110	
111	impl Context for SingleCtx<'_> {
112	    /// Push `(dst_id, msg)` onto the shared dispatch queue.
113	    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {
114	        self.queue.push_back((dst_id, msg));
115	    }
116	}
117	
118	#[cfg(test)]
119	mod tests {
120	    use super::*;
121	
122	    /// Minimal actor that drops every message without replying.
123	    struct Sink;
124	
125	    impl Actor for Sink {
126	        fn handle_message(&mut self, _ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {}
127	    }
128	
129	    /// Bounded ping-pong actor: replies to its peer up to `max`
130	    /// times, then goes silent. Used to produce a deterministic
131	    /// message count in unit tests without wall-clock dependence.
132	    struct Bouncer {
133	        peer: u32,
134	        sent: u32,
135	        max: u32,
136	    }
137	
138	    impl Actor for Bouncer {
139	        fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {
140	            if self.sent < self.max {
141	                ctx.send(self.peer, Message);
142	                self.sent += 1;
143	            }
144	        }
145	    }
146	
147	    #[test]
148	    fn seed_is_handled_and_queue_drains() {
149	        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("test.drain");
150	        let id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink));
151	        rt.seed(id, Message);
152	        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_millis(1), 1);
153	        assert_eq!(n, 1);
154	    }
155	
156	    #[test]
157	    fn bounded_ping_pong_count() {
158	        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("test.bounded");
159	        let a = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {
160	            peer: 1,
161	            sent: 0,
162	            max: 5,
163	        }));
164	        let _b = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {
165	            peer: 0,
166	            sent: 0,
167	            max: 5,
168	        }));
169	        rt.seed(a, Message);
170	        // seed (1) + 5 from a + 5 from b = 11, all whole batches at inner=1.
171	        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs(1), 1);
172	        assert_eq!(n, 11);
173	    }
174	
175	    #[test]
176	    fn add_actor_assigns_sequential_ids() {
177	        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("test.ids");
178	        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 0);
179	        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 1);
180	        assert_eq!(rt.add_actor(Box::new(Sink)), 2);
181	    }
182	
183	    #[test]
184	    fn partial_trailing_batch_is_dropped() {
185	        // 3 total dispatches available, inner=2 ⇒ one whole batch
186	        // counted (2) and one partial (1) discarded.
187	        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("test.partial");
188	        let a = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {
189	            peer: 1,
190	            sent: 0,
191	            max: 1,
192	        }));
193	        let _b = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {
194	            peer: 0,
195	            sent: 0,
196	            max: 1,
197	        }));
198	        rt.seed(a, Message);
199	        // Events: seed→b, b→a (b.sent=1), a→b (a.sent=1), b stops.
200	        // 3 dispatches total. inner=2 ⇒ first batch of 2 recorded

  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn partial_trailing_batch_is_dropped() {\n        // 3 total dispatches available, inner=2 ⇒ one whole batch\n        // counted (2) and one partial (1) discarded.\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"test.partial\");\n        let a = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 1,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 1,\n        }));\n        let _b = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 0,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 1,\n        }));\n        rt.seed(a, Message);\n        // Events: seed→b, b→a (b.sent=1), a→b (a.sent=1), b stops.\n        // 3 dispatches total. inner=2 ⇒ first batch of 2 recorded\n        // (count=2), then partial batch of 1 discarded.\n        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs(1), 2);\n        assert_eq!(n, 2);\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn partial_trailing_batch_is_dropped() {\n        // 3 total dispatches available, inner=2 ⇒ one whole batch\n        // counted (2) and one partial (1) discarded.\n        let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new(\"test.partial\");\n        let a = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 1,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 1,\n        }));\n        let _b = rt.add_actor(Box::new(Bouncer {\n            peer: 0,\n            sent: 0,\n            max: 1,\n        }));\n        rt.seed(a, Message);\n        // Events: seed→b, b→a (b.sent=1), a→b (a.sent=1), b stops.\n        // 3 dispatches total. inner=2 ⇒ first batch of 2 recorded\n        // (count=2), then partial batch of 1 discarded.\n        let n = rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs(1), 2);\n        assert_eq!(n, 2);\n    }\n}\n\n// --- MultiThreadRuntime -------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Multi-threaded runtime: one thread per actor, messages flow\n/// over per-actor [`std::sync::mpsc`] channels. Each actor thread\n/// owns a [`TProbe2`] recording per-`handle_message` latency at\n/// `inner=1`; probes are returned to the caller when threads join.\n///\n/// Unlike [`SingleThreadRuntime`], actors are registered as\n/// factory closures — construction runs inside the actor's own\n/// thread, so users don't need `Send` on the trait itself, only on\n/// the actor implementor and its factory.\n///\n/// Shutdown is in-band: an internal `Signal::Shutdown` value\n/// travels the same channel that carries user messages, so the\n/// actor's `recv()` loop exits cleanly after the measurement\n/// window elapses. A `Signal::ClearProbe` is used at the warmup →\n/// measurement boundary so only steady-state data reaches the\n/// report.\n///\n/// Ping-pong style workloads hold one message in flight between\n/// two actors; `inner > 1` batching would leave every probe scope\n/// partial (queue empties mid-batch) so this runtime hardcodes\n/// `inner = 1`. If we later add a Goal with higher queue depth\n/// we'll revisit.\npub struct MultiThreadRuntime {\n    factories: Vec<ActorFactory>,\n    probe_name_prefix: String,\n    pending_seeds: Vec<(u32, Message)>,\n}\n\n/// Boxed factory: produces a `Box<dyn Actor + Send>` once, called\n/// inside the actor's dedicated thread. `Send` on the outer\n/// closure is required so it can be moved into `thread::spawn`.\ntype ActorFactory = Box<dyn FnOnce() -> Box<dyn Actor + Send> + Send>;\n\n/// Internal channel payload. User sends become [`Signal::User`];\n/// [`Signal::ClearProbe`] and [`Signal::Shutdown`] are injected by\n/// the runtime to drive phase transitions and exit.\nenum Signal {\n    User(Message),\n    ClearProbe,\n    Shutdown,\n}\n\nimpl MultiThreadRuntime {\n    /// Create an empty runtime. `probe_name_prefix` is combined\n    /// with the actor id to form each per-thread probe's name\n    /// (e.g. `\"goal2.dispatch.actor0\"`).\n    pub fn new(probe_name_prefix: &str) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            factories: Vec::new(),\n            probe_name_prefix: probe_name_prefix.to_string(),\n            pending_seeds: Vec::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Register an actor factory; returns the assigned id\n    /// (0-indexed). `factory` is called inside the spawned thread\n    /// that will drive this actor, so the actor type itself does\n    /// not need to be `Send` before the call — only the factory.\n    pub fn add_actor<F, A>(&mut self, factory: F) -> u32\n    where\n        F: FnOnce() -> A + Send + 'static,\n        A: Actor + Send + 'static,\n    {\n        let id = self.factories.len() as u32;\n        self.factories\n            .push(Box::new(move || Box::new(factory()) as Box<dyn Actor + Send>));\n        id\n    }\n\n    /// Enqueue an initial message for `dst_id`. Delivered after\n    /// all threads spawn and before warmup begins.\n    pub fn seed(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {\n        self.pending_seeds.push((dst_id, msg));\n    }\n\n    /// Spawn one thread per registered actor, run the warmup\n    /// phase, clear all probes, run the measurement phase, shut\n    /// down and join every thread. Returns each actor's\n    /// `(messages_handled, probe)` pair in id order. Consumes the\n    /// runtime's registered factories and pending seeds.\n    pub fn run(&mut self, warmup: Duration, measurement: Duration) -> Vec<(u64, TProbe2)> {\n        let factories = std::mem::take(&mut self.factories);\n        let seeds = std::mem::take(&mut self.pending_seeds);\n        let n = factories.len();\n\n        // Per-actor channels.\n        let mut senders: Vec<Sender<Signal>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);\n        let mut receivers: Vec<Option<Receiver<Signal>>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);\n        for _ in 0..n {\n            let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();\n            senders.push(tx);\n            receivers.push(Some(rx));\n        }\n\n        // Spawn one thread per actor.\n        let mut handles: Vec<JoinHandle<(u64, TProbe2)>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);\n        for (id, factory) in factories.into_iter().enumerate() {\n            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n            // OK: receivers[id] was Some(...) since we pushed one per actor above.\n            let rx = receivers[id].take().unwrap();\n            let peers: Vec<Sender<Signal>> = senders.iter().cloned().collect();\n            let probe_name = format!(\"{}.actor{}\", self.probe_name_prefix, id);\n            let h = thread::Builder::new()\n                .name(format!(\"actor-{id}\"))\n                .spawn(move || {\n                    let actor = factory();\n                    actor_loop(actor, rx, peers, probe_name)\n                })\n                .expect(\"failed to spawn actor thread\");\n            handles.push(h);\n        }\n\n        // Seed initial messages.\n        for (dst, msg) in seeds {\n            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n            // OK: receivers are alive (threads just spawned); a send failure would\n            //   indicate an immediate thread panic on startup which the subsequent\n            //   join will surface with a clearer message.\n            senders[dst as usize].send(Signal::User(msg)).unwrap();\n        }\n\n        // Phase 1: warmup.\n        thread::sleep(warmup);\n\n        // Clear every probe at the phase boundary.\n        for tx in &senders {\n            let _ = tx.send(Signal::ClearProbe);\n        }\n\n        // Phase 2: measurement.\n        thread::sleep(measurement);\n\n        // Phase 3: shutdown.\n        for tx in &senders {\n            let _ = tx.send(Signal::Shutdown);\n        }\n        // Drop the main-thread copies so no dangling senders keep\n        // channels open past thread exit.\n        drop(senders);\n\n        // Join and collect (count, probe) in id order.\n        let mut results = Vec::with_capacity(n);\n        for h in handles {\n            results.push(h.join().expect(\"actor thread panicked\"));\n        }\n        results\n    }\n}\n\n/// [`Context`] impl used inside each actor thread: dispatches\n/// user sends over the shared peer senders.\nstruct MultiCtx<'a> {\n    senders: &'a [Sender<Signal>],\n}\n\nimpl Context for MultiCtx<'_> {\n    /// Send `msg` to `dst_id`'s mpsc channel. A closed channel\n    /// (peer has already exited) is silently ignored — matches\n    /// the shutdown semantics.\n    fn send(&mut self, dst_id: u32, msg: Message) {\n        let _ = self.senders[dst_id as usize].send(Signal::User(msg));\n    }\n}\n\n/// Actor thread's main loop. Creates a [`TProbe2`] named\n/// `probe_name`, blocks on `rx.recv()`, and for each\n/// `Signal::User` message probes a scope, dispatches\n/// `handle_message`, and increments the count.\n/// `Signal::ClearProbe` resets the probe and count;\n/// `Signal::Shutdown` (or a closed channel) exits the loop and\n/// returns `(count, probe)`.\nfn actor_loop(\n    mut actor: Box<dyn Actor + Send>,\n    rx: Receiver<Signal>,\n    peers: Vec<Sender<Signal>>,\n    probe_name: String,\n) -> (u64, TProbe2) {\n    let mut probe = TProbe2::new(&probe_name);\n    let mut count: u64 = 0;\n    loop {\n        match rx.recv() {\n            Ok(Signal::User(msg)) => {\n                let id = probe.start(0);\n                let mut ctx = MultiCtx { senders: &peers };\n                actor.handle_message(&mut ctx, msg);\n                probe.end(id);\n                count += 1;\n            }\n            Ok(Signal::ClearProbe) => {\n                probe.clear();\n                count = 0;\n            }\n            Ok(Signal::Shutdown) | Err(_) => break,\n        }\n    }\n    (count, probe)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod mt_tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// Unbounded ping-pong actor used for multi-thread tests.\n    struct ThreadPingPong {\n        peer: u32,\n    }\n\n    impl Actor for ThreadPingPong {\n        fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _: Message) {\n            ctx.send(self.peer, Message);\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn multi_thread_runs_measures_and_shuts_down_cleanly() {\n        let mut rt = MultiThreadRuntime::new(\"test\");\n        rt.add_actor(|| ThreadPingPong { peer: 1 });\n        rt.add_actor(|| ThreadPingPong { peer: 0 });\n        rt.seed(0, Message);\n        let results = rt.run(Duration::from_millis(5), Duration::from_millis(5));\n        assert_eq!(results.len(), 2);\n        // Ping-pong is symmetric: both actors should have handled\n        // messages during the measurement window.\n        let total: u64 = results.iter().map(|(c, _)| *c).sum();\n        assert!(\n            total > 0,\n            \"expected some messages processed during measurement\"\n        );\n    }\n}"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/bin/goal2.rs",
  "content": "//! Goal2 binary: two actors, each on its own thread, ping-ponging\n//! an empty [`Message`] over `std::sync::mpsc` channels for a\n//! caller-supplied duration in seconds, after an optional warmup.\n//! Prints messages-handled / throughput, an apparatus-overhead\n//! diagnostic, and a `tprobe2` band-table report per actor.\n//!\n//! Unlike goal1, `inner` is fixed at 1: ping-pong keeps at most\n//! one message in flight per channel, so batched probe scopes\n//! could never fill. If we later add a workload with deeper\n//! per-channel queues we'll revisit.\n//!\n//! Usage: `goal2 --help`\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse actor_x1::perf::{self, ticks};\nuse actor_x1::runtime::MultiThreadRuntime;\nuse actor_x1::{Actor, Context, Message};\nuse clap::Parser;\n\n/// Actor that, on every message received, sends exactly one\n/// message back to its configured peer. Same as goal1's but must\n/// also be `Send` to cross thread boundaries.\nstruct PingPongActor {\n    peer_id: u32,\n}\n\nimpl Actor for PingPongActor {\n    /// Forward a single `Message` to `self.peer_id`. Never blocks.\n    fn handle_message(&mut self, ctx: &mut dyn Context, _msg: Message) {\n        ctx.send(self.peer_id, Message);\n    }\n}\n\n/// CLI for goal2: two-thread two-actor ping-pong with warmup,\n/// per-dispatch probe, apparatus-overhead subtraction, and\n/// ns-or-ticks band-table report per actor.\n#[derive(Parser, Debug)]\n#[command(\n    version,\n    about,\n    long_about = None,\n    max_term_width = 80,\n    before_help = concat!(env!(\"CARGO_PKG_NAME\"), \" \", env!(\"CARGO_PKG_VERSION\")),\n)]\nstruct Cli {\n    /// Measurement window in seconds (runs after warmup).\n    #[arg(value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]\n    duration_s: f64,\n\n    /// Warmup duration in seconds before measurement. Runs the\n    /// same dispatch loop as the measurement phase (probe active)\n    /// so cache / branch-predictor / frequency land in the same\n    /// state by the time the probe is cleared and measurement\n    /// begins.\n    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 10.0, value_parser = parse_non_negative_secs)]\n    warmup: f64,\n\n    /// Display probe values as raw ticks instead of nanoseconds.\n    #[arg(short = 't', long)]\n    ticks: bool,\n\n    /// Skip apparatus-overhead calibration and report uncorrected\n    /// per-event values.\n    #[arg(long)]\n    raw: bool,\n}\n\n/// `value_parser` helper: reject negative, NaN, and infinite values.\nfn parse_non_negative_secs(s: &str) -> Result<f64, String> {\n    let v: f64 = s\n        .parse()\n        .map_err(|e: std::num::ParseFloatError| e.to_string())?;\n    if !v.is_finite() || v < 0.0 {\n        Err(format!(\"'{s}' is not a non-negative finite number\"))\n    } else {\n        Ok(v)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Parse CLI, calibrate apparatus (unless --raw), run two\n/// ping-pong actors on their own threads, print summary and\n/// per-actor `tprobe2` band-table reports.\nfn main() {\n    let cli = Cli::parse();\n    println!(\"{} {}\", env!(\"CARGO_PKG_NAME\"), env!(\"CARGO_PKG_VERSION\"),);\n\n    // Calibrate on main thread before spawning. Matches goal1's\n    // calibration code path; actor threads share the same CPU\n    // microarchitecture so a single Overhead is good enough.\n    let overhead = if cli.raw {\n        None\n    } else {\n        Some(perf::calibrate())\n    };\n\n    let mut rt = MultiThreadRuntime::new(\"goal2.dispatch\");\n    let a_id = rt.add_actor(|| PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 });\n    let b_id = rt.add_actor(|| PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 });\n    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));\n    rt.seed(a_id, Message);\n\n    let warmup = Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.warmup);\n    let measurement = Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.duration_s);\n    let results = rt.run(warmup, measurement);\n\n    let total_count: u64 = results.iter().map(|(c, _)| *c).sum();\n    let mmps = total_count as f64 / cli.duration_s / 1e6;\n    println!(\n        \"goal2: {total_count} messages in {:.3}s ({mmps:.3} M msg/s, {n} actors, inner=1)\",\n        cli.duration_s,\n        n = results.len(),\n    );\n    if let Some(ovh) = &overhead {\n        let framing_ns = ovh.framing_ticks as f64 / ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n        let per_event_tk = ovh.per_event_ticks(1);\n        let per_event_ns = per_event_tk as f64 / ticks::ticks_per_ns();\n        println!(\n            \"  apparatus: framing={} tk ({:.2} ns); per-event at inner=1 = {} tk ({:.2} ns)\",\n            ovh.framing_ticks, framing_ns, per_event_tk, per_event_ns,\n        );\n    } else {\n        println!(\"  apparatus: raw (no overhead subtraction)\");\n    }\n\n    for (i, (count, mut probe)) in results.into_iter().enumerate() {\n        println!(\"  actor {i}: handled {count} messages\");\n        probe.report(cli.ticks, overhead.as_ref());\n    }\n}\n"
}

An experiment in the actor model in Rust (Communicating Sequential Processes,
Hoare 1978).

Usage: goal2 [OPTIONS] <DURATION_S>

Arguments:
  <DURATION_S>  Measurement window in seconds (runs after warmup)

Options:
      --warmup <WARMUP>  Warmup duration in seconds before measurement. Runs the
                         same dispatch loop as the measurement phase (probe
                         active) so cache / branch-predictor / frequency land in
                         the same state by the time the probe is cleared and
                         measurement begins [default: 10]
  -t, --ticks            Display probe values as raw ticks instead of
                         nanoseconds
      --raw              Skip apparatus-overhead calibration and report
                         uncorrected per-event values

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/notes/chores-01.md",
  "old_string": "- 14/14 tests pass. Calibration adds ~10 ms to default runs.",
  "new_string": "- 14/14 tests pass. Calibration adds ~10 ms to default runs.\n\n## Goal2: per-actor threads + mpsc channels (0.1.0-7)\n\nImplements Goal2 of Stage 1: two actors running on their own\nthreads, ping-ponging an empty [`Message`] over `std::sync::mpsc`\nchannels, with the same warmup/measurement/calibration lifecycle\nas goal1.\n\n### Design decisions\n\n- **Factory closures instead of pre-boxed actors.** `add_actor`\n  takes `FnOnce() -> A` where `A: Actor + Send + 'static`; the\n  factory runs inside the actor's own thread. Keeps the [`Actor`]\n  trait free of a `Send` bound and matches how real actor systems\n  typically spawn state.\n- **In-band shutdown via internal `Signal` enum.** Channel payload\n  is `Signal { User(Message), ClearProbe, Shutdown }`, not\n  `Message` directly. Main thread injects `ClearProbe` at the\n  warmup → measurement boundary and `Shutdown` after measurement;\n  actors match on the variant in their `recv()` loop. Avoids an\n  out-of-band atomic flag and the associated polling.\n- **`inner` fixed at 1.** Ping-pong keeps at most one message in\n  flight per channel, so batched probe scopes (`inner > 1`) could\n  never fill — every scope would hit the partial-batch drop path.\n  The `--inner` flag from goal1 is not exposed; the hardcoded\n  `inner=1` is reflected in the throughput line.\n- **Per-actor probes, merged reporting.** Each actor thread owns\n  a [`TProbe2`] named `\"goal2.dispatch.actor{id}\"`. Main collects\n  `(count, probe)` on join and prints a per-actor band table (not\n  merged — two independent distributions are more informative\n  than one blended histogram in a ping-pong workload where the\n  actors should be near-identical).\n- **Calibration pre-spawn on main thread.** Matches goal1's\n  calibration code path. Same CPU → same `Overhead` applies to\n  all actor threads. Could be refined later to calibrate\n  per-thread, but unnecessary for Stage 1.\n\n### File-level edits\n\n- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-7.\n- `src/runtime.rs`: add `MultiThreadRuntime`, `Signal` enum,\n  `ActorFactory` alias, `MultiCtx`, and `actor_loop` helper below\n  the existing single-thread code. New `#[cfg(test)] mod mt_tests`\n  with a ping-pong smoke test (50 ms warmup + 50 ms measurement).\n- `src/bin/goal2.rs`: new binary mirroring goal1's CLI shape\n  (version banner, help width cap, --warmup, -t/--ticks, --raw),\n  no --inner. Constructs `MultiThreadRuntime`, adds two\n  `PingPongActor` factories, seeds one message, calls\n  `rt.run(warmup, measurement)`, prints summary + apparatus +\n  per-actor reports.\n\n### Smoke (release, `--warmup 0`)\n\n- `goal2 0.5` — 116,495 messages in 0.5 s (~0.23 M msg/s).\n  Mean per-dispatch ~1.17 µs; median ~1.34 µs; p99 tail to\n  ~22 µs (mostly scheduling jitter). Actors near-symmetric\n  (58,247 vs 58,248 messages).\n- `goal2 -h` — wraps at 80 cols, shows the `actor-x1 0.1.0-7`\n  banner, no `--inner` flag.\n- `goal2 0.2 --raw` — apparatus: `raw (no overhead subtraction)`;\n  calibration skipped.\n- goal1 unchanged: still ~200 M msg/s at `--inner 1000`.\n- Tests: 15/15 pass (adds `multi_thread_runs_measures_and_shuts_down_cleanly`).\n\n### The ~250× single-thread gap\n\nGoal1 at `inner=1000` hits ~205 M msg/s (~5 ns/dispatch);\ngoal2 at `inner=1` hits ~0.23 M msg/s (~4 µs/round-trip, ~1.2 µs\nper-event inside the probe scope). The bot thinks the gap is\ndominated by: (1) mpsc's `Mutex` + condvar wake for every\nmessage, (2) two cross-thread context switches per ping-pong\nround, (3) cache-line bouncing between the two actors' threads.\nAn unbounded channel or a lock-free mpsc implementation would\nclose some of this but the context-switch floor is fundamental."
}
```

> AGENT

Step 0.1.0-7 complete.

- **`Cargo.toml`**: `0.1.0-7`.
- **`src/runtime.rs`**: `MultiThreadRuntime` added below existing single-thread code — factory-closure actor registration, internal `Signal::{User, ClearProbe, Shutdown}` enum, per-thread `actor_loop` owning a `TProbe2`. `MultiCtx` dispatches sends over the shared peer channels. `#[cfg(test)] mod mt_tests` smoke test runs 50ms warmup + 50ms measurement on a ping-pong pair.
- **`src/bin/goal2.rs`** (new): clap CLI mirrors goal1's shape (banner, 80-col help, `--warmup`, `-t`, `--raw`) minus `--inner` (fixed at 1). Calibrates on main thread pre-spawn, prints summary + apparatus + per-actor band tables.
- **`notes/chores-01.md`**: new `## Goal2: per-actor threads + mpsc channels (0.1.0-7)` section with design decisions (factories, in-band shutdown, inner=1 rationale, per-actor vs merged reports), file edits, smoke, and a note on why goal2 is ~900× slower than goal1 inner=1000 (mpsc mutex/condvar + context switches + cache bouncing).

Pre-commit: fmt/clippy/tests (15/15, added one for MultiThreadRuntime) all green, `cargo install` built both binaries. Smoke at `goal2 0.5 --warmup 0` → 116k messages, per-actor mean ~1.17 µs, actors near-symmetric (58,247 vs 58,248). Goal1 unchanged at ~200 M msg/s.

One wart: my initial test used 5ms phases and flaked with total=0 — too tight for thread spawn + a couple of phase transitions. Bumped to 50ms each, consistently passes. Documented the choice inline.

Ready for review.

## Checkpoint 1 — proposed commit block

ChangeIDs: app `nnuvrporzlko`, session `xupnwtysqnwn`.

**Title** (57 chars): `feat: Goal2 multi-thread runtime (0.1.0-7)`

**Unified body**:
```
Implements Goal2 of Stage 1: two actors running on their own
threads, ping-ponging Message over std::sync::mpsc channels with
the same warmup/measurement/calibration lifecycle as goal1.

- Cargo.toml: bump to 0.1.0-7
- src/runtime.rs: add MultiThreadRuntime, internal Signal enum
  (User/ClearProbe/Shutdown), ActorFactory alias, MultiCtx,
  actor_loop helper, mt_tests::multi_thread_runs_measures_and_shuts_down_cleanly
- src/bin/goal2.rs: new binary. Mirrors goal1's CLI shape
  (banner, 80-col help, --warmup, -t/--ticks, --raw) minus
  --inner (fixed at 1 — ping-pong with 1 msg in flight can't
  fill batched scopes). Per-actor TProbe2, per-actor band-table
  reports on join.
- src/bin/goal1.rs + src/bin/goal2.rs: blank line between
  `apparatus:` line and the probe report for readability
- notes/chores-01.md: ## Goal2: per-actor threads + mpsc channels

1	//! Thread CPU-pinning helpers: `--pin` parsing, affinity snapshot
2	//! and restore, and human-readable mask/plan summaries.
3	
4	use std::collections::BTreeSet;
5	
6	/// Parse a `--pin` value (comma-separated list with optional ranges) into
7	/// an ordered vector of logical CPU IDs. Accepts forms like `"0,1"`,
8	/// `"0-11"`, `"0,3-5,7"`. Duplicates are preserved in the given order so
9	/// oversubscription (`"0,0,0"`) works.
10	pub fn parse_cores(spec: &str) -> Result<Vec<usize>, String> {
11	    let mut out = Vec::new();
12	    for part in spec.split(',').map(str::trim).filter(|s| !s.is_empty()) {
13	        match part.split_once('-') {
14	            None => out.push(
15	                part.parse::<usize>()
16	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid core id {part:?}: {e}"))?,
17	            ),
18	            Some((lo, hi)) => {
19	                let lo = lo
20	                    .trim()
21	                    .parse::<usize>()
22	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid range start {lo:?}: {e}"))?;
23	                let hi = hi
24	                    .trim()
25	                    .parse::<usize>()
26	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid range end {hi:?}: {e}"))?;
27	                if hi < lo {
28	                    return Err(format!("range {lo}-{hi} is empty"));
29	                }
30	                out.extend(lo..=hi);
31	            }
32	        }
33	    }
34	    Ok(out)
35	}
36	
37	/// Print the current thread's CPU id to stderr with a label.
38	/// Useful for debugging pinning — not called in normal paths.
39	#[allow(dead_code)]
40	pub fn print_core_id(prompt: &str) {
41	    let cid = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() };
42	    eprintln!("{prompt}: cid={cid}");
43	}
44	
45	/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None`.
46	///
47	/// Constructs a `CoreId` directly rather than querying
48	/// `get_core_ids()`, which only returns cores in the caller's
49	/// current affinity mask — after the first pin that mask is
50	/// narrowed and subsequent lookups for other cores would fail.
51	pub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {
52	    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };
53	    core_affinity::set_for_current(core_affinity::CoreId { id: target });
54	}
55	
56	/// Read the current thread's CPU affinity mask, or `None` if the
57	/// syscall fails (very unusual on Linux).
58	pub fn current_affinity() -> Option<libc::cpu_set_t> {
59	    let mut set: libc::cpu_set_t = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed() };
60	    let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_getaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &mut set) };
61	    (ret == 0).then_some(set)
62	}
63	
64	/// Snapshot the current thread's affinity mask so it can be restored
65	/// later. Logs the captured mask at `info` level. Returns `None` on
66	/// syscall failure (very unusual on Linux).
67	pub fn save_affinity() -> Option<libc::cpu_set_t> {
68	    match current_affinity() {
69	        Some(set) => {
70	            log::info!("save_affinity: mask={}", affinity_summary(&set));
71	            Some(set)
72	        }
73	        None => {
74	            log::warn!("save_affinity: sched_getaffinity failed");
75	            None
76	        }
77	    }
78	}
79	
80	/// Restore a previously-saved affinity mask. Used after calibration
81	/// to widen the mask back to what we started with (typically "all
82	/// CPUs the process was launched with", preserving any outer
83	/// `taskset` restrictions). Logs the restored mask at `info` level.
84	pub fn restore_affinity(set: &libc::cpu_set_t) {
85	    let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_setaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), set) };
86	    if ret == 0 {
87	        log::info!("restore_affinity: mask={}", affinity_summary(set));
88	    } else {
89	        log::warn!("restore_affinity: sched_setaffinity failed");
90	    }
91	}
92	
93	/// Format a CPU set as a compact range list with a count suffix,
94	/// e.g. `"0-11,13-15 (15 cpus)"` or `"5 (1 cpu)"`.
95	pub fn affinity_summary(set: &libc::cpu_set_t) -> String {
96	    let cpus: Vec<usize> = (0..libc::CPU_SETSIZE as usize)
97	        .filter(|&i| unsafe { libc::CPU_ISSET(i, set) })
98	        .collect();
99	    if cpus.is_empty() {
100	        return "<empty>".to_string();
101	    }
102	    let mut ranges: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
103	    let mut start = cpus[0];
104	    let mut prev = cpus[0];
105	    for &c in &cpus[1..] {
106	        if c == prev + 1 {
107	            prev = c;
108	        } else {
109	            ranges.push(if start == prev {
110	                start.to_string()
111	            } else {
112	                format!("{start}-{prev}")
113	            });
114	            start = c;
115	            prev = c;
116	        }
117	    }
118	    ranges.push(if start == prev {
119	        start.to_string()
120	    } else {
121	        format!("{start}-{prev}")
122	    });
123	    format!(
124	        "{} ({} cpu{})",
125	        ranges.join(","),
126	        cpus.len(),
127	        if cpus.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" }
128	    )
129	}
130	
131	/// Report the pinning plan (what the user asked for) as a human-readable
132	/// summary for the startup banner.
133	pub fn plan_summary(cores: &[usize]) -> String {
134	    if cores.is_empty() {
135	        return "none (unpinned)".to_string();
136	    }
137	    let unique: BTreeSet<usize> = cores.iter().copied().collect();
138	    format!(
139	        "{cores:?} ({} slot{}, {} unique CPU{})",
140	        cores.len(),
141	        if cores.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
142	        unique.len(),
143	        if unique.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
144	    )
145	}
146	
147	#[cfg(test)]
148	mod tests {
149	    use super::*;
150	
151	    #[test]
152	    fn parse_plain_list() {
153	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0,1,2").unwrap(), vec![0, 1, 2]);
154	    }
155	
156	    #[test]
157	    fn parse_range() {
158	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0-3").unwrap(), vec![0, 1, 2, 3]);
159	    }
160	
161	    #[test]
162	    fn parse_mixed() {
163	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0,3-5,7").unwrap(), vec![0, 3, 4, 5, 7]);
164	    }
165	
166	    #[test]
167	    fn parse_duplicates_preserved() {
168	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0,0,0").unwrap(), vec![0, 0, 0]);
169	    }
170	
171	    #[test]
172	    fn parse_empty_string_ok() {
173	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("").unwrap(), Vec::<usize>::new());
174	    }
175	
176	    #[test]
177	    fn parse_reverse_range_errs() {
178	        assert!(parse_cores("5-3").is_err());
179	    }
180	
181	    #[test]
182	    fn parse_garbage_errs() {
183	        assert!(parse_cores("abc").is_err());
184	        assert!(parse_cores("1-x").is_err());
185	    }
186	
187	    #[test]
188	    fn pin_current_can_switch_cores() {
189	        let mut set = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed::<libc::cpu_set_t>() };
190	        let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_getaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &mut set) };
191	        if ret != 0 {
192	            eprintln!("can't query affinity");
193	            return; // can't query affinity
194	        }
195	        let available = unsafe { libc::CPU_COUNT(&set) } as usize;
196	        if available < 2 {
197	            eprintln!("single-core or restricted by taskset");
198	            return; // single-core or restricted by taskset
199	        }
200	        let a = 0;
201	        let b = 1;
202	
203	        pin_current(Some(a));
204	        super::print_core_id("after pin to a");
205	        let cpu_a = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() } as usize;
206	        assert_eq!(cpu_a, a);
207	
208	        pin_current(Some(b));
209	        super::print_core_id("after pin to b");
210	        let cpu_b = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() } as usize;
211	        assert_eq!(cpu_b, b);
212	    }
213	}
214

5:mod pin;
49:    /// Pin bench threads to logical CPUs (comma-separated, ranges OK).
50:    /// The list is a core *pool*: thread `i` of a bench is pinned to
52:    /// Examples: `--pin 0,1` (2 threads → 2 CPUs), `--pin 0-5` (6-thread
53:    /// pool), `--pin 0,0` (two threads on the same CPU). On 3900X,
55:    /// core — `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max contention), `--pin 0,1`
56:    /// gives independent cores. Omit to leave threads unpinned.
58:    pin: Option<String>,
60:    /// Skip pinning the main thread for calibration. Always takes
61:    /// effect, including when `--pin` is set — bench threads still
62:    /// pin per `--pin`, but main stays on whatever affinity mask
64:    /// calibration runs with main pinned to `pin[0]` if set, else
67:    /// the bench pinning pool (or to reproduce pre-0.6.0 behavior
68:    /// when `--pin` is absent).
70:    no_pin_cal: bool,
116:    if let Some(mask) = pin::current_affinity() {
117:        info!("startup affinity: {}", pin::affinity_summary(&mask));
120:    let pin_cores: Vec<usize> = match cli.pin.as_deref() {
122:        Some(spec) => match pin::parse_cores(spec) {
125:                eprintln!("error: --pin: {e}");
131:    // Pin main for calibration. Always pinned by default so
133:    // regardless of --pin. --no-pin-cal restores pre-0.6.0
134:    // behavior (pin iff --pin given).
136:    // When we're doing the cal-only pin (--pin absent, --no-pin-cal
139:    // bench threads — otherwise main's pin leaks into the
141:    // "both ends hot and spinning" fast path.
142:    let cal_pin: Option<usize> = if cli.no_pin_cal {
145:        pin_cores.first().copied().or(Some(0))
148:    // cal pin that wasn't the user's explicit bench-pin request —
149:    // i.e. the cal-only pin (--pin absent, --no-pin-cal absent).
150:    // --pin ⇒ user already wants main on pin[0]; leave alone.
151:    // --no-pin-cal ⇒ we aren't pinning at all; nothing to save.
152:    let saved_affinity = if pin_cores.is_empty() && !cli.no_pin_cal {
153:        pin::save_affinity()
157:    pin::pin_current(cal_pin);
159:    if let Some(cpu) = cal_pin {
160:        info!("pinned main to core {cpu} for calibration");
162:        info!("cal pin skipped");
164:    if let Some(mask) = pin::current_affinity() {
165:        debug!("affinity during cal: {}", pin::affinity_summary(&mask));
194:        pin::restore_affinity(set);
197:    let cal_pin_display = match (pin_cores.first(), cli.no_pin_cal) {
198:        (_, true) => "none (--no-pin-cal)".to_string(),
199:        (Some(c), false) => format!("core {c} (from --pin)"),
200:        (None, false) => "core 0 (unpinned after cal; --no-pin-cal to skip)".to_string(),
211:    println!("  cal pin           {cal_pin_display}");
212:    println!("  bench pin         {}", pin::plan_summary(&pin_cores));
234:        pin_cores: &pin_cores,

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/src/perf/pin.rs",
  "content": "//! Thread CPU-pinning helpers: `--pin` parsing and per-thread\n//! affinity setting.\n//!\n//! Adapted from `../iiac-perf/src/pin.rs`. The affinity\n//! save/restore, diagnostics, and human-readable summary helpers\n//! are intentionally elided — Stage 1 only needs parse + pin.\n//! Revisit if/when the perf probes get a `--no-pin-cal` equivalent.\n\n/// Parse a `--pin` value (comma-separated list with optional\n/// ranges) into an ordered `Vec` of logical CPU ids. Accepts\n/// forms like `\"0,1\"`, `\"0-11\"`, `\"0,3-5,7\"`. Duplicates are\n/// preserved in order so oversubscription (`\"0,0,0\"`) works.\npub fn parse_cores(spec: &str) -> Result<Vec<usize>, String> {\n    let mut out = Vec::new();\n    for part in spec.split(',').map(str::trim).filter(|s| !s.is_empty()) {\n        match part.split_once('-') {\n            None => out.push(\n                part.parse::<usize>()\n                    .map_err(|e| format!(\"invalid core id {part:?}: {e}\"))?,\n            ),\n            Some((lo, hi)) => {\n                let lo = lo\n                    .trim()\n                    .parse::<usize>()\n                    .map_err(|e| format!(\"invalid range start {lo:?}: {e}\"))?;\n                let hi = hi\n                    .trim()\n                    .parse::<usize>()\n                    .map_err(|e| format!(\"invalid range end {hi:?}: {e}\"))?;\n                if hi < lo {\n                    return Err(format!(\"range {lo}-{hi} is empty\"));\n                }\n                out.extend(lo..=hi);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(out)\n}\n\n/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None`.\n///\n/// Constructs a `CoreId` directly rather than querying\n/// `core_affinity::get_core_ids()`, which only returns cores in\n/// the caller's current affinity mask — after the first pin that\n/// mask is narrowed and subsequent lookups for other cores would\n/// fail.\npub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {\n    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };\n    core_affinity::set_for_current(core_affinity::CoreId { id: target });\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_plain_list() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_cores(\"0,1,2\").unwrap(), vec![0, 1, 2]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_range() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_cores(\"0-3\").unwrap(), vec![0, 1, 2, 3]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_mixed() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_cores(\"0,3-5,7\").unwrap(), vec![0, 3, 4, 5, 7]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_duplicates_preserved() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_cores(\"0,0,0\").unwrap(), vec![0, 0, 0]);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_empty_string_ok() {\n        assert_eq!(parse_cores(\"\").unwrap(), Vec::<usize>::new());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_reverse_range_errs() {\n        assert!(parse_cores(\"5-3\").is_err());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_garbage_errs() {\n        assert!(parse_cores(\"abc\").is_err());\n        assert!(parse_cores(\"1-x\").is_err());\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

  "new_string": "        // Spawn one thread per actor.\n        let mut handles: Vec<JoinHandle<(u64, TProbe2)>> = Vec::with_capacity(n);\n        for (id, factory) in factories.into_iter().enumerate() {\n            #[allow(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n            // OK: receivers[id] was Some(...) since we pushed one per actor above.\n            let rx = receivers[id].take().unwrap();\n            let peers: Vec<Sender<Signal>> = senders.to_vec();\n            let probe_name = format!(\"{}.actor{}\", self.probe_name_prefix, id);\n            let pin_target = if pin_cores.is_empty() {\n                None\n            } else {\n                Some(pin_cores[id % pin_cores.len()])\n            };\n            let h = thread::Builder::new()\n                .name(format!(\"actor-{id}\"))\n                .spawn(move || {\n                    crate::perf::pin::pin_current(pin_target);\n                    let actor = factory();\n                    actor_loop(actor, rx, peers, probe_name)\n                })\n                .expect(\"failed to spawn actor thread\");\n            handles.push(h);\n        }"
}

95	/// ping-pong actors, print message count + throughput, then
96	/// render the per-dispatch band-table report.
97	fn main() {
98	    let cli = Cli::parse();
99	
100	    println!("{} {}", env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),);
101	
102	    // Pin the main thread before any work — calibration and the
103	    // dispatch loop both run here, so they share the pinned core.
104	    let pin_core: Option<usize> = cli.pin.as_ref().and_then(|v| v.first().copied());
105	    pin::pin_current(pin_core);
106	
107	    let mut rt = SingleThreadRuntime::new("goal1.dispatch");
108	    let a_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 1 }));
109	    let b_id = rt.add_actor(Box::new(PingPongActor { peer_id: 0 }));
110	    assert_eq!((a_id, b_id), (0, 1));
111	    rt.seed(a_id, Message);
112	
113	    // Warmup: same loop, probe active, records discarded at the boundary.
114	    rt.run_for(Duration::from_secs_f64(cli.warmup), cli.inner);
115	    rt.probe_mut().clear();
116	
117	    // Calibration (skipped with --raw). Runs on the warmed system,
118	    // before measurement begins, so freq/cache state matches what
119	    // the probe will see during measurement.
120	    let overhead = if cli.raw {
121	        None
122	    } else {
123	        Some(perf::calibrate())
124	    };

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_013CRo8i8LhZ2qUaS14cm1Bv
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/notes/chores-01.md",
  "old_string": "An unbounded channel or a lock-free mpsc implementation would\nclose some of this but the context-switch floor is fundamental.",
  "new_string": "An unbounded channel or a lock-free mpsc implementation would\nclose some of this but the context-switch floor is fundamental.\n\n## `--pin` for thread CPU affinity (0.1.0-8)\n\nAdds `--pin <CORES>` to both binaries. Tightens the stdev of\nprobe reports by eliminating OS thread-migration noise, at the\ncost of exposing placement sensitivity in goal2 (pinning to\ncross-CCX cores raises the mean because every message forces a\ncross-complex cache coherence round-trip).\n\nAdapted from `../iiac-perf/src/pin.rs` with only the two\nprimitives we need (`parse_cores`, `pin_current`); the affinity\nsave/restore, `--no-pin-cal`, and mask-summary helpers are\nelided — Stage 1 doesn't need them.\n\n### File-level edits\n\n- `Cargo.toml`: bump to 0.1.0-8; add `core_affinity = \"0.8\"`.\n- `src/perf/pin.rs`: new module. `parse_cores(&str) -> Result<Vec<usize>, String>`\n  (comma-separated / range list, duplicates preserved for\n  oversubscription) and `pin_current(Option<usize>)` (pin the\n  calling thread; no-op on `None`). 7 unit tests for the parser\n  (plain list, range, mixed, duplicates, empty string, reverse\n  range errors, garbage errors).\n- `src/perf/mod.rs`: `pub mod pin`.\n- `src/runtime.rs`: `MultiThreadRuntime::run` gains a\n  `pin_cores: &[usize]` parameter; actor `i` pins to\n  `pin_cores[i % pin_cores.len()]` inside its spawned thread\n  (before the actor factory runs or the probe is created). Empty\n  slice = unpinned (existing behavior). The lone mt_test updated\n  to pass `&[]`.\n- `src/bin/goal1.rs`: `--pin <CORES>` flag (`Option<String>`;\n  parsed via `pin::parse_cores` in `main`). Uses the first core\n  of the list (goal1 is single-threaded); extra cores silently\n  ignored. Pins the main thread before calibration and dispatch.\n  New `pinning: …` line in the output.\n- `src/bin/goal2.rs`: `--pin <CORES>` flag, same parsing. Parsed\n  list passed through to `rt.run(warmup, measurement, &pins)`.\n  `pinning: actor0→coreN, actor1→coreM, …` line in the output.\n\n### A/B on this box (3900X, 12-core / 24-thread)\n\n`goal2 0.5 --warmup 0.5` — same workload, three placements:\n\n| Pinning     | Throughput | Mean / event | Mean min-p99 | Stdev min-p99 |\n|---          |---         |---           |---           |---            |\n| unpinned    | 0.374 M/s  | 632 ns       | 622 ns       | **246 ns**    |\n| `--pin 0,1` | 0.308 M/s  | 667 ns       | 654 ns       | 171 ns        |\n| `--pin 0,4` | 0.212 M/s  | 1,496 ns     | 1,487 ns     | **80 ns**     |\n\n**Stdev tightens dramatically** with pinning: min-p99 stdev\ndrops 246 → 80 ns (3×) at `--pin 0,4`. Migration noise was a\nreal component of the unpinned variance.\n\n**p10–p40 rises** (user's prediction): `--pin 0,4` puts the two\nactors on different CCX complexes (each CCX = 6 cores on Zen 2),\nso every ping-pong round-trip incurs a cross-CCX cache coherence\ntransfer. Mean climbs from 632 ns → 1,496 ns — the placement cost.\n`--pin 0,1` (same CCX) stays close to unpinned throughput\n(~0.31 M/s vs 0.37) but still narrows stdev somewhat.\n\nThe bot thinks these numbers are the intended story: pinning\ntrades a potentially higher mean for much more reproducible\nmeasurements, which is the right tradeoff for code-change A/B\nwork. The placement itself then becomes a tunable — users pick\ncores that match what they want to measure (best-case\nsame-complex, worst-case cross-socket, etc.).\n\n### `goal1 --pin`\n\nSingle-thread goal1 sees little throughput change with\n`--pin 5` (~206 M msg/s, identical to unpinned) — all the work\nis on one thread anyway, and migration cost is already amortized\nover the 200 M msg/s inner loop. Value of pinning is purely\nreproducibility."
}
```

- **`src/perf/pin.rs`** (new): `parse_cores` (comma+range) and `pin_current`, adapted from `../iiac-perf/src/pin.rs` with only the primitives we need (no affinity save/restore, no mask diagnostics). 7 parser tests.
- **`src/perf/mod.rs`**: `pub mod pin`.
- **`src/runtime.rs`**: `MultiThreadRuntime::run` gains `pin_cores: &[usize]`; actor `i` pins to `pin_cores[i % pin_cores.len()]`. mt_test updated to pass `&[]`.
- **`src/bin/goal1.rs`**: `--pin <CORES>` flag, parsed in `main` via `pin::parse_cores`; pins main thread before calibration+dispatch; new `pinning: …` output line.
- **`src/bin/goal2.rs`**: `--pin <CORES>` flag, parsed list passed through to `rt.run`; `pinning: actor0→coreN, …` output line.
- **`notes/chores-01.md`**: new `## --pin for thread CPU affinity (0.1.0-8)` section with an A/B comparison table validating both user observations.

Pre-commit: fmt/clippy/tests (22/22, +7 parser tests) all green, installed both binaries. 

**Validated user observations on this 3900X** (goal2 0.5s, warmup 0.5s):

| Pinning | Throughput | Mean/ev | Mean min-p99 | Stdev min-p99 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| unpinned | 0.374 M/s | 632 ns | 622 ns | **246 ns** |
| `--pin 0,1` (same CCX) | 0.308 M/s | 667 ns | 654 ns | 171 ns |
| `--pin 0,4` (cross-CCX) | 0.212 M/s | 1,496 ns | 1,487 ns | **80 ns** |

Stdev drops 3× with pinning (246 → 80 ns); mean rises when crossing CCX boundaries (cross-complex cache coherence per round-trip). Hit one clap snag along the way (`Option<Vec<usize>>` with a custom parser trips a TypeId mismatch) — fell back to `Option<String>` with manual parse in `main`.

Ready for review.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/actor-x1/README.md",
  "old_string": "And these can be combined using 'update --init' if you like\n```\ngit clone git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1\ngit submodule update --init\n```\n\n## jj Tips for Git Users",
  "new_string": "And these can be combined using 'update --init' if you like\n```\ngit clone git@github.com:winksaville/vc-template-x1\ngit submodule update --init\n```\n\n## Usage\n\nStage 1 (the PoC landed at 0.1.0) provides two binaries:\n\n- **`goal1`** — two actors on one thread, ping-ponging a\n  message through a shared `VecDeque`.\n- **`goal2`** — two actors on two threads, ping-ponging over\n  `std::sync::mpsc` channels.\n\nInstall with `cargo install --path .`; both share a common CLI\nshape:\n\n```\n$ goal1 0.5 --warmup 0 --inner 1000 --pin 0\nactor-x1 0.1.0\ngoal1: 102087000 messages in 0.500s (204.174 M msg/s, inner=1000)\n  pinning: main → core 0\n  apparatus: framing=21 tk (5.54 ns); per-event at inner=1000 = 0 tk (0.00 ns)\n\n  tprobe2: goal1.dispatch [count=102,087]\n              first     last    range        count mean\n    min-p1        0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns\n    p1-p10        0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns\n    p10-p20       4 ns     4 ns     0 ns    32,684    4 ns\n    p20-p30       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns\n    p30-p40       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns\n    p40-p50       5 ns     5 ns     0 ns    30,128    5 ns\n    p50-p60       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns\n    p60-p70       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns\n    p70-p80       5 ns     5 ns     0 ns    34,218    5 ns\n    p80-p90       0 ns     0 ns     0 ns         0    0 ns\n    p90-p99       5 ns     7 ns     2 ns     3,971    6 ns\n    p99-max       7 ns    34 ns    27 ns     1,086    9 ns\n    mean                                              5 ns\n    stdev                                             1 ns\n    mean min-p99                                      5 ns\n    stdev min-p99                                     0 ns\n```\n\n### Flags\n\n- **`<duration_s>`** — positional, required; measurement window\n  in seconds.\n- **`--warmup <SECS>`** — default 10.0; run the same dispatch\n  loop with the probe active for this long, then clear the probe\n  at the boundary so only steady-state data reaches the report.\n  Set to 0 for quick iteration.\n- **`--inner <N>`** *(goal1 only)* — default 1, ≥1; dispatches\n  per probe scope. Larger values amortize the probe's two-`rdtsc`\n  apparatus cost and give the histogram meaningful dynamic range.\n  goal2 fixes `inner=1` because its per-channel queue holds at\n  most one message.\n- **`-t` / `--ticks`** — display probe values as raw ticks\n  instead of nanoseconds.\n- **`--raw`** — skip apparatus-overhead calibration; show the raw\n  per-event cost including framing contamination.\n- **`--pin <CORES>`** — pin threads to logical CPUs. goal1 uses\n  the first core in the list; goal2 pins actor `i` to\n  `cores[i % len]`. Accepts comma lists and ranges: `--pin 5`,\n  `--pin 0,1`, `--pin 0-3`, `--pin 0,0` (oversubscribe).\n\n### Typical invocations\n\n```\n# Short iterative smoke, no warmup:\ngoal1 0.1 --warmup 0 --inner 1000\n\n# Full measurement with pinning:\ngoal1 2 --inner 1000 --pin 0\n\n# Goal2 with actors on separate cores (same complex):\ngoal2 1 --pin 0,1\n```\n\n## Reading the band table\n\nThe `tprobe2` block is a 12-row percentile histogram with four\nsummary rows. Every row renders every run, even when a band\nholds no data — a zero row is distinguishable from a missing row.\n\n### Header\n\n```\ntprobe2: <name> [count=N]\n```\n\n`<name>` identifies the probe (`goal1.dispatch`,\n`goal2.dispatch.actor0`, …). `count=N` is the number of **scopes**\ndrained into the histogram, not total dispatches — at `--inner\n1000` one scope covers 1,000 dispatches, so `count` here is\n`(total_messages / inner)`. Total dispatch count is in the summary\nline above.\n\n### Rows\n\nTwelve percentile bands in order: `min-p1`, `p1-p10`, …, `p90-p99`,\n`p99-max`. Each row covers the samples whose rank in the sorted\ndistribution falls within that percentile range.\n\n### Columns\n\n- **first** — lowest value observed in the band.\n- **last** — highest value observed in the band.\n- **range** — `last - first + 1`; width of the band's value range.\n- **count** — number of samples in the band.\n- **mean** — average value within the band.\n\n### Summary rows\n\n- **mean / stdev** — whole-histogram statistics, including the\n  `p99-max` tail.\n- **mean min-p99 / stdev min-p99** — same statistics excluding\n  `p99-max`. Useful for A/B-ing code changes because the tail can\n  be dominated by rare outliers (OS preemption, lock contention,\n  interrupts) that drown signal in the body of the distribution.\n\n### Why a band can be all zeros\n\nThe renderer walks `hdrhistogram` buckets and assigns each bucket\nto one band by its cumulative mid-rank. A tight distribution has\nmost samples in a handful of buckets, and each bucket lands\nentirely in one band — so neighboring bands show zeros even\nthough they'd nominally hold ~10% of the data. It's a display\nartifact, not missing data: the samples are visible in the\nadjacent non-zero band. A `stdev min-p99` near 0 alongside a\ncouple of dense bands is a strong signal that the underlying\ndistribution is spike-heavy.\n\n### The `apparatus:` line\n\n```\napparatus: framing=21 tk (5.54 ns); per-event at inner=1000 = 0 tk (0.00 ns)\n```\n\nReports the fitted overhead model. `framing` is the hardware\nfloor cost of the probe's two-`rdtsc` pair, measured by a\ntwo-point fit on empty `black_box(1)` scopes. `per-event at\ninner=N` is what the report subtracts from each stored value —\n`framing / N`, which amortizes toward zero as `inner` grows.\nUnder `--raw` this line reads `raw (no overhead subtraction)`\nand calibration is skipped.\n\n### The `pinning:` line\n\n```\npinning: actor0→core0, actor1→core4\n```\n\nShows what `--pin` produced for each workload thread;\n`none (unpinned)` means the OS is free to schedule threads\nanywhere.\n\n## The bot's understanding\n\nA handful of recurring patterns in the output, with the bot's\nbest read of why the numbers look the way they do. Specific\nnumbers below are from an AMD Ryzen 3900X at ~3.7 GHz — compare\nyour own numbers to your own baseline, not to these.\n\n### goal1 `--inner 1` shows ~9 ns; `--inner 1000` shows ~5 ns\n\nThe probe's two-`rdtsc` framing has a real cost (~4 ns on modern\nx86) that contaminates every stored scope delta. At `inner=1`\nthe full framing lands on every sample, pushing the reported\nper-event cost to ~9 ns; at `inner=1000` framing is amortized\n(~0.004 ns per event), so the stored value converges on the\nactual `handle_message` cost of ~5 ns. `--raw` makes this\nconcrete — `--raw --inner 1` reads ~9 ns, `--raw --inner 1000`\nreads ~5 ns. Default overhead calibration subtracts the framing,\nso adjusted `--inner 1` also lands at ~5 ns, matching\n`--inner 1000`.\n\n### goal2 is ~900× slower than goal1 `--inner 1000`\n\ngoal1 `--inner 1000`: ~5 ns per event (~200 M msg/s).\ngoal2 `--inner 1`: ~1–1.5 µs per event (~0.25 M msg/s).\nThe bot thinks the gap is dominated by:\n\n- **`mpsc`'s `Mutex` + condvar wake** on every send and recv —\n  even a fast bounded mpsc pays this on every cross-thread\n  hand-off.\n- **Two context switches per round-trip** — one per channel\n  hand-off, so a ping-pong cycle of (A send → B recv → B send →\n  A recv) crosses the scheduler four times.\n- **Cache-line bouncing** — the shared channel metadata and the\n  message migrate between the two actors' L1 caches each round.\n\nAn unbounded lock-free mpsc would close some of this; the\ncontext-switch floor is fundamental to any \"block, wake, run\"\nchannel pattern.\n\n### `--pin` tightens `stdev` but can raise the mean\n\nPinning eliminates OS thread-migration noise — a thread landing\non a different core than the previous message's cache\nwarmed — so `stdev min-p99` drops (on this box: 246 ns → 80 ns,\nabout 3× tighter). But pinning also *forces* placement, which\ncan be worse than what the OS would have chosen. On this 3900X\n(12 cores split across 4 CCX complexes of 3 cores each),\n`--pin 0,4` puts the two actors on different CCX complexes, so\nevery ping-pong round forces a cross-complex cache coherence\ntransfer — the mean rises from ~630 ns to ~1,500 ns. `--pin 0,1`\n(same CCX) keeps the mean near unpinned while still tightening\nthe stdev.\n\nThis isn't a flaw; it's the intended trade. Pinning gives you\ncontrol over what you measure — pick cores that match the\nscenario you care about (best-case same-complex, worst-case\ncross-socket, etc.).\n\n### The `p99-max` tail occasionally spikes to tens of µs or ms\n\nAlmost always an OS interrupt, scheduler preemption, or a rare\nlock contention sneaking into a probe scope. The probe sees real\nevents the system experiences, so the measurement is honest —\nthey just aren't what you typically want to compare across\nruns. `mean min-p99` excludes this tail and is usually the\nright comparator for A/B work.\n\n### Numbers travel with the hardware\n\n- **Framing** is ~20–50 ticks regardless of clock speed\n  (pipeline cost in cycles, not time).\n- **goal1 per-event cost at large `inner`** scales with clock\n  frequency.\n- **goal2 latency** depends heavily on CPU topology (single\n  socket vs NUMA), cache architecture, and scheduler tuning.\n\nIf the numbers on your box differ from those above, that's\nexpected; consistency run-to-run and sensitivity to\n`--pin` / `--inner` / `--raw` are the invariants worth trusting.\n\n## jj Tips for Git Users"
}
```
